### V4 playground path page

Light tier, D8 checkpoint V4 ([D8](p3-d8-parallel-tracks.md)): a playground path page with fills, holes, both fill rules, pan, zoom and rotation, and headed screenshots. The user resumed feature development on 2026-10-20 (AL-U8 decision) and decided that on 2026-10-20 only, verification is skipped in favour of feature work: the independent diff review and long identity or benchmark runs are **NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20**. Functional evidence only: precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4 has A04 evidence, and no Q4 coverage threshold is claimed. V4 closes no A01-A08 criterion and no C03-C05 item.

**Builder work cap (decision for the V3c gate).** The [V3c entry](#v3c-implementation) says V4 must not give a host a real port until B0's E3 result exists or a work cap for the builder is decided. V4 decides the cap, following the recommended direction:

- The cap is on the D8-interim TypeScript fringe builder (`buildPathFringe`), measured as the number of distinct vertex indices in the port mesh's `boundaryEdges`. It is `fringeBoundaryVertexCap: 128` in `PATH_MESH_LIMITS` (`path-mesh-pipeline.ts`); tests may lower it, production never does. The reviewer's timing on convex n-gons put n = 128 at 47 ms and n = 256 at 141 ms, so 128 bounds a single first build near the measured n = 128 cost; it is a functional bound, not a performance claim.
- A mesh above the cap is drawn **region-only**: its region triangles, no fringe (aliased edges, opacity applied once), through the existing region-only draw (`fringe: null`, the `c = 1` mode V3b's packer and shader already carry). The builder is never called for that cache entry.
- Each such cache entry queues one D8-interim notice, scope `renderer`, code `fringe-work-cap`, with the entry's tolerance exponent; the diagnostic message says the path "is drawn without its coverage fringe". It reuses `render.path-mesh-interim-failure` and its removal condition (V3 contract section h) and adds no public diagnostic code. Unlike every other section h code, this one does not hide the path; that is V3-Q2 option (c)'s behavior, scoped to the cap.
- The cap and the code are D8-interim. They are removed with the TypeScript builder when R2/K1 decides production placement, or when B0's E3 result replaces the bound.

Under the pre-B1 kernel caps (24 source verbs, 16 segments, 4 contours, 72 flat commands, `cubic_fill.rs`) a port mesh rarely reached 128 boundary vertices. After B1 it does: the 32-cubic ring at λ = 8 has 256 boundary vertices, so it is capped and draws region-only with the `fringe-work-cap` notice. That is V3-Q2 (c), which the user ratified on 2026-10-20 after the post-merge review (the [V3 contract](p3-v3-renderer-mesh-pipeline-contract.md) records the decision beside V3-Q2).

**Notice churn (design note, no change).** The notice is queued once per cache entry, so it repeats whenever an entry is recreated: a geometry-revision bump, a fill-rule change, an operation-identity change, eviction and re-request, or a new tolerance exponent each start an entry that notifies again. A path edited every frame above the cap notifies every frame.

**Interior vertices (post-merge review item M4; functional measurement, not a performance claim).** The cap counts boundary vertices only, while the fringe builder's T-junction check scales with the unique edges times the used vertices. Measured on `fill-v0+r2` (a scratch run, not committed): over the 40 V4 scene meshes (8 paths at e = −3 to −7) and the 168 B0-corpus and B1-ring meshes that mesh (ring and snapped fixtures, e = −3, up to 256 vertices) the port meshes have no interior vertex at all: `positions.length / 2` equals the distinct boundary vertices in all 208 meshes (maximum 256), so total vertices over boundary vertices is 1.0 and the cap bounds the whole builder input. The 15 unsnapped O1 paths in the sample fail with `unsupported-geometry` and are not counted. This holds for the current kernel's meshes (triangulation over boundary vertices only); a kernel that adds Steiner points would need the cap reconsidered.

Landed:

- `renderer-core`: the cap in `path-mesh-pipeline.ts` (limit, per-entry boundary-vertex count, region-only serving, one notice per entry, the message).
- `apps/playground`: `p3-v4-paths.html` with `src/p3-v4-paths.ts` (the host page) and `src/p3-v4-paths-scene.ts` (scene, views, probes). The page instantiates the real port with `createFillMeshPort` (geometry-wasm, `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2`) from the kernel WASM, passes it as `fillMesh` with `fillMeshMemoryBytes`, and offers drag to pan, wheel and buttons to zoom about a point, and Q/E or a slider to rotate the world container about the scene center. A side panel shows the view, port calls, cache bytes, kernel memory and diagnostics. The scene has eight paths in paint order: a cubic teardrop (nonzero); a square with a cubic circle of the same winding under evenodd (hole) and nonzero (no hole); the same square with a reversed circle under nonzero (hole); a pentagram under nonzero (center filled) and evenodd (center hole); a frame with two square holes (evenodd); and a cubic lens at opacity 0.6 over the frame. Shapes are sized so that every cubic path meshes within the kernel caps for `zoom · DPR ≤ 2` (probed against `fill-v0+r2`); before B1, beyond that the cubic paths reported `work-limit` and were not drawn. After B1 they mesh (see the 2026-10-20 re-run below).
- The playground manifest gains the `@vector-studio/geometry-wasm` workspace dependency and the project reference, the edge `tooling/boundary-policy.mjs` already allowed (P-B1). `vite.config.mjs` gains the page entry and a dev-server route `/__vector-studio/geometry-kernel.wasm` that serves the local kernel build (default `.tools/geometry-build/.../vector_studio_geometry_kernel.wasm`, override `VECTOR_STUDIO_GEOMETRY_WASM`). No WASM binary is committed; the static build has no kernel and the page then reports how to build it.
- V3-A18: as the criterion foresaw, V4 removes both V3-A18 unit tests and replaces them with V4 tests in the same files (`v3a-path-pipeline.test.ts`, `v3b-mesh-backend.test.ts`): only `src/p3-v4-paths.ts` (with the manifest and `tsconfig.json` edges) references `geometry-wasm` or injects `fillMesh`; the V3 smoke fixture still imports no `geometry-wasm`; every other playground file still has no `fillMesh`, `geometry-wasm` or mesh-pipeline reference.

Open risks:

- **The cap changes a V3 contract behavior.** Section h lists the codes that reach the D8-interim outcome and says the failing path is not drawn; `fringe-work-cap` is a new failure code that draws the path region-only. Resolved 2026-10-20: the user ratified V3-Q2 (c) for `fringe-work-cap` after the post-merge review; the V3 contract records it beside V3-Q2 and `graphics-engine-architecture.md` names it in the fringe cache identity bullet.

- **Open P1 Edge recovery flake.** `P1 latest-scene recovery` fails intermittently in Edge with a device-loss `mapAsync` abort on `main`'s renderer too (above); it needs its own P1 item.
- **WASM provenance on the dev server.** The page loads whatever kernel the dev server's path holds; the page and `observations.json` report its SHA-256, and the adapter rejects a kernel whose fill-v0 version or revision differs.

### B1 kernel caps

Plan entry (light tier): raise the fill-kernel caps so that one single-contour path of 32 cubics at B0's densities up to λ = 8 (L\* = 256 leaves, [B0 contract section 5](p3-b0-feasibility-benchmark-contract.md#5-extrapolation-and-the-feasibility-decision)) is admitted, on the fast rung where it applies, with no allocation in a call. Authority: the [D8 B1 row](p3-d8-parallel-tracks.md) and the user's decision of 2026-10-20 (chat) to resume feature development, which relaxes AL-Q6's "B1 starts only after a FEASIBLE product verdict". Acceptance check: the 32-cubic λ = 8 rings are fast-admitted, their exact rung admits them too, and `process` on them allocates nothing; every existing test passes, with only old-cap assertions re-baselined; `pnpm test:geometry`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm test:p2-identity` and `git diff --check` pass.

**Caps.** Three base values fix the rest, derived as the pre-B1 caps were. The topology and fast-rung caps follow `cubic_fill.rs`. Update 2026-10-25: E14-U1 (fill `r3`) gives rung 0's prefix its own edge cap E0 = 8,192 and a work cap W0 = 2^22; the exact rung and `FILL_V0_LIMITS` keep the B1 values below ([E14 contract](d9-e14-fill-rung0-edge-cap-contract.md)). Update 2026-10-25: E16-U1 (fill `r4`) gives rung 0's prefix source caps of 512 contours and 8,192 segments, and the exact rung 64 contours, 256 segments, 384 flat commands and 320 contour points on line-only paths (`FILL_V0_LIMITS.max_contours` becomes 64); cubic paths and the topology caps keep the B1 values below ([E16 contract](d9-e16-fill-rejection-remedy-contract.md)).

| Cap                                                    | Pre-B1             | B1                     | Rule                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------ | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Contours (`MAX_CONTOURS`)                              | 4                  | 4                      | base                                                           |
| Source segments (`MAX_SOURCE_SEGMENTS`)                | 16                 | 64                     | base                                                           |
| Edge owners (`MAX_EDGE_OWNERS`), E                     | 64                 | 256                    | base, = L\*                                                    |
| Source verbs                                           | 24                 | 72                     | segments + 2·contours                                          |
| Source scalars                                         | 104                | 392                    | 6·segments + 2·contours                                        |
| Flat commands                                          | 72                 | 264                    | E + 2·contours                                                 |
| Contour points                                         | 68                 | 260                    | E + contours                                                   |
| Combined workspace heap                                | 16 MiB             | 16 MiB                 | unchanged; the new reservation fits                            |
| Topology cubics, leaves, pairs                         | 16, 64, 2,016      | 64, 256, 32,640        | segments, E, E(E − 1)/2                                        |
| Topology bytes per workspace                           | 1 MiB              | 1 MiB                  | unchanged; 256 rounded leaves are 897,024 bytes                |
| Arrangement crossing ceiling                           | 32 (`leaves / 2`)  | 32 (`MAX_CROSSINGS`)   | kept, so the triangle-free scan and V2's ceiling are as before |
| `FILL_V0_LIMITS` input vertices, edges                 | 64, 64             | 256, 256               | E                                                              |
| `FILL_V0_LIMITS` pairs, events, sections               | 2,016, 128, 8,320  | 32,640, 512, 131,584   | E(E − 1)/2, 2E, 2E² + 2E                                       |
| `FILL_V0_LIMITS` nodes, cells, vertices, triangles     | 256 each           | 1,024 each             | 4E                                                             |
| `FILL_V0_LIMITS` boundaries, contributors              | 512 each           | 2,048 each             | 8E                                                             |
| `FILL_V0_LIMITS` work, bytes                           | 2,000,000, 16 MiB  | 32,000,000, 16 MiB     | work scales with the pair count (×16); bytes unchanged         |
| Fast rung points, edges, sources, triangles, diagonals | 68, 64, 16, 74, 77 | 260, 256, 64, 266, 269 | the prefix caps, derived at compile time (Alt-A1 section 3.4)  |

The frozen native test constant `LIMITS` in `native_cubic_fill_tests.rs` and the B0 contract's caps are unchanged. `FILL_V0_LIMITS` is no longer equal to `LIMITS`; it dominates it field by field, and the test that compared them now pins the new values and the domination. The frozen spike module `p3_b0_spike.rs` keeps its 68-point arrays and rejects larger prefix views with `PREFIX`, as before.

**ABI, contract and identity.** No export, layout, status, magic or `packages/contracts` change. [V2](p3-v2-fill-mesh-transport-contract.md#memory-and-allocation) says "B1 changes these values without a port change", so the values are not part of the ABI. V2's sentence that `FILL_V0_LIMITS` is "equal today" to `LIMITS` is now historical; this entry is the pointer. Under V2's [operation identity rule](p3-v2-fill-mesh-transport-contract.md), a change that only turns a failure into a success, "such as a B1 cap raise", keeps the `operationId` and the revision, so the operation stays `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'` at revision 2. Within the old caps no `ok` mesh can change: the fast rung's triangle and diagonal capacities were already the worst-case bounds, so they never overflowed; the widened bitsets equal the pre-B1 single word for at most 64 leaves or edges, and the candidate order is unchanged; the crossing ceiling is unchanged; and the rounded fill's L1 and L2 guards (64 edges, 256 events) are unchanged, so a pre-B1 path takes the same loops. The plain identity and `--revision-change` identity runs that would show this are NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20. The plain run was done afterwards, and it found failure-to-failure status changes that the rule above does not name; see [B1 review follow-ups](#b1-review-follow-ups).

**Pre-B1 cap mode for the frozen oracles (Primary decision 2026-10-20, option (c)).** The new caps let the S1 prefix through on B0-corpus paths that failed at the old caps, so the frozen oracles stopped comparing like with like: SL-O4 (the exact rung against the [2026-10-17 record](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-17_p3_p3-b0-feasibility-v1_native_desktop-dkvusav_20261017T150336Z-23973138.md); 16 `O1/P4/s=16` and `O1/P8/s=8` paths gave `Topology(KnotMismatch)` where the record has `OwnerLimit`) and AL-I3 (rung 0 against the frozen spike, whose 68-point arrays return `PREFIX`). The gates and contracts stay unchanged. Instead a test-only cap mode reproduces the pre-B1 kernel: `CubicFillWorkspace::new_pre_b1` enforces the pre-B1 prefix caps (24, 104, 16, 72, 68, 64) and topology limits (16 cubics, 64 leaves, 2,016 pairs), and `fill_export::PRE_B1_FILL_V0_LIMITS` is the pre-B1 `FILL_V0_LIMITS` (equal to `LIMITS`, asserted). `FillEngine`'s `TestHooks.pre_b1_caps` builds the engine from both at reserve. All three are `cfg(test)`, so no non-test build can reach them; the production constructor `new` always enforces the B1 caps. The arrays are sized for the B1 caps in both modes, and the pre-B1 mode only lowers the checks, in the same order. The mode is used by the frozen spike module's `SpikeLadder` and its tests and runner (`p3_b0_spike.rs`, `p3_b0_spike_tests.rs`, `p3_b0_spike_bench.rs`), by the AL-S1 harness (`p3_fill_ladder_bench.rs`), by the AL-U2 oracle dump (`fill_ladder_dump_tests.rs`), and by the module-level ladder harness of `fill_ladder_tests.rs` (AL-F7, AL-I2, AL-I3). `fill_ladder_tests.rs`'s `big-circle` keeps radius 1,024, and the oracle's `fixture/big-circle` literal still expects `CommandLimit`. The identity tools (`fill_ladder_identity.rs`, `p3_b0r_identity.rs`) stay on the production caps, so they show B1's changes. The AL-U7 reference runs were measured on pre-B1 `main` `e2cf5b7` (PR #163), so no reference run is pending against the new caps. `b1_pre_b1_cap_mode_restores_the_old_caps` checks the mode: the B1 ring and 25 source verbs fail with `SourceLimit` under it and pass under the default.

**Restructured code.** `simple_cubic_topology.rs`'s B0R sweep held its candidate rows, unboxed-leaf mask and test-only `touched` rows in one `u64` per row, which limited it to 64 leaves. B1 widens each row to `LEAF_WORDS = 4` words and stores the x-order as `u16`. `RowBits` reads word 0 first, so the lexicographic candidate order and Lemma B's counter are the pre-B1 ones. `fill_ladder.rs`'s FR5 candidate marks, which only feed a debug assertion, are widened in the same way, replacing the compile-time `MAX_EDGES <= 64` assertion. The rounded fill keeps its 64-edge and 256-event size guards: a path above them runs the current all-pairs loop and insertion sort, which are correct but slower. Raising those guards is a performance change outside B1.

**Stack and allocation.** Nothing in a call allocates. The new paths, the exact rung included, are covered by `b1_rings_process_without_allocation_or_storage_change`. The largest new stack frames at 256 leaves are the topology sweep (256 boxes, 8 KiB; the 8 KiB bitset; 512 bytes of order) and FR5 (8 KiB of marks plus the existing 256-entry sort arrays). The fast rung's triangulation arrays of `MAX_HALF_EDGES` = 798 entries are at most 6.4 KiB each natively and half that in WASM. All of these are far below the 1 MiB default WASM stack. `TransverseArrangementWorkspace` grows from 2,512 to 7,120 inline bytes (the tests that froze 2,512 now freeze 7,120 and at most 16 KiB).

**Memory.** Each rounded-knot topology workspace reserves 897,024 bytes (224,256 before B1). The native bridge's combined reservation under `LIMITS` grows from 1,111,552 to 2,908,672 bytes. The engine's native heap reservation under `FILL_V0_LIMITS` (workspace, scratch and ladder; `FillEngine::heap_bytes`) is 8,036,952 bytes, of which the workspace is 7,591,936 under its 16 MiB cap; pre-B1 it was 1,307,736. `CubicFillWorkspace` grows from 12,952 to 42,952 inline bytes, and the native tests and TypeScript verifiers that froze 12,952 now freeze 42,952. All of it is reserved once at `fill_v0_reserve`, inside the per-instance arena rules of V2 section b, and far below the 256 MB working ceiling.

**Tests.** New in `fill_export_tests.rs`:

- `b1_ring_of_32_cubics_at_lambda_8_is_fast_admitted`: three rotations of a `p3-b0-ring/v1`-shaped ring with s = 32 and h = 3/8. Each has 258 flat commands, 256 points and owners, and 32 sources. Each takes rung 0 with no fallback reason and publishes 256 vertices, 254 triangles and 256 boundary pairs that pass the exact mesh invariants. A 33-cubic ring (266 commands) fails with `CommandLimit`, and maps to `WORK_LIMIT`.
- `b1_ring_exact_rung_also_admits`: the exact Legacy attempt admits the same rings under `FILL_V0_LIMITS`, at 256 topology leaves and 256 rounded edges.
- `b1_rings_process_without_allocation_or_storage_change`: the three rings and the over-cap ring, through `process` twice (`OUTPUT_CAPACITY`, then `OK` after the reserve), with zero allocations and unchanged storage.
- `b1_pre_b1_cap_mode_restores_the_old_caps`: the test-only mode above.

Re-baselined old-cap assertions (each now one unit above the new cap, or the new size):

- `fill_export_tests.rs`: `runtime_limits_equal_the_native_test_limits` (new values, domination over `LIMITS`, scratch 3·(1,024 + 2·1,024) slots and 3,072 split pairs); the `SourceLimit` fixtures of `reachable_mapping_rows_have_fixtures` and `mapping_row_fixtures_reach_their_kernel_values`, now 72 lines (73 verbs); the `Rounded(NodeLimit)` fixture, now the star {21/10} of radius 1,000, because {13/6} meshes under the new node cap; the `CommandLimit` labels.
- `fill_export_ladder_tests.rs`: the `source-limit` `PREFIX` fixture, now 72 lines.
- `native_cubic_fill_tests.rs`: the 25-MOVE and 105-scalar cheap inputs (now 73 and 393), the 17-segment inputs (now 65), the zero-line source ceiling (now 61 and 62 zero lines), and the combined reservation 2,908,672.
- `mixed_line_arrangement_tests.rs`, `triangle_free_arrangement_tests.rs`, `transverse_arrangement_tests.rs`: rounded topology reservation 897,024, inline size 7,120 or at most 16 KiB, and `max_pairs` one above the new absolute (32,641).
- `native_cubic_fill_tests.rs` (the carrier emitter), `tests/geometry/native-mixed-cubic.test.ts`, `native-mixed-cubic/verify.ts`, `native-triangle-free-cubic/verify.ts`: combined reservation 2,908,672 and `CubicFillWorkspace` inline size 42,952.
- `tests/geometry/native-mixed-line-arrangement.test.ts`, `native-triangle-free-arrangement.test.ts`, `native-transverse-arrangement.test.ts`: 897,024, 7,120, and at most 16,384 inline bytes.

Validation (worktree with the main checkout's pinned toolchain through `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME`, and its own target directory):

- `pnpm test:geometry`: PASS. `cargo fmt --check` and clippy with `-D warnings` pass. Native: 382 passed and 52 ignored. Vitest: 48 files passed and 4 skipped, with 791 tests passed and 6 skipped. WASM independent-build SHA-256 `65a9f3c0c2571165beeecd87e92928cd0803ea8c1a0ccc8f6ee5eb6d0201212b`. An earlier run, before the pre-B1 cap mode, failed SL-O4 and AL-I3 as described above.
- `pnpm check`: format, lint, typecheck and `check:boundaries` PASS. `test:unit` failed twice on one timeout, `tests/unit/geometry-cubic-boundary.test.ts` "certifies every frozen baseline" (5 s limit), while other agents loaded the shared machine. That file passes alone (22 tests, 2.4 s), and `pnpm run test:unit --testTimeout=30000` passes all 66 files and 740 tests. The test is TypeScript-only and imports nothing B1 changes.
- `pnpm build`: PASS.
- `pnpm test:p2-identity`: PASS (P2 output bytes identical to `main` for every differential batch).
- `git diff --check`: PASS.
- Plain and `--revision-change` fill identity runs, benchmark runs and the independent review: NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20. The plain identity run was done afterwards ([B1 review follow-ups](#b1-review-follow-ups)); `--revision-change` is not applicable, because the revision is unchanged.

Deviations and notes:

- **No review.** The independent diff review is NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20.
- **No performance claim.** The 32,000,000 work budget follows the scaling rule; it is not measured. The B0 and AL-S1 records and their caps are unchanged, and no benchmark was run (NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20).
- **Exact-rung cost above 64 edges.** Because the rounded fill's guards stay at 64 edges and 256 events, an exact-rung fallback on a path with more than 64 edges runs the pre-B0R loops. The fast rung admits the B1 rings, so this affects only fallbacks.
- **Tooling.** `tooling/test-geometry.mjs`, `tooling/compare-p2-wasm.mjs` and `tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs` now honour `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME` when set, falling back to the repository's `.tools/rust` as before, so an agent worktree can use the main checkout's pinned toolchain without a junction. The first run used uncommitted copies with the paths edited in; they are deleted.

### B1 review follow-ups

Light tier, branch `claude/p3-b1-review-followups`, from `main` `2940bee`. Scope: the post-merge review findings on B1 (PR #168, squash `049ff85`): SF1 identity evidence, SF2 the V4 link, SF3 at-limit tests, SF4 stale V2 caps, M5 to M7, and the wasm32 reserve memory row. No kernel behavior, ABI, contract or threshold changes. Acceptance check: the identity run has no `ok` case that changed, the new tests fail under seeded cap mutations, and the release WASM equals `main`'s byte for byte.

**SF1: identity evidence.** `pnpm test:p3-fill-identity --base eca697e` (B1's parent) with the 15 changed existing test files passed to `--allow-test-change`, run on the B1 tree `049ff85` (detached checkout), run `20261020T134953Z-23ca6e02`, measured stages 3,058 s (the `b0r_oracle_full` oracles took 2,777 s). Plain mode flags differences by design, so the tool's own verdict is **FAIL**, for the expected reasons only: the inline test module of `native_cubic_fill_tests.rs` differs from the base (O5; B1 re-baselined its constants), the native and WASM digests differ, and the tamper self-checks report NOT DETECTED because the compared digests already differ. Everything else passes: both kernels report `fill_v0_revision()` 2; the WASM-against-native digests are all equal; the 16 full-size oracles pass; release WASM SHA-256 base `f13fb345…6127`, B1 `65a9f3c0…1212b` (the latter equals the B1 entry's independent build). No existing identity-tool option compares failure statuses, so `node tooling/classify-p3-fill-identity-statuses.mjs <run-dir>` (committed with the review follow-up, exits nonzero on any `ok` change in the published layers) classifies every case of the run's dumps (`native-base.tsv`, `native-branch.tsv`, `wasm-base.tsv`, `wasm-branch.tsv`; SHA-256 `17be15e0…ce8a4`, `6f1313d4…ace1`, `2b45d9fc…f1ab` and `638ccadd…bab`, kept in the run directory, which is not committed) by `ok` or failure, and its output is the manifest below. A status is `ok` when the cascade reports `path_status=0` or the attempt reports `OK`.

| Layer (cases)                          | Unchanged | failure to `ok` | failure to a different failure | `ok` to changed or failed |
| -------------------------------------- | --------: | --------------: | -----------------------------: | ------------------------: |
| `fill_v0` native cascade (15,270)      |    13,453 |           1,355 |                            462 |                     **0** |
| `fill_v0` through release WASM (6,041) |     5,179 |             813 |                             49 |                     **0** |

- **`ok` cases are byte-identical.** All 9,465 native cascade cases and all 4,107 WASM cases whose base status is `ok` have equal output digests, and no `ok` case failed. The native `ok` cascade digests cover the whole published output. There is no blocking regression.
- **Failure to failure.** Every one of the 462 native (49 WASM, all among the 462) changes is port status `WORK_LIMIT` (5) to `UNSUPPORTED_GEOMETRY` (6). They are paths the pre-B1 caps rejected (`OwnerLimit` or `CommandLimit`) and the B1 caps carry on to a topology or rounded-fill failure. By corpus family: `b0/O1/P4/s=16` 3, `b0/O1/P8/s=8` 4, `p3-b0r-random/v1` F1 20, F2 11, F3 81, F4 325, and the ladder family L1 18. The failure to `ok` cases are `b0/O1/P4/s=16` 5, `b0/O1/P8/s=8` 4, F1 555, F2 99, F4 473, F5 40 and L1 179.
- **SL-O4 paths.** The 16 B0-corpus paths of the SL-O4 note (8 each in `O1/P4/s=16` and `O1/P8/s=8`) move from `OwnerLimit` to `Topology(KnotMismatch)` in all four attempt entries (64 attempt rows). Through the cascade, 7 of the 16 become `UNSUPPORTED_GEOMETRY` and 9 become `ok`.
- **Exact-rung attempt rows (the four `CubicFillWorkspace` entries, 61,080 rows).** 7,084 failure to a different failure: `CommandLimit` to `Topology(KnotMismatch)` 2,001, to `Rounded(InputLimit)` 2,469, to `Topology(Unresolved)` 1,755, to `Topology(WorkLimit)` 3; `OwnerLimit` to `Topology(KnotMismatch)` 415, to `Rounded(InputLimit)` 216, to `Topology(Unresolved)` 225. A further 2,432 failures keep their error and differ only in statistics. Of the 24,972 `ok` rows, every output digest is equal: the tool's full digest differs on all of them because it includes `allocated_bytes()`, which B1 changes by design; with that field left out (scratch harness builds of both trees, not committed), 24,364 are fully equal and 608 differ only in `topology_stats()`, the leaf and pair counts of the topology attempt that ran before the rounded fill (for example 48 leaves and 24 pairs against 80 and 40), with the result, `diagnostics()`, `rounded_stats()` and the whole published output unchanged. Those 608 are in F3 (64), F4 (348), F5 (20) and L1 (176).
- **What the rule covers.** The V2 identity rule's text, "A change that only turns a failure into a success, such as a B1 cap raise", does not name failure to failure. A failed result never enters the mesh cache (V1 section d), and the V3 failure memo is per pipeline, bound to one port and never persisted, so such a change cannot leave a stale value, and no `ok` mesh changes, so the revision stays 2 and the operation stays `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'`. The addendum in [V2](p3-v2-fill-mesh-transport-contract.md#addendum-2026-10-20-b1-caps-and-failure-status-transitions) records this and the observed transitions; its frozen text is unchanged. The one visible effect is that a rejected path's status can change between `work-limit` and `unsupported-geometry`.

**SF3: at-limit tests** in `fill_export_tests.rs`, with the helpers `b1_ring_contour` (a ring contour of cubics with an optional tail of collinear lines), `prefix_counts` and `legacy_attempt`:

- `b1_257_leaves_fail_with_owner_limit`: 31 ring cubics and nine collinear lines make one contour of 257 leaves, 259 flat commands. It fails `OwnerLimit` in the legacy attempt and in the fast rung's prefix (`PrefixFailure::Error(OwnerLimit)`) and maps to `WORK_LIMIT`. With eight lines (256 leaves) the prefix reports 256 owners and the path meshes `OK`; 14 lines (262 leaves, 264 commands) still fail `OwnerLimit`; 15 lines (263 leaves, 265 commands) fail `CommandLimit` first and map to `WORK_LIMIT`.
- `b1_source_and_command_caps_admit_exactly_their_limit`: four contours of 16 cubics (edge 2, four leaves per cubic) have exactly 72 source verbs, 392 source scalars, 64 segments, 264 flat commands and 256 owners. The prefix view is `[264, 256, 256, 64]` (commands, points, owners, sources), the legacy attempt is `OK`, and the export meshes it with the exact mesh invariants. One MOVE more (73 verbs) and one scalar more (393) fail `SourceLimit`, and the export maps the first to `WORK_LIMIT`. The 73 and 393 cases sit beside the existing one-above fixtures.
- Seeded mutations of `cubic_fill.rs`, each run against `cargo test --lib b1_`: `source_verbs` check `>` to `>=` (both `run_prefix` and `prepare_prefix`): KILLED by the at-limit test; `source_scalars` `>` to `>=`: KILLED; command emission `>=` to `>` (admits 265): KILLED by three tests, including the 257-leaf test; command emission `len + 1 >=` (rejects 264): KILLED by two; `push_owner` `>=` to `>` (admits 257): KILLED by the 257-leaf test, through an index-out-of-bounds panic at `cubic_fill.rs:1088` rather than a failed assertion; `push_owner` `len + 1 >=` (rejects 256): KILLED by six tests; topology-leaf `leaf_len >=` to `+ 1 >=` (rejects 256): KILLED by three. One mutant SURVIVES: topology-leaf `leaf_len >= edge_owners` to `>`. It is equivalent: `push_owner` already fails the 257th owner earlier in every call order, so that check is unreachable defensive code. The `b1_` tests do not isolate the verb cap at limit + 1 (the extra MOVE also adds a fifth contour and a 394th scalar); the native suite (`native_cubic_fill_tests::canonical_source_caps_and_decoded_identity_are_exact` and others) kills that mutant.

**M5.** `b1_ring_exact_rung_also_admits` now asserts what its comment says: for each ring the legacy attempt is `OK` with `topology_stats()` of 256 leaves and 32,640 pairs and `rounded_stats().edges` of 256, and a fast-rung fallback forced by `LadderFault::TriangleFlipped` selects `Rung::Exact` with `Reason::MeshCheck` and publishes a mesh that is `OK`, has positive area and passes `assert_mesh_invariants`.

**M6.** `FillEngine::build_workspace` chooses the constructor before building (`match` on `pre_b1_caps` under `cfg(test)`, `new` otherwise), so the test hook no longer builds a B1 workspace and discards it. The change keeps the same number of lines, so the release WASM is byte-identical to `main`'s (see Validation).

**M7: AL-S2 pin.** `p3_fill_ladder_bench.rs` `Harness::new` runs the engine in the pre-B1 cap mode, so AL-S2's native leg is pinned to the pre-B1 caps while its WASM leg is the production kernel with the B1 caps. A rerun of AL-S2 is valid only while every decision cell is within the pre-B1 caps (16 source segments, 24 source verbs, 64 leaves, 72 flat commands): a path outside them is rejected natively and admitted in WASM, so the two legs would no longer measure the same work. This holds for the committed AL-S2 record: its 34 decision cells were admitted at the pre-B1 caps (the B0 contract review's admission probe, and the record's `largest-cells-admitted` check: every path of every largest primary cell, `P2/s=16`, `P4/s=16` and `P8/s=8`, is `ok`), and the identity run above shows every `ok` path's output unchanged under B1. The record is not affected by B1.

**Memory row (functional, not a performance claim).** `fill_v0_reserve(64, 64)` on `main`'s release WASM (SHA-256 `e2fae964…2a21`, the same bytes as this branch's build) grows linear memory from 1,114,112 bytes after instantiation to **6,815,744 bytes** (about 2.5% of the 256 MB ceiling), measured in Node with the raw exports. The pre-B1 figure is 2,293,760 bytes ([AL-S2 record](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-20_p3_p3-b0-wasm-v1_chrome-edge_desktop-dkvusav_20261020T050227Z-f86bda54.md)). The growth is the workspace, scratch and ladder reservation of the B1 entry's Memory paragraph.

**SF2: the 32-cubic ring in the playground.** The 32-cubic λ = 8 ring has 256 boundary vertices, above the V4 fringe builder's cap of 128, so it draws region-only with the `fringe-work-cap` notice. This is the [V4 entry](#v4-playground-path-page) behavior under the user's V3-Q2 (c) ratification of 2026-10-20 (PR #177), not a B1 defect.

The release kernel of `main` (`640a28f`; `2940bee` changed no kernel file) and of this branch, built with the identity tool's flags and path remapping, are both 424,114 bytes with the SHA-256 above. A first M6 form with the `.map_err` folded into the `if`/`else` was one line longer and changed the WASM by 3 bytes, consistent with shifted panic-location line numbers; the final form keeps the line count.
- Plain identity run on `049ff85` against `eca697e`: the tool verdict is FAIL by design (SF1); no `ok` case changed; failure-to-failure transitions are classified above.

### Alt-A1 AL-OB5 OB5-U1 generator and admission expectation

Light-tier unit of the approved [AL-OB5 proposal](p3-al-ob5-workload-scenario-proposal.md#6-units), 2026-10-21. Test-only: no product source, contract, dependency, threshold or B0 module changes, and no timing or performance claim. Precision is UNVERIFIED against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4.

**Deliverables.**

- `tests/geometry/p3-al-ob5/corpus.ts`: cells OB5-W2, OB5-W4 and OB5-W8 (s = 32, λ = 2, 4, 8, B0's bulges 3/64, 3/16, 3/8, `snapped: true`, 1,000 paths, seed `0x12345678`) through B0's unchanged `generateCellPaths` and `encodeCorpus`; and the OB5-Q7 (a) observation cell OB5-W8U (`snapped: false`, role `o1`), kept out of the decision cells and out of the decision corpus bytes.
- `tests/geometry/p3-al-ob5/fixture.json`: the scalar bits and verbs of paths 0 and 999 of each decision set, written by the independent Rust generator in `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/p3_al_ob5_tests.rs` (its own xorshift32 ρ draws, `sin`/`cos` and `round_ties_even`; ignored test `emit_p3_al_ob5_fixture`) before any comparison. The native test `ob5_fixture_matches_the_independent_generator` checks the committed file against that generator; `tests/geometry/p3-al-ob5-corpus.test.ts` checks the TypeScript corpus against the same file, so the TypeScript-versus-fixture check is not circular. The unsnapped set is not in the fixture, because its bits depend on the platform `sin`/`cos`.
- `ob5_decision_sets_are_all_ok_on_rung_0_with_the_expected_counts` runs all 3,000 decision paths through `FillEngine::process` at the production caps in one batch per set, asserting zero allocations in both the capacity-query and the real call, and reads each path's rung from `build_one` on the same engine.
- `ob5_unsnapped_observation_records_status_and_rung_mix` records OB5-W8U as an observation, with no assertion on its outcome.

**Corpus SHA-256** (little-endian `P3B0COR1` framing, bytes from `encodeCorpus`; pinned in the Vitest file):

| Corpus                                   | SHA-256                                                            |
| ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Decision: OB5-W2, OB5-W4, OB5-W8 (3,000) | `353fef77a5f7fe9208bdd6566a16477833ea0dc47528dbaca8581a37222acc2f` |
| Observation: OB5-W8U, unsnapped (1,000)  | `8c6618bec379f5e1041ef5803913e16208427cbb178c784afa1238580b2596fc` |

B0's corpus bytes are unchanged.

**Committed expectation** (every path of the set; V, T and boundary pairs B per path):

| Set    | Paths | Status | Rung     | V   | T   | B   |
| ------ | ----- | ------ | -------- | --- | --- | --- |
| OB5-W2 | 1,000 | ok     | 0 (fast) | 64  | 62  | 64  |
| OB5-W4 | 1,000 | ok     | 0 (fast) | 128 | 126 | 128 |
| OB5-W8 | 1,000 | ok     | 0 (fast) | 256 | 254 | 256 |

**Observation only, not an expectation: OB5-W8U (unsnapped, generic coordinates).** Native run on the same engine over the Rust generator's own unsnapped bits: 911 paths `ok` on rung 0 with V 256, T 254, B 256; 89 paths status 5 (`work-limit`) with the exact rung as the last rung tried. This is recorded for the OB5-Q7 (a) observation. It is outside the decision sets and the verdict, and it does not change the generator. Those bits differ from the hashed TypeScript OB5-W8U corpus (`8c6618be…96fc`) in most paths (the review counted 712 of 1,000 paths and 4,717 scalars), because unsnapped coordinates keep the platform `sin`/`cos` rounding; so the split describes the Rust bits, not the hashed corpus. OB5-U2 and OB5-U3 take the Q7 (a) observation from the TypeScript corpus bytes. The decision sets are unaffected: all 3,000 snapped paths are bit-identical between the two generators on this machine (V8 and MSVC libm), and OB5-U2's native leg reads the TypeScript-encoded corpus or verifies its SHA-256 (OB5-V4), so the expectation does not depend on CI's libm.

### Alt-A1 AL-OB5 OB5-U2 harness

Light-tier unit of the approved [AL-OB5 proposal](p3-al-ob5-workload-scenario-proposal.md#6-units), 2026-10-21. Test-only: no product source, contract, dependency, threshold or budget changes, no AL-S1 harness change (its pre-B1 pin stays), and no performance claim; the functional run below is a harness check, not a measurement. Precision is UNVERIFIED against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4. The reference profile is NOT RUN: it needs the user's quiet-machine statement (OB5-U3).

**Deliverables.**

- Page `tests/geometry-benchmark/p3-al-ob5/` (`main.ts`, `workload.ts`, `index.html`, `vite.config.ts`, `benchmark.spec.ts`) and `playwright.p3-al-ob5.config.ts`: the release WASM through `createFillMeshPort` in a Vite production page, cross-origin isolated, headed Chrome then Edge, a fresh page per repetition. The page verifies the WASM and corpus SHA-256 and decodes the TypeScript-encoded corpus outside timing. Per set in B0's shuffled order: an untimed preparation call; OB5-SP-init warm-up and timed calls (window from before `buildFillMeshes` to after the AL-S2 fold); OB5-SP-edit (three untimed one-path calls, then each edit path once in the order shuffled by `0x0b5e0000 + r`); OB5-SP-cold on a second adapter created and disposed outside timing; OB5-SR, `PathMeshPipeline.prepare` on the 1,000 path nodes with `PATH_MESH_LIMITS` unchanged and `buildPathFringe` (wrapped only to count its calls and time). The clock probe, `crossOriginIsolated`, visibility, and the epoch, arena and linear-memory checks of OB5-V9 are read outside every window. The Vite config resolves `@vector-studio/contracts` to its sources, because OB5-SR imports its runtime constants.
- Native harness `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/p3_al_ob5_bench.rs` (new, `cfg(test)`, declared at the end of `lib.rs`), on the production caps: `run_ob5_native` (one repetition per fresh process; per-call allocation counter outside the window; per-path status, counts, fold, flat command count and `build_path` rung record; the corpus file's FNV-1a 64 so the record ties it to the bytes the runner hashed); `run_ob5_observation` (OB5-Q7 (a), untimed, over the TypeScript OB5-W8U bytes); `run_ob5_oracle` (the AL-U2 dump format on the production caps, `build_path` records and export-level AL-I2). `fill_ladder_dump_tests.rs` gains `DumpHarness::production()` and `dump_production`; the existing `dump` and `emit_fill_ladder_dump` keep the pre-B1 cap mode unchanged. A debug-layer test checks that the per-path folds compose and a one-path call reproduces path 0's fold.
- Runner `tooling/run-p3-al-ob5-benchmark.mjs` and `.d.mts`, root script `pnpm benchmark:p3-al-ob5 --profile functional|reference --environment-json <file>`. Order: WASM build, native build, native repetitions, page build, Chrome, Edge, observation, oracle run and evaluation, record, analysis, validator. It honours `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME`, records the commit check of OB5-V13 (`git ls-tree` against `git hash-object` for every source-manifest file) and accepts an optional `quietMachine` observation, which the reference profile's OB5-V14 requires. Run directories go to `artifacts/p3-al-ob5/` (ignored by Git and ESLint); the `.json` records are listed in `.prettierignore`.
- Analysis `tests/geometry/p3-al-ob5/analysis.ts`: `scenarioConfig`, `evaluateOb5Oracle` (SL-O1, SL-O2, SL-O3, AL-I2, harness; `MESH_CHECK`; SL-O5 and SL-O6 reported; the fold of every dumped export output, compared with the native per-path folds in OB5-V7), `analyze` (section 3.4 statistics: T the maximum per-repetition init median, T p95 beside it, t_edit the maximum edit p95, M; native ratios, cold, OB5-SR and memory reported; OB5-V1 to OB5-V14; the section 4 verdict with INVALID on OB5-V1, V2, V3, V7 or V8) and `validateOb5Record`. Test `tests/geometry/p3-al-ob5-analysis.test.ts`.

**Interpretations, for the reviewer.** OB5-V13 is required for the reference profile only (section 3.1 asks a clean committed tree "for the reference run"); a functional record reports the tree state in the item's detail. The OB5-SR port call is included in OB5-V9 (section 3.2 defines that window with no arena growth). A missing oracle run fails OB5-V7 and OB5-V8, so it gives no verdict. The browser leg runs before the observation and the oracle run, so the native leg stays just before the browsers. The native leg identifies the corpus by its FNV-1a 64 and length, which the runner and the validator check against the bytes they hashed with SHA-256; a native SHA-256 would need a dependency (OB5-V4 "natively" is read this way). After the review of PR #185: the identity items OB5-V1 to OB5-V3 are judged only on admitted legs (every path and edit ok, no batch failure), so a failing path fails OB5-V5 and gives INCONCLUSIVE as section 2.4 says, not INVALID; and SL-O3 must have checked at least one entry, like SL-O1, SL-O2 and the harness.

**Reference configuration hash** (pinned in the test): `cb23d4ca9d6c2908597ba3a146234a0d6a5ace4dd0512bff0881a627205ea651` (functional `35dedae3cc095c399c434c0f5b07cd2301df8570dba9b9acaed443bafbbf72bf`).

### Alt-A1 AL-OB5 OB5-U3 reference run

Unit OB5-U3 of the approved [AL-OB5 proposal](p3-al-ob5-workload-scenario-proposal.md#6-units), 2026-10-21: the reference run of scenario `p3-al-ob5-workload` v1 on the reference machine DESKTOP-DKVUSAV, in headed Chrome 155 and Edge 155 and natively, from the clean `main` checkout at `4713307` (run `20261021T032827Z-eff5af6e`, 5 repetitions per leg, `pnpm benchmark:p3-al-ob5 --profile reference --environment-json <file>`). This entry adds records only: no product source, harness, contract, dependency, budget, rule or past record changes. Precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Records:

- [Record](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-21_p3_p3-al-ob5-workload-v1_chrome-edge_desktop-dkvusav_20261021T032827Z-eff5af6e.md) and [raw `.json`](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-21_p3_p3-al-ob5-workload-v1_chrome-edge_desktop-dkvusav_20261021T032827Z-eff5af6e.json), a byte-for-byte copy of the runner's `record.json` (4,881,364 bytes, SHA-256 `bd515058…6db4`). `.prettierignore` already lists it (`*_p3_p3-al-ob5-workload-v1_*.json`). `validateOb5Record` accepts the committed file, and its stored analysis equals `analyze()` of the raw samples.

**Result: verdict FEASIBLE; the independent evidence review ACCEPTED it WITH NOTES on 2026-10-21 (no blocking item; the review note is appended to the record).** Every validity item OB5-V1 to OB5-V14 passes. T = 464.0 ms against B_init = 1,000 ms (0.464; the maximum OB5-SP-init p95 is 502.8 ms), t_edit = 0.740 ms against B_edit = 4 ms (0.185) and M = 7,144,000 bytes against 64 MiB (0.106). Every browser fold equals the native fold of its set, SL-O1, SL-O2, SL-O3 and the harness gate pass on all 3,000 paths (AL-I2 has 0 fallback paths to check), and the `MESH_CHECK` count is 0. The AL-U7 context values T̂ 1,031.1 ms, t̂_edit 4.367 ms and t̂_p95 · k̂₁ 4.467 ms are 2.2, 5.9 and 6.0 times the measurements; the record's comparison attributes the gap to the native per-path extrapolation (the repetition-0 slope and the prediction bound), not to the browser factor. OB5-SR, OB5-SP-cold and the OB5-Q7 (a) observation (911 of 1,000 unsnapped paths `ok` on the fast rung, 89 `work-limit` on the exact rung) are reported only. Scope: simple, single-contour, snapped rings at zoom 1, λ = 2, 4 and 8, e = −3, nonzero, in Chrome and Edge on the reference machine.

Environment disclosure: the operator's quiet-machine statement ("지금 진행 (PC 비움)") and the runner's process listing (its hash and count are in the record; the listing itself is an uncommitted artifact) are recorded; the review note completes the third-party list (Teams, TeamViewer, Video.UI, Telegram, KakaoTalk, Outlook, Everything, the Windows Search indexer and others) and finds that every individual sample is within budget, so the verdict does not depend on the quietness of the machine. Third-party and interactive processes were present and not stopped: the user's own Chrome, Microsoft Edge WebView2, the Claude desktop app and a third-party `hyperframes preview` tool. No CPU-load sampling, per the user's standing instruction.

Evidence review ACCEPTED WITH NOTES. The decision goes to the user under AL-Q6 (a) and [proposal section 4](p3-al-ob5-workload-scenario-proposal.md#4-decision-use). With an accepted review, a FEASIBLE verdict closes AL-OB5 as B1's acceptance measurement. This entry chooses no remedy and changes no budget.

### B2 ST-U2 stroke oracle

Unit ST-U2 of the [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units): the independent oracle ST-O1 to ST-O5 with the positive controls of [section 11.1](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#111-oracles), and the named fixtures ST-F1 to ST-F26 of [section 11.2](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#112-named-fixtures) with their literal expectations. Light tier. Acceptance for this unit: ST-A5, with ST-A12. Precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target; this unit makes no precision or performance claim and closes no A01-A08 or C03-C05 item. The independent diff review is NOT RUN: the user skipped independent reviews for 2026-10-20 only, so it is owed.

Files: `packages/geometry-reference/src/stroke-region.ts` (new), `packages/geometry-reference/src/index.ts` (one export line) and `tests/unit/geometry-stroke-region.test.ts` (new). The module imports nothing from `geometry-wasm`.

Design:

- **Exact arithmetic.** Binary64 inputs become BigInt dyadic rationals. An irrational ideal position is decided by isolating and squaring its radicals: sample points, square-cap corners and collinear turning points in Q(√s), and miter tips in Q(√|a|², √|b|²). No Float64 comparison decides any check.
- **The oracle's own classification.** Decoding, zero-length removal and the one-sided tangents follow ST-S1 and ST-S2. Joins are classified as continuation, reversal or turn, with the outer side and the exact miter predicate of section 4.3. For cubics, the collinear case uses the projected-derivative roots in Q(√disc). Otherwise the exact polynomial gcd of `Dx` and `Dy` gives the stationary parameter `t*`. `classifyStrokeSource` exposes the result.
- **ST-O1, `checkStrokePieceBounds`.** Every polygon must equal the boundary-inclusive hull of its generators (`strokeConvexHull`). Sweep, join, cap and tip samples must lie within `2^e` of their ideal points. Disk pieces must have every vertex within `r + 2^(e−2)` of the center and every chord at least `r − 2^(e−2)` from it. Cusp and turning-point centers must lie within `2^e` of the exact `C(t*)`. Cubic leaves use the P3.0b-s1 continuous verifier on both sides: outward-rounded rational intervals, power-basis Horner, and the s1 `M` bound with cell bisection. Next to a simple stationary root the verifier normalizes `L = C'/(t − t*)` with the side's sign. A leaf that contains `t*` because of the Float64 split `t̃` is clipped at `t*`. The hull term `r·sin θ` is computed from the emitted samples, and side bound plus hull term must be at most `2^e`.
- **ST-O2, `checkStrokeInventory`.** The dump must follow the section 4.9 order against the oracle's own slots. Leaves must tile `[0, t̃]` and `[t̃, 1]` with no gap or overlap, and `t̃` must lie within `2^-40` of `t*`. Join pieces must appear only at source joins, with the oracle's kind. Caps must appear at open ends only, and cusp disks exactly where the classification puts them. Knot and forward-continuation triples must be shared bit for bit, and join outer points and cap triples must be the adjacent sweep samples.
- **ST-O3, `checkStrokeResolution`.** The V2 mesh checks are done in the package, because it cannot import `tests/support/fill-mesh-check.ts`: lengths and indices, exact bounds, strictly positive triangles, and interior-disjointness (the summed area equals the union area). They also cover conformity and `boundaryEdges` equal to the edges used once. An exact slab decomposition then compares the mesh region with the nonzero union of the emitted polygons. `exact` mode requires a zero symmetric difference; `band` mode requires the area difference to be at most perimeter times `τ`.
- **ST-O4 and ST-O5.** `checkStrokeExactRegion` compares the mesh with the ideal union when every ideal piece is an exact polygon (`idealStrokePieces`, `idealStrokeExactArea`). `checkStrokeMembership` decides named points on the closed triangles.
- **Dump shape for ST-U3.** `StrokeDumpPiece` fixes what the `emit_stroke_dump` reader must produce. `verb` is the source verb ordinal (the CLOSE ordinal for a closing line). A leaf is given as its range (`0`, `t̃` or `1`) plus `index` and `depth`, so its exact interval is recovered. Joins carry `outerIn`, `outerOut` and `tip`; caps carry their triple and square-cap extension points. A dropped zero-area hull is `polygon: null`.
- **Fixtures.** `STROKE_FIXTURES` lists 140 cases: every ST-F variant, each cap, join and limit separately, and ST-F15 at 54 cases. Each case carries its expected status, area and tolerance, bounds, join decision, tip, cubic class, disk count and membership points.

Fixture results. Every literal expectation was checked before any kernel run, against the oracle's own ideal geometry: exact areas for the polygonal cases, and union areas with the `n`-gon disks within `1e−12`. The checks also cover the bounds, the join decisions and the ideal tips `(5,−1)` and `(25.75,−1)`, the three ST-F5 predicate answers, and ST-F25 decided on squared ratios. They also cover the disk counts (16 and 32), the stationary classes of ST-F16 to ST-F20 with `t* = 1/2` and center `(0.5, 0.75)`, the ST-F22 to ST-F24 guard arithmetic and the ST-F26 sizes. No literal expectation in the contract was found wrong.

Interpretations, fixed here for the owed review:

- **ST-F26 edges** assumes one arrangement input edge per emitted contour vertex on axis-aligned data: 6 per line sweep, 3 per bevel and 5 per square cap. ST-U3 confirms this against its edge accounting before the first run.
- **ST-F26 pieces 512** cannot reach the piece cap before the 1,024 input-edge cap, because every piece has at least three vertices. Its expected status is "`ok` or another listed status", as the contract allows.
- **ST-F25** uses width 2 and butt caps, as ST-F4 does.
- **Cusp disks** are checked in two parts: the center against the curve share `2^e`, and the polygon against the arc share `2^(e−2)` around its emitted center.

Validation:

- `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/geometry-stroke-region.test.ts`: 169 passed. Every positive control of section 11.1 is rejected and its unmutated baseline passes. For ST-O1, these are the s2 chord-normal R4 leaf (at its `t=0` endpoint) and the flipped R3 start normal. For ST-O2, a removed and a duplicated leaf interval, a join at a generated knot and a missing cusp disk. For ST-O3, a dropped, a flipped and a duplicated triangle and a dropped boundary pair. Further controls are a halved disk count (ST-D9), a moved miter tip, a square cap along the normal (ST-D10), an unshared continuation triple (ST-D3), a stroked closing edge (ST-D4), a clockwise piece (ST-D13), a coarse R4 leaf set, a bevel mesh against the miter region, and R6 uniform leaves at its folded apex. Synthetic Float64 dumps of 28 fixture cases pass ST-O1 and ST-O2, including the cusp at `t* = 1/2` and a non-dyadic cusp at `t* = 1/3` through the `t̃` clip.
- `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/geometry-`: 18 files, 456 tests passed.
- `pnpm check`: format, lint, typecheck and `check:boundaries` PASS. `test:unit` had 907 of 909 passing. The two failures were 5-second timeouts in `geometry-cubic-boundary.test.ts` and `p3-b0.test.ts` while a concurrent `cargo` build ran. They fail the same way with this unit's test file excluded, and they pass alone.
- `pnpm build`: PASS.
- `pnpm test:geometry`: NOT RUN. This unit changes no kernel or adapter code, the geometry harness that consumes the oracle arrives with ST-U3, and this worktree has no `.tools` toolchain.
- `git diff --check`: PASS.

### B2 ST-U3 line-stroke kernel

Unit ST-U3 of the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units), light tier. Plan entry: the line-stroke kernel front end of sections 2 to 4 and its dump hook. Acceptance check: the line fixtures of [section 11.2](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#112-named-fixtures) meet their literal expectations, natively and through the hand-off; each seeded defect of section 11.3 assigned to ST-U3 fails its named check; the real memory of `STROKE_V0_LIMITS` is recorded; the guard constants are proved or tightened for line strokes. Every result is functional evidence, and precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4. Independent review NOT RUN: the user skipped independent reviews for 2026-10-20 only. ST-A0's owed contract review is still due before this unit merges.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_outline.rs` (new): decoding, triples, exact join classification and miter predicate, disk polygons, hulls, guards, the arrangement call and the refinement.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_outline_tests.rs` and `stroke_dump_tests.rs` (new, `cfg(test)`).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/lib.rs`: three module lines appended at the end of the file.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_export.rs`: `FillScratch::reserve` made `pub(crate)`; no other change (see deviations).
- `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle.test.ts` and `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle/` (`dump.ts`, `fixtures.ts`), new.

Design:

- **Stage order (section 7.8).** `StrokeWorkspace::build` applies the stages in this order: `validate_path`, then the stroke values, the tolerance range (−1020 to 1023) and empty. It then runs the front end, `RoundedFillWorkspace::tessellate(pieces, Nonzero, 2^(e−2))` under `STROKE_V0_LIMITS`, `FillScratch::refine`, and finally the empty-region check. Front-end and arrangement errors map through exhaustive matches with no wildcard. The status constants are section 7.2's. A front end that emits no piece returns `empty` without an arrangement call. When more than 1,024 vertices are emitted, the result is the arrangement's own `InputLimit`, but only after every front-end stage, so the front end's own failures come first.
- **Decoding (ST-S1, ST-S2).** It removes zero-length segments, adds the closing line only for `CLOSE` with a distinct last point, applies ST-S10 to open subpaths whose segments are all removed, and enforces the source, segment and subpath caps. A cubic that is not zero-length is `UnsupportedCubic`, which maps to `unsupported-geometry`, until ST-U4.
- **Triples (section 4.2).** For each segment, `n(d)` of the rounded difference gives one offset `r·N`, so both end triples are `C ± r·N`. At an exact forward continuation, the outgoing start triple is replaced by the incoming end triple, which also holds at a closed seam.
- **Joins (section 4.3).** Turn, continuation and reversal come from exact `Signed` cross and dot products of the source differences. The miter predicate is the section's four-case test in `Signed<266>`, with compile-time assertions `PREDICATE_LIMBS ≥ 8·COORDINATE_LIMBS + 2` and `2·LINE_LIMBS ≥ 4·COORDINATE_LIMBS`. The tip uses section 4.3's formula, rounded per operation.
- **Disks (section 4.4).** One 65-entry quadrant-I table is built by bisection at level 6. A level-k polygon takes every `2^(6−k)`-th entry, because bisection gives the same values at every level, and the other quadrants come from exact rotations. The level comes from the count rule with `g_arc`; when no level 1 to 6 qualifies, the result is `DiskLimit`.
- **Hulls.** Andrew's monotone chain with exact `orient2d` keeps collinear boundary points and drops duplicates (−0 is normalized to +0). A hull with zero exact area is dropped. Its vertices are listed counter-clockwise from the lexicographically smallest point.
- **Emission (section 4.9)** per subpath: the start cap; then for each segment its sweep and the join that follows it, with the seam join last on a closed subpath; then the end cap; or a single ST-P7 piece.
- **Memory.** `StrokeWorkspace::new` reserves every buffer once. `build` allocates nothing, which the tracking allocator checks on fixtures, the cap fixtures and the failures.
- **Dump hook.** The ignored test `stroke_dump_tests::emit_stroke_dump` reads `P3STKIN1` (one tab-separated line per case, with every binary64 value given as 16 hexadecimal digits of its bits). It writes `P3STKDUMP1`: one length-framed JSON line per case, with the request, the status, the subpaths, and the segments with their triples and join classes. Each line also has the pieces and the mesh. A piece has its kind, kernel segment, source verb ordinal (the CLOSE ordinal for a closing line), outgoing verb for a join, parameter interval, disk vertex count, a dropped flag, the extra points (join outer points and miter tip, square-cap extension) and its polygon. Every float is given as its bits. A zero-area hull is kept in the piece record with the dropped flag, but it is never passed to the arrangement and does not count toward the 512-piece cap. `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle/dump.ts` converts each entry to ST-U2's `StrokeDumpPiece` shape (`oraclePieces`). In that shape a dropped hull is `polygon: null`, a join carries `outerIn`, `outerOut` and `tip`, and a cap carries `triple` and `extension`. The test `dump_reads_its_input_and_frames_each_case` checks the framing.
- **Test-only seeded defects** live in `StrokeDefects`, with fields under `cfg(test)`. In production every switch is a constant `false`.

**Stage-to-proof table (line strokes).** Here `u = 2^-53`, `M` is the largest source coordinate magnitude, and every bound is a Euclidean distance from an emitted point to its ideal position.

| Piece                     | Emitted points                               | Per-point bound                                                              | Guard and requirement                                         | Piece bound vs share                                                   |
| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ST-P1 sweep               | `A`, `B` exact; `E±` from `C ± fl(r·N)`      | `‖n(d) − d/‖d‖‖ ≤ 5u`; `‖E − E*‖ ≤ 5u·r + u·r + √2·u·(M + r) ≤ 7.5u·(M + r)` | ST-G1 `16u·(M + r) ≤ 2^(e−4)`, with `d̃·d̃` normal and finite   | Hull of points within `g_off`: `≤ 2^(e−4) ≤ 2^e`                       |
| ST-P3 bevel               | `B` exact; `O_in`, `O_out` are triple points | as ST-P1                                                                     | ST-G1                                                         | `≤ 2^(e−4)`                                                            |
| ST-P4 miter               | as ST-P3 plus `T`                            | `‖T − T*‖ ≤ 1.42u·‖B‖∞ + 23.1u·r·L³` (proof below)                           | ST-G3 as tightened: `L ≤ 2^24`, `64u·(‖B‖∞ + r·L³) ≤ 2^(e−1)` | `≤ 2^(e−1) ≤ 2^e`                                                      |
| ST-P5 round join or cap   | disk vertices, `B`, two triple points        | direction `≤ (3.5k + 3)u`; vertex `≤ (3.5k + 6)u·(‖c‖∞ + r) ≤ g_arc`         | ST-G2 inside the count rule; triple points by ST-G1           | Outside the disk by `≤ g_off`; uncovered `≤ sagitta + g_arc ≤ 2^(e−2)` |
| ST-P6 square cap          | triple points, `E± + fl(r·τ̂)`                | `≤ g_off + 6u·r + u·(M + 2r) ≤ 2·g_off`                                      | ST-G1                                                         | `≤ 2^(e−3) ≤ 2^e`                                                      |
| ST-P7 point square, round | `p ± r` (one rounding each); disk            | `≤ u·(M + r)`; ST-P5's arc bound                                             | ST-G1; ST-G2                                                  | `≤ 2^(e−4)`; `≤ 2^(e−2)`                                               |
| All                       | every coordinate                             | finite                                                                       | ST-G6 at every candidate point                                | —                                                                      |
| Resolution                | the union of the pieces                      | exact arrangement up to its rounded embedding                                | `τ = 2^(e−2)`, native assertion (ST-D12)                      | topology row, section 3                                                |

**Guard proofs and tightenings (sections 4.4 to 4.7, line cases).**

- **ST-G1, proved with one range condition.** The rounded direction `d̃` has relative error at most `u` in each component, so its angle error is at most `u`. `d̃·d̃` has relative error at most about `2u`, `sqrt` adds `u/2`, and the division adds `u` per component. So `|n(d)| = 1 ± 3.5u`, and with the angle error `‖n(d) − d/‖d‖‖ ≤ 5u`. Then `fl(r·N)` adds `u·r`, and `C ± fl(r·N)` adds `u·(M + r)` per coordinate, so the total is at most `7.5u·(M + r) < g_off = 16u·(M + r)`. **Tightening:** the argument needs `d̃·d̃` to be a finite normal number. Otherwise it overflows, or its squares underflow and lose their relative bound. ST-U3 therefore makes a squared length that is nonfinite or below `2^-1022` `numeric-range` (`unit_vector`). This only adds failures for segments longer than about `2^511` or shorter than about `2^-511`.
- **ST-G2, proved.** Each bisection level forms `a + b`, whose angle is the mean of its neighbours' angles up to half their length mismatch. It then renormalizes, which adds at most `3.5u` of angle and `3u` of length error. So after `k` levels the direction error is at most `(3.5k + 3)u`. The vertex `c + r·dir` adds `√2·u·(‖c‖∞ + 2r)`. The sum stays below `g_arc = (8k + 16)u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`. Measured against `Math.cos` and `Math.sin`, the level-6 table's largest direction error is `2.83u`, against the bound's `64u`. The count rule's `(1 − 2^-40)` factor covers the rounding of the rule's own evaluation.
- **ST-G3, tightened from `L²` to `L³`.** The contract states `g_tip = 32u·(‖B‖∞ + r·L²)`, with an amplification of at most `L²/2`. That amplification bounds the geometric sensitivity, but not the cancellation in `1 + d̂`. Near a reversal, `1 + d = 2c²` with `c = cos(φ/2) ≥ 1/L`. The normals' length and angle errors of about `5u` each give `|d̂ − d| ≤ 12.2u`, and with the addition `|den − (1 + d)| ≤ 14.3u`. With `L ≤ 2^24`, `den ≥ 0.776·(1 + d)`. Then `|s/den − σ/(1 + d)| ≤ 8.31u/c² + 9.21u/c³ ≤ 17.6u·L³`, and with the remaining roundings `‖T − T*‖ ≤ 1.42u·‖B‖∞ + 23.1u·r·L³`. A fixed-point (2^-300) reference over 2,800 random near-reversal turns measured `max ‖T − T*‖ / (u·ratio³) = 1.46`. At ratio 89.5 the error `1.16e−10` exceeds the contract's `32u·L² = 2.84e−11` four times. ST-U3 therefore uses `g_tip = 64u·(‖B‖∞ + r·L³) ≤ 2^(e−1)`, and treats a kept miter with `L > 2^24` as `numeric-range`. The guard only gets larger, as section 4.5's last paragraph allows before ST-U5. No contract fixture changes outcome: ST-F5, ST-F24 and ST-F25 keep their literal results.
- **ST-G6** is checked at every candidate point before the hull. ST-G4 and ST-G5 belong to ST-U4.

**Memory of `STROKE_V0_LIMITS` (section 7.6).** `RoundedFillWorkspace::new(STROKE_V0_LIMITS)` passes its validation. Its byte totals:

| Target   | Front end | `RoundedFillWorkspace` (cap 32 MiB) | `FillScratch` | Total (cap 48 MiB) |
| -------- | --------: | ----------------------------------: | ------------: | -----------------: |
| x86_64   |   200,864 |                          14,262,272 |     2,654,208 |         17,117,344 |
| `wasm32` |   184,480 |                           9,146,368 |     2,359,296 |         11,690,144 |

The native row is printed by `stroke_v0_limits_fit_the_workspace_and_heap_caps`. The `wasm32` row comes from a throwaway copy of this kernel with one extra export, run in Node. That copy was not committed, and the instance's linear memory after reserve was 12,910,592 bytes. Both rows fit both caps, so the limits table needs no amendment.

Fixture results. Native results come from `stroke_outline_tests.rs`, and the hand-off results from `stroke-oracle.test.ts` on the dumped meshes, with exact BigInt areas and membership. Every `ok` mesh passes `checkFillMesh`. **ST-U2's oracle**, merged into this branch from `claude/p3-b2-st-u2-stroke-oracle` (`c3199aa`), runs on every dumped `ok` and `empty` case (64 of the 67 cases; the other three are the `numeric-range` and `work-limit` cases): ST-O1 (`checkStrokePieceBounds`) and ST-O2 (`checkStrokeInventory`) pass on all of them. ST-O3 (`checkStrokeResolution`) passes on every `ok` case, in `exact` mode for axis-aligned integer fixtures and in the τ band otherwise. ST-O4 (`checkStrokeExactRegion`) passes on every axis-aligned fixture with an exact ideal region.

- **Caps.** ST-F1: area 8, bounds `(0,−1,4,1)`, and the region equals the rectangle under `checkFillRegion` (ST-O4). ST-F2: area 12 and bounds `(−1,−1,5,1)` (ST-O4). ST-F3: two 16-gons and area `8 + 3.0614674589207183` within 1e−12.
- **Joins.** ST-F4: miter `L = 2` gives area 16, tip `(5,−1)` and the exact region; `L = 1` and bevel give 15.5; round gives `15 + 0.7653668647301796`.
- **Miter predicate.** ST-F5: `L = 1.25` keeps the miter, with the tip within `g_tip` of `(25.75,−1)`. `1.2499999999999998` gives a bevel and `1.2500000000000002` a miter. The predicate on `u = (25,0)`, `v = (7,24)` returns the same three answers.
- **Reversal.** ST-F6: miter and bevel give area 8, with the exact region and only the two sweeps; round gives `8 + 1.5307337294603591`.
- **Degenerate subpaths.** ST-F7 and ST-F9: `empty` for every cap and join. ST-F8: butt is `empty`, round gives one 16-gon, square gives area 4 with bounds `(3,4,5,6)` (ST-O4). ST-F10: area 16, with the pieces sweep, miter and sweep.
- **Closed square.** ST-F11 is the same for every cap. Miter gives area 32 and region `[−1,5]² ∖ (1,3)²` (ST-O4), bevel gives 30, round gives `28 + 3.0614674589207183`.
- **Open subpath.** ST-F12: `(0,4)` and `(−0.5,4)` are outside, `(4,4)` and `(4,0.5)` inside (ST-O5).
- **Continuation.** ST-F13: no join piece, the outgoing start triple equals the incoming end triple bit for bit, one boundary loop, and area `25.298221281347036` within 1e−12.
- **Overlap.** ST-F14 (a) gives 12 and (b) gives 20 with three miters, both with exact regions (ST-O3, ST-O4).
- **Thin and out of range.** ST-F21: area exactly `2^-18`. ST-F22 is `empty` and ST-F23 is `numeric-range` for every cap. ST-F24 is `numeric-range` at `L = 2^30`, and at `L = 4` gives a bevel and `ok`.
- **Miter limits.** ST-F25: a miter appears exactly where the ratio is at most `L`, for `sqrt 2`, `5/3` and `sqrt 50` with `L` = 1, `Math.SQRT2`, 4 and 100 (`Math.SQRT2` rounds above `sqrt 2`, so it keeps the 90° miter). Every case is `ok`.
- **ST-F26 current-cap fixtures**, labelled; a limit raise may re-baseline them:

  | Case       | Construction                                                       | Status       | Cause                                                                                                |
  | ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | pieces 512 | closed convex 256-gon, bevel                                       | `work-limit` | 512 pieces, but more than 1,024 vertices (arrangement `InputLimit`)                                  |
  | pieces 513 | open 256-segment staircase, square caps, bevel                     | `work-limit` | `PieceLimit` at the 513th piece                                                                      |
  | edges 1024 | 64 square-capped horizontal subpaths                               | `work-limit` | exactly 1,024 vertices and edges; `max_work` (64,000,000) exhausted in the all-pairs pass            |
  | edges 1025 | 62 such subpaths, a two-segment bevel staircase, two square points | `work-limit` | exactly 1,025 vertices: `InputLimit`, which is reached before `EdgeLimit` because the caps are equal |
  | n = 256    | ST-F1 with round caps, `w = 400`                                   | `ok`         | two 256-gon caps                                                                                     |
  | n = 512    | ST-F1 with round caps, `w = 1000`                                  | `work-limit` | `DiskLimit`                                                                                          |

  Edge accounting confirmed for ST-U2's assumption. `copy_edges` makes one arrangement edge per contour vertex and skips only zero-length edges, and a hull has no duplicate points. So on axis-aligned data a sweep has 6 edges, a bevel 3 and a square cap 5, and the two edge fixtures have exactly 1,024 and 1,025 (`RoundedFillStats.edges = 1,024` on the first). The edge cap is not reachable on its own, because `max_input_vertices` equals `max_edges`. The 1,024-edge case shows that `max_work` binds before the edge cap. In the arrangement, the 512-contour cap cannot bind before the 1,024-vertex cap, since every piece has at least three vertices, as ST-U2 notes. The front end's own `PieceLimit` is checked during emission, before the deferred vertex total, so pieces 513 reports `PieceLimit` even though its vertices also exceed 1,024. Both map to `work-limit`.

Deviations and open items:

- **`fill_export.rs`.** V2's refinement must be reused unchanged, and `FillScratch::reserve` was private. Making it `pub(crate)` is the only edit to this file, which B1 is also changing; the edit is one word on a line B1 does not touch.
- **ST-U2 merged into this branch.** The Primary asked for it, because ST-U2 is not on `main` yet. The merge commit brings in ST-U2's three files and its plan section unchanged; the only conflict was the two plan sections, and both are kept.
- **ST-O4's scope against `idealStrokeExactArea`.** The oracle reports an exact ideal region for the ST-F5 and ST-F25 turns, because their normals are rational (`(7,24)/25`, `(24,7)/25`). The kernel's binary64 samples and the arrangement's τ rounding cannot reproduce those exactly: the symmetric differences are 0.134 to 0.471, inside the τ band. Section 11.1 limits ST-O4 to "axis-aligned segments, integer data, no arcs", so the test applies `exact` mode and ST-O4 only to axis-aligned integer sources, and the band to the others. ST-U2 may want `idealStrokeExactArea`'s documentation, or a helper, to say this.
- **ST-F13 cannot detect ST-D3.** The directions `(3,1)` and `(9,3)` normalize to the same binary64 unit vector, so recomputed triples equal the shared ones bit for bit. ST-U3 adds the supplementary fixture **ST-F13x**, `M(0,0) L(1,1) L(4,4)`, where `(1,1)` and `(3,3)` normalize differently. It catches ST-D3. Its area is checked by ST-O3's τ band, not to 1e−12. The first run with a 1e−12 expectation failed by about `2^-7`, because the arrangement rounds its slanted sections at interior columns. That is the τ band's case, which this unit had not foreseen for a supplementary fixture. The contract's own ST-F13 literal passes unchanged. The Primary may want ST-F13x added to section 11.2 with a dated note.
- **ST-G3 and ST-G1 tightenings** are described above. The Primary records them in the contract with a dated note, since the frozen text gives `L²`.
- **Section 7.7 wording.** At `e = −1020`, `2^(e−4) = 2^-1024` is subnormal, not normal as the section states. `power_of_two` builds subnormal powers exactly, and every guard comparison stays exact. Only the sentence is inaccurate.
- **"Each coordinate rounded once" (section 4.2)** is implemented as `C ± fl(r·N)`. That is two roundings per coordinate, because fused multiply-add is a library call outside section 4.2's allowed operations. ST-G1's bound covers both roundings.
- **`UnsupportedCubic`** is a unit-local front-end error for a cubic that is not zero-length. It maps to `unsupported-geometry` and is removed by ST-U4. No export exists, so no port behavior depends on it.
- **No export, adapter, ABI or contract change.** The release WASM's stroke code is dead code there, until ST-U5 calls it.

### B2 ST-U4 cubic stroke

Unit ST-U4 of the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units), light tier. Plan entry: cubic strokes — the exact stationary classification of section 4.6, the cubic leaves of section 4.5, cusp disks (B2-Q2), guards ST-G4 and ST-G5 and the leaf work limits; ST-U3's interim `UnsupportedCubic` is removed. Acceptance check: the cubic fixtures ST-F15 to ST-F20 meet their literal expectations natively and through the hand-off; ST-U2's ST-O1 to ST-O4 pass on the cubic dumps (the τ band where the ideal is not exact); ST-D6 to ST-D8 fail their named checks; ST-G4 and ST-G5 are proved or tightened. Every result is functional evidence, and precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4. Independent review NOT RUN: the user skipped independent reviews for 2026-10-20 only.

**ST-F16 under `STROKE_V0_LIMITS`: `work-limit`, by user decision (chat, 2026-10-20).** ST-F16's front end completes (91 pieces, ST-O1 and ST-O2 pass), and the unchanged arrangement stops it with `NodeLimit` (`max_nodes` 8,192). Under the kernel's test-only `WIDE_TEST_LIMITS` it is `ok` and meets every original literal (one reversal at `t* = 1/2`, cusp center exactly `(0.5, 0.75)`, `n = 32`, `(0.5, 0.8)` inside). It needs 78,314 nodes, 101,537 sections, 1,076 columns and 553,015,839 work units against the caps 8,192, 262,144, 4,096 and 64,000,000.
