Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_cubic.rs` (new): exact classification (`classify_cubic`), the exact root bisection, the de Casteljau splits and the leaf test (`measure_leaf`).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_cubic_tests.rs` (new, `cfg(test)`), with `WIDE_TEST_LIMITS`.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_outline.rs`: cubic segments and their ST-S2 tangents, classification with ST-G5, collinear parts, reversal ranges, depth-first leaves, cusp disks, the ST-D6 to ST-D8 switches, piece metadata for the hand-off, a 4,096-record piece store whose overflow is `PieceLimit`, and a `cfg(test)` constructor with other arrangement limits.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_dump_tests.rs`: segment classes, piece ranges, depths, indices and sample triples, the failing stage, and an optional ninth input field of test flags (`wide` and the three defects).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_outline_tests.rs`: ST-U3's interim `unsupported-geometry` assertion becomes an `ok` cubic (see deviations).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/lib.rs`: two module lines appended.
- `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle.test.ts`, `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle/dump.ts` and `fixtures.ts`: the cubic cases and their checks.
- `docs/plans/p3-b2-stroke-contract.md`: one dated stricter-guard note after ST-U3's.

Design:

- **Decoding (ST-S1, ST-S2).** A cubic that is not zero-length is a segment with its four controls. Its start tangent is the pair `(P0, Pk)` for the first `Pk` of `P1, P2, P3` that differs from `P0`. Its end tangent is chosen the same way at `P3`. Joins, the miter predicate, the tip and square caps use these exact tangents and their `n(d)`. Source-end triples use the exact end point and `n(tangent)`, as for lines.
- **Classification (section 4.6).** All decisions use `Signed` integers. Collinearity is tested with three exact crosses. In the collinear case, the projected derivative `Ap t² + Bp t + Cp` gives the odd-order roots strictly inside `(0, 1)`: two roots when the discriminant is positive, one when the polynomial is linear, and nothing for a double root. In the other case, `u = Ax By − Ay Bx` and `v = Ax Cy − Ay Cx`. With `u = 0` there is no common root. Otherwise `t* = −v/u` is checked exactly, `A v² − B v u + C u² = 0` in both components. Then `t* ∈ (0, 1)` is a cusp, `t* = 0` or `1` a stationary end, and anything else regular. Each root becomes `t̃`, the largest binary64 `≤ t*`, by bisection on bit patterns with the exact sign of `t − t*`: degree 3 for a ratio, and for a quadratic root a degree-6 comparison of `(2a t + b)²` with `disc`.
- **Collinear cubics.** The pieces are consecutive ST-P1 sweeps between `P0`, the turning points `c_k = C(t̃_k)` (de Casteljau) and `P3`, with a cusp disk at each `c_k`. Part `k` uses the normal `(−1)^k·N0`, where `N0` comes from the start tangent. The knot triple is computed once, and the next part reads it with its outer points swapped, which is the same arithmetic bit for bit. The last part ends at the source end triple.
- **Reversal (cusp).** The cubic is split once at `t̃`. The ranges `[0, t̃]` and `[t̃, 1]` keep the split's controls. The cusp disk at the split point lies between their leaves. The split point is a stationary parameter of both ranges, so its leaf gets the reduced cone, and its normal is the first nonzero of the cone set toward the interior (`D1`).
- **Leaves (section 4.5).** Each range is subdivided by midpoints, depth-first and left-first, from a 22-entry stack. A leaf is accepted by section 4.5's test with ST-U4's terms (guards below). At depth 20 a rejected leaf is `DepthLimit`. An accepted leaf's start triple is the previous leaf's end triple, or the source triple, or, after a split, its own. Its end triple is the source triple at `t = 1`, or `Q3 ± fl(r·n(D2))`, or `D1` at a split end. The leaf hull is ST-P2. A range's leaves tile it as `[index, index + 1]/2^depth`, and the knot triples are shared bit for bit.
  - Correction, 2026-10-21 ([cusp shared triple follow-up](#b2-st-u4-cusp-shared-triple-review-follow-up)): "after a split, its own" departed from contract §4.2, because the cusp knot then had two triples. Since stroke revision 2, the right range's first leaf starts from the left range's last end triple with its outer points swapped.
- **Seeded defects** `knot_join` (ST-D6), `omit_cusp_disk` (ST-D7) and `leaf_factor_two` (ST-D8) are `cfg(test)` switches, constant `false` in production.
- **Test-only wide limits.** `StrokeWorkspace::with_limits` (`cfg(test)`) reserves another `RoundedFillLimits`. `WIDE_TEST_LIMITS`, defined in the contract's §7.6 dated note, is `STROKE_V0_LIMITS` with only `max_events` (events and columns), `max_sections`, `max_nodes` and `max_work` raised, plus `max_bytes` for their storage. The front-end limits, `τ`, `max_input_vertices`, `max_edges`, `max_contours` and the other arrangement fields are the same. It exists only to check current-cap fixtures (ST-F16). Production uses `STROKE_V0_LIMITS`, unchanged.

**Stage-to-proof table (cubic pieces).** `u = 2^-53`, `M` the segment's largest source coordinate magnitude, `ε = (4ℓ + 16s)·u·M` the control error per coordinate (`ℓ` the leaf depth, `s = 1` on a reversal's ranges), `α` the cone half-angle that `sinUp` bounds.

| Piece                | Emitted points                                      | Per-point bound                                            | Guard and requirement                                   | Piece bound vs share                                                                                                          |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ST-P2 leaf           | `Q0`, `Q3`; `C ± fl(r·n(D))`, or the source triples | center `≤ √2·ε`; normal `≤ dN` plus ST-G1's `7.5u·(M + r)` | ST-G4 as tightened, inside the leaf test                | side `≤ 0.75·deltaUp + 3.6ε + r·(2 sinα + dN)`, hull `≤ r·(2 sinα + 2dN)`, plus `13.5u·(M + r)`: at most the test's left side |
| ST-P1 collinear part | source triples; `c_k ± fl(±r·N0)`                   | `c_k` within `η` of `C(t*)`; offsets as ST-P1              | ST-G1; ST-G5 `2η ≤ 2^(e−4)`                             | `≤ g_off + η ≤ 2^(e−3)`                                                                                                       |
| ST-P5 cusp disk      | disk at `c = C(t̃)`                                  | center `≤ η = 64u·M`; vertices as ST-G2                    | ST-G5; ST-G2 inside the count rule                      | center in the curve share; polygon within the arc share of a disk at `c`; the sliver `[t̃, t*]` lies inside `D(C(t*), r + η)`  |
| Classification       | exact                                               | exact (`Signed`, no epsilon)                               | range: a non-collinear cubic needs `2^-250 ≤ M ≤ 2^250` | —                                                                                                                             |

**Guard proofs and tightenings (sections 4.5 to 4.7, cubic cases).** All of these only enlarge the frozen guards. The dated note in the contract records them.

- **Control error.** One midpoint split forms each control by at most three halvings `fl(a + b)·0.5`. Each adds at most `u·M'`, and the errors propagate as convex combinations, so a split adds at most `3u·M'` per coordinate, with `M' ≤ M·(1 + 2^-40)`. The split at `t̃` uses three lerps `a + t·(b − a)`, each within `5.01u·M`, for `15.1u·M`. So `ε = (4ℓ + 16s)·u·M` bounds every control of a leaf against the exact subcurve on its exact dyadic interval of the binary64 range.
- **ST-G4, tightened.** Section 4.5's argument needs four bounds. (1) The exact cone. The exact hodograph coefficients differ from the computed ones by at most `6√2·ε ≤ 8.5ε`. Dot and cross products with `V` also have rounding of at most `4.1u·|V|·|D| ≤ 99u·M²`. Next to a split, the exact limit direction is `2D1 − κD0` with `κ·|V|·|D0| ≤ 288u·M²`, because `|t̃ − t*| ≤ 2^-53`. ST-U4 subtracts `768u·M² + 20ε·|V|` from `a` and adds the same to `b`. (2) Side error. `|chord(t) − O(t)| ≤ 0.75·delta_exact + √2·ε + r·(2 sinα + dN) + 7.5u·(M + r)`, with `delta_exact ≤ delta + 2√2·ε`. (3) Hull term. `r·sin θ ≤ r·(2 sinα + 2dN) + 6u·(M + r)`. (4) Normal error. `|n(D′) − n(De)| ≤ 2|D′ − De|/|De|` and `|De| ≥ aLow/|V|`, so `dN ≤ 18ε·|V|/aLow + 8u`. At a split end, the limit direction adds `576u·M²/aLow`, and `L(t̃)` against `L(t*)` adds `17u·M·|V|/aLow`, which the `16s` part of `ε` covers. A knot's `dN` comes from the leaf that computed it, which may be deeper or shallower, so the next leaf inherits it. The leaf test therefore gains `5ε + 3r·max(dN_start, dN_end)`. The oracle clips a leaf at `t*` and reparameterizes its chord by at most `2^-53/(leaf length)`, so leaves next to a split add `κ·(|V| + 2r)` with `κ = 2^-52/(leaf length)`, as the code charges. The frozen form alone misses three things. `gCtrl = 4ℓu·M·(…)` is 0 at `ℓ = 0`, although a split range already carries `15.1u·M`. `√2·3ℓu·M = 4.24ℓu·M` exceeds `4ℓu·M`. And `768u·M²` cannot hold `8.5ε·|V|` once `ℓ > 10` with `|V|` near `2√2·M`.
- **Range condition (new).** A non-collinear cubic is `numeric-range` unless `2^-250 ≤ M ≤ 2^250`. The upper bound keeps every product (`≤ 24M²`) and the squares in `sinUp` finite. The lower bound keeps `768u·M²` normal, so a product that underflows, with absolute error `≤ 2^-1074`, stays inside the guard. Collinear cubics are lines and have no such condition.
- **ST-G5, proved; `t̃` is an algorithm change (§4.6).** Section 4.6 said "`t̃`, the Float64 evaluation of `t*`". ST-U4 uses the largest binary64 `≤ t*` instead, found by exact bisection, for both reversals and collinear turning points. The bisection gives `|t̃ − t*| ≤ 2^-53`. `C′(t*) = 0` at a reversal, because `t*` is a common root, and at a collinear turning point, because `p(t*) = 0` and `C′` is parallel to the line. So `|C(t̃) − C(t*)| ≤ ½·34M·2^-106` plus the de Casteljau rounding `√2·15.1u·M`, about `21.4u·M < η = 64u·M`. The requirement `2η ≤ 2^(e−4)` is kept. A root below the smallest subnormal (`t̃ = 0`) is `numeric-range`.
- **Observed cost of the frozen absolute term.** `768u·M²` does not shrink with the leaf, but `a ≈ |V|·|D|` shrinks by 4 per level. So at slow parts of a curve, `aLow` becomes nonpositive or `sinUp` stops falling. R6 at `e ≤ −4` and R4 at `e = −11` end in `DepthLimit` for this reason, not geometry. The `20ε·|V|` term ST-U4 adds scales correctly. Replacing `768u·M²` with it would relax a frozen guard, so it is left to ST-OB7, the deferred tighter bound.

**Memory.** Native totals: front end 1,255,120 B, up from 200,864 B because of the 4,096-record piece store and the leaf stack. `RoundedFillWorkspace` is 14,262,272 B and `FillScratch` 2,654,208 B, for a total of 18,171,600 B. This is under the 48 MiB heap cap. `wasm32` was not measured in this unit. `build` allocates nothing on cubic cases either: the tracking allocator covers ST-F16, F17, F18, R5, R6 and R4-width R3, including depth, piece and arrangement limits.

Fixture results. Native results come from `stroke_cubic_tests.rs`, and the hand-off results from `stroke-oracle.test.ts` on the dumped meshes, with exact BigInt areas and membership. Every `ok` mesh passes `checkFillMesh`.

- **ST-F15.** R1 and R2 are collinear, one sweep, and `ok` at every `e`. R3 and R4 are `ok` at `e = −3`, with 1 and 28 leaves. The other `ok` cases are R3 at `e` = 3, 2, 2, −4, −5, −9, −10 (1 to 128 leaves), R4 at 3, 2, 2, −4, −5 (1 to 87), R5 at 3, 2, 2 and R6 at 3, 2, 2. There are 16 `work-limit` cases:

  | Family | `e`               | Cause                                                                        |
  | ------ | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | R3     | −11               | `InputLimit` (256 leaves, more than 1,024 vertices)                          |
  | R4     | −9, −10           | `PieceLimit`                                                                 |
  | R4     | −11               | `DepthLimit` (the frozen `768u·M²` cone term)                                |
  | R5     | −3; −4; −5        | arrangement `NodeLimit`; `EventLimit`; `InputLimit` (58, 114 and 220 leaves) |
  | R5     | −9, −10, −11      | `PieceLimit`                                                                 |
  | R6     | −3                | arrangement `EventLimit` (84 leaves)                                         |
  | R6     | −4, −5, −9 to −11 | `DepthLimit` at the near-reversal (the frozen `768u·M²` cone term)           |

  Under `WIDE_TEST_LIMITS`, R5 at `e = −3` is `ok`, with 69,881 nodes and 120,932,305 work units. R6 at `e = −3` still stops, at 262,144 nodes.

- **ST-F16.** By user decision (above): `work-limit` (`NodeLimit`) at `STROKE_V0_LIMITS`, and every literal met under the wide limits. The leaves tile `[0, 0.5]` and `[0.5, 1]`, with the disk between them.
- **ST-F16p** (added after the ST-A0 review; its literal was fixed before its first run). This is ST-F16's curve and `w = 0.125` at `e = −5`. It is `ok` at `STROKE_V0_LIMITS`, with one cusp disk at `t = 1/2`, center exactly `(0.5, 0.75)` and `n = 8`, and `(0.5, 0.8)` inside, natively and through the hand-off. ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 pass on it. It keeps ST-S8 evidenced at production limits.
- **ST-U4/tip-ratio** (ST-A0 review S2). `M(0,0) L(10,0) L(10,10)`, `w = 20`, miter `L = 1e4`, `e = −3`. It was `numeric-range` under ST-U3's `L³` tip guard. With ST-G3 on the certified actual ratio (contract dated note), it is `ok` with a kept miter, tip `(20, −10)`, and region the square `[0,20]×[−10,10]` of area 400 (ST-O4). ST-F5, ST-F24 and ST-F25 keep their results; ST-F24 at `L = 2^30` is still `numeric-range`.
- **ST-F17.** One turning point at `(0, 0)` with a cusp disk, and two sweep parts. Butt gives `6 + 1.5307337294603591`, square `8 + 1.5307337294603591` and round `6 + 3.0614674589207183`, each within 1e−12.
- **ST-F18.** The start tangent is `(1, 0)`, so the start triple is `(0,−1), (0,0), (0,1)`, and the first leaf takes the reduced cone. `(−0.9, 0.9)` is inside; `(−1.1, 0)` and `(−0.5, 1.1)` are outside.
- **ST-F19.** A collinear continuation with no cusp disk and one sweep. The area is exactly 4, the bounds are `(−1,−1,1,1)`, and the region is the square (ST-O4).
- **ST-F20.** All nine probes are `ok`, classified as endpoint stationary, collinear reversal (one turning point) and collinear continuation by both the kernel and the oracle. ST-O1 and ST-O2 pass on them.

**Oracle results (ST-U2's ST-O1 to ST-O4 on the cubic dumps).** ST-O1 (`checkStrokePieceBounds`, the s1 continuous verifier on both sides of every leaf) and ST-O2 (`checkStrokeInventory`) pass on every cubic case whose front end completed. That is every `ok` case, plus the arrangement-limited ones (ST-F16, R5 at −3 and −4, R6 at −3). ST-O2 also passes on the `InputLimit` cases, whose polygons are not stored. ST-O3 in the τ band passes on the `ok` cubic cases of at most 32 pieces: R1 to R6 where small, R4 at `e = −3` with 28 leaves, ST-F17 to ST-F20 and the folded arc. ST-O4 passes on ST-F19. ST-O3's union-area band was not run on the larger `ok` meshes: it took about 12 s at 28 pieces and 198 s at 87. Those meshes get V2's mesh checks only. On ST-F16 under the wide limits, the oracle's 4,096-edge region cap refuses the band, so ST-F16 is checked by membership and ST-O1, ST-O2.

Seeded defects of section 11.3 assigned to ST-U4. The native test `seeded_cubic_defects_fail_their_named_checks` checks each switch after a control run on the clean workspace. The hand-off test "seeded defects ST-D6 to ST-D8" applies the named oracle:

| ST-D7  | `omit_cusp_disk`  | ST-F16 membership: `(0.5, 0.8)` falls outside; ST-O2 reports the missing cusp disk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ST-D8  | `leaf_factor_two` | The case named before its first run, ST-F15 R6 at `e = −3`, does **not** expose it: ST-O1 still passes, with a largest leaf bound of 0.1246 against 0.125. So, as section 11.3 provides, ST-U4 adds the synthetic folded-band fixture `ST-U4/folded-arc`, its expectation fixed before its first run: a cubic arc of radius 1 over 0.2 rad, `w = 9.94` (`r = 4.97 > 1`), `e = 0`. Factor 4 gives two depth-1 leaves that ST-O1 accepts. Factor 2 accepts the whole arc as one depth-0 leaf, and ST-O1 rejects it ("endpoint error lower bound 0.0198 exceeds the side budget at t = 0.5") |

### B2 ST-U3b scaling probe

Unit ST-U3b of the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units), light tier, decision B2-Q8. Plan entry: an ignored native test that times line strokes of 1 to 512 segments with each join and cap, within the stroke limits, and reports the observation with a recommendation on ST-U9. Acceptance check: the probe runs in the release profile and prints per-stroke times, arrangement counts and growth slopes. **No threshold, no performance claim and no committed result record**: B2-Q8 asks for an observation, and every number below is **diagnostic, not evidence**. Independent review NOT RUN: the user skipped independent reviews for 2026-10-20 only; one diff review is owed under the contract's review plan.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_probe_tests.rs` (new, `cfg(test)`): the probe `stroke_scaling_probe` (ignored) and one ordinary test of its input generator and slope fit.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/lib.rs`: one module line appended at the end of the file.

Design:

- **Inputs.** Open polylines of `n` = 1, 2, 4, …, 512 LINE segments in two families. `zigzag` steps `(16, ±16)`, so every interior vertex turns 90° and pieces overlap only their neighbours. `chords` puts its points on a circle of radius 100 at multiples of the golden angle, so most segments cross many others and crossings grow quadratically. The miter limit is 4.
- **Sweeps.** Table 1 covers family × join × cap × `n` at `w = 2`, `e = −3` (zoom 1, DPR 1 in the s2 mapping), 180 cases. Table 2 covers width at `n = 32` (zigzag; miter/butt and round/round). Table 3 covers the tolerance exponent at `n = 32`.
- **Counts.** Each case has one untimed build on a fresh `StrokeWorkspace`, whose `RoundedFillStats` start at zero, so zero edges means the arrangement never ran. It records the emitted pieces, the emitted vertex total, the arrangement vertices, edges, pair checks, events, sections, nodes, work units and triangles.
- **Timing.** The timed builds reuse one workspace, as production does: one warm-up, then builds until a 200 ms budget is spent (`P3_STROKE_PROBE_BUDGET_MS`), at least one and at most 101. The median is reported. Every timed build must reproduce the counting build's outcome.
- **Labels.** A `work-limit` is labelled `front:pieces` (the 512-piece cap), `front:vertices` (more than 1,024 emitted vertices, the arrangement's preflight `InputLimit`), `front:other` (for example `DiskLimit`), or `arr:<counter>` for the first arrangement counter found at its cap.
- **Slopes.** Ln-ln least squares over the `ok` cases with `n ≥ 8`, or with `n ≥ 2` when fewer than three such cases exist. This is the B0 style of fitted growth slope.

Command (release profile, one thread), run with the pinned toolchain through `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME`:

```text
cargo test --manifest-path packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/Cargo.toml --release --lib --locked \
    --offline stroke_probe_tests -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads 1
```

Environment: Windows 11 Pro, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, Rust 1.94.1, on a shared desktop where other worktrees were building at the same time. Two full runs on the same tree differed by 10-35% on single cases (chords miter/butt at `n = 8`: 23.0 and 31.2 ms). Each run took about 46 s. The numbers below come from the second run, on the tree of PR #171's head `c50ad96`. Its probe file and `lib.rs` are byte-identical to the squash `e4eb2b4` on `main` (checked with `git diff c50ad96 e4eb2b4`, empty). Every median is a **single run of the probe**, and the "Builds" column shows how many timed builds stood behind it. The probe prints its own builds column (`stroke_probe_tests.rs:261` and `:292`), but this table did not copy it. The values here are therefore **derived**, not read from the run: the loop at `stroke_probe_tests.rs:232` keeps building until the 200 ms budget is spent, at least once and at most 101 times, so a median `m` stands behind approximately `min(101, ⌈200 / m⌉)` builds (the loop stops on the sum of the actual samples, so a count can be off by one or more; the probe's own count was not recorded). The two rows above 500 ms are a single sample, and the 168 ms row has two. Added 2026-10-21 (PR #171 review).

**Results, diagnostic, not evidence.** Selected rows of Table 1 (`w = 2`, `e = −3`), median time per stroke:

Beyond those, zigzag fails `front:vertices` and then `front:pieces` (from `n = 256` or 512). Chords fails `arr:nodes` at 16 and 32, `arr:events` at 64 (with miter or bevel joins), and `front:vertices` or `front:pieces` above that.

The other chords series lie between 2.31 and 2.90 for time/n. Chords slopes rest on three points, `n` = 2, 4 and 8, which include the fixed per-stroke cost.

Width (Table 2, zigzag, `n = 32`, `e = −3`), diagnostic, not evidence:

- **Miter/butt.** From `w = 0.0625` to `w = 16` the run stays at 312-315 edges, 777-788 sections and 7.1-8.7 ms. At `w = 64` (four times the step), neighbouring pieces overlap widely: 3,022 sections, 1,850 nodes and 84.3 ms.
- **Round/round.** At `w ≤ 0.25` it has 454 edges and 11-13 ms; at `w` = 1 and 2, 719 edges and 27-32 ms. From `w = 4` it is `work-limit(front:vertices)`, with 1,246 to 4,412 emitted vertices.

Tolerance exponent (Table 3, zigzag, `n = 32`, `w = 2`), diagnostic, not evidence:

- **Miter/butt** is identical from `e = 3` to `e = −14` (315 edges) at 7.2-8.3 ms. Line sweeps and miters do not depend on `e`.
- **Round/round.** At `e` = 3 and 0 it has 455 edges and 12-13 ms, and at `e = −3`, 719 edges and 29.7 ms. At `e` = −6, −9 and −11 it is `work-limit(front:vertices)`, with 2,303, 4,415 and 8,639 emitted vertices. At `e = −14` it is `work-limit(front:other)` (`DiskLimit`: the first round cap needs more than 256 disk vertices).

Observations, diagnostic, not evidence:

- **Every case takes the all-pairs loop.** Pair checks equal `E·(E−1)/2` in every `ok` case (for example 315 edges and 49,455 pairs). The swept crossing collector runs only up to 64 edges (`SWEEP_EDGES`), which a stroke of more than about 10 segments exceeds. Pair checks grow with slope 1.7-2.1. On zigzag, time grows more slowly (slope 1.3-1.5 over `n` = 8-64), because sections and nodes grow linearly there, and time per work unit has slope 0.8-0.9.
- **The limits bind before time does.** On zigzag the 1,024-vertex cap binds at 64-127 segments for line joins and at 33-63 for round joins at zoom 1. With round joins or caps the cap binds already at 32 segments at `e ≤ −6` (zoom 8 or more), and at `w ≥ 4`. A failing `front:` case costs at most 0.9 ms.
- **Crossing strokes are expensive, and they fail late.** Chords reach the 8,192-node cap at 16 segments. Such a build runs most of the arrangement before failing, for 0.15-0.8 s per stroke. The `arr:events` failures at 64 segments fail early (2.1-4.1 ms).
- **Fixed cost.** One segment takes 40-65 µs with butt caps, 75-140 µs with square caps and 220-420 µs with round caps (two disk polygons, 38-42 edges).

**Recommendation on ST-U9** (for the user; no threshold, so this is not a gate decision). Family A alone gives about 5-30 ms per stroke at 32 segments and zoom 1. A scene of hundreds of such strokes is far from interactive. (Amended 2026-10-21, ST-A0 amendments: "far from interactive" is an extrapolation from these per-stroke times, not a measurement.) Two limits also bind early: the 1,024-vertex limit, at about 32 round-joined segments or at any zoom of 8 or more, and the 8,192-node limit, at about 16 self-crossing segments. The probe therefore supports building ST-U9, the certified direct-tessellation fast rung (family B). It would mesh non-overlapping consecutive pieces directly and fall back to family A only where its certificate fails. Since most line strokes overlap only at their joins, as zigzag does, they would avoid the quadratic all-pairs arrangement. A self-crossing stroke still needs the exact path, so ST-U9's contract should also decide one of two things:

- whether a raise of `STROKE_V0_LIMITS` (vertices and nodes) or a swept crossing collector above 64 edges belongs with it;
- or whether the arrangement should stop early once its node or section count makes failure certain. Today a failing crossing stroke costs up to 0.8 s.

These are inputs to the user's decision, not adopted changes.

Added 2026-10-21 (ST-A0 amendments): cubic strokes also reach `work-limit` at the frozen limits, per the ST-U4 fixtures. ST-F16 at `e = −8` needs 78,314 nodes against 8,192; the R5 class stops at `NodeLimit` at `e = −3`; and 64 square-capped line subpaths exhaust `max_work` (ST-F26 `edges-1024`). The cubic data is part of the same pending ST-U9 decision.

Validation: listed on the ST-U3b PR.

### Alt-A1 AL-U4b3 FR5 boxes once

Unit AL-U4b3, the third certificate optimization under [section 5.1](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#51-a-certificate-stage-optimization) of the Alt-A1 contract, light tier with one independent diff review. It applies hot spots 6 and 8 of the [AL-U4a split](#alt-a1-al-u4a-diagnostic-split) to FR5 (SL-C3), the clearance sweep, with classes AL-C1 and AL-C4 only. AL-U4b2 was merged through PR #160 (`ad6ce26`), and B1 through PR #168. The branch starts from `main` `ae91297`. Acceptance check: AL-A11 (`pnpm test:p3-fill-identity` plain mode PASS against `ae91297`, AL-A5, AL-A7, the seeded defects still detected), with AL-A2, AL-A8, AL-A12 and AL-A16. The timings below are diagnostics, **not evidence**. This entry makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder.rs`: FR5 moves from `FastRung::clearance`, `consecutive` and `clear_pair` into the free functions `clearance_edges`, `consecutive` and `clear_pair`, with the new `EdgeBox` and `total_order_key`. `merge_sort` becomes generic over its item type; FR7's call is unchanged. `cfg(test)` hooks are added: `FastRung::clearance_inputs`, `clearance_trace`, the types `ClearanceTestEdge` and `ClearanceTrace`, and the `VISIT_*` outcome codes. No check, threshold, reason or `FastStats` field changes.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_tests.rs`, by additions only: the equivalence tests and their `cfg(test)` copy of the AL-U4b2 FR5.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_diag_tests.rs`: the mirror's FR5 brought up to date, under the Primary ruling recorded in the [AL-U4b1 entry](#alt-a1-al-u4b1-shared-subdivision).

Diagnostic before the change (the AL-U4a command, unchanged, on `ae91297`). The mirror's fidelity holds on 1,270 B0 paths, with 0 differ. Since B1, 1,130 of them pass FR1-FR5 in production, against AL-U4a's 1,121. Mirror shares of its own FR1-FR5 sum: FR2 56.6% (`restrict` 23.3%, terms 34.2%), FR4 31.7% (steps 28.8%, chords 6.9%), FR5 8.6% (box gap 4.1%, sort 2.6%, scan 1.3%) and FR1 3.1% (lookups 1.3%). Production stops, as shares of the fast rung: S1 14%, FR6-FR8 12% and AL-FR9 7%. FR2 and FR4 are interval arithmetic; outcome identity leaves them only AL-C1 and AL-C2, which AL-U4b1 and AL-U4b2 have used, and the Primary ruling excludes AL-C3 from FR2. So FR5 is the largest certificate stage still open to AL-C1 and AL-C4.

Design and decision-equality argument (the code comments carry it):

- **Boxes once per edge, AL-C1.** `EdgeBox` holds xlo, xhi, ylo and yhi, each the same `f64::min` or `f64::max` call on (start, end) that AL-U4b2 made. AL-U4b2 computed xlo and xhi once per edge for the sort and the scan, and recomputed all four extremes of both edges in step 1 for every candidate. Every value read is the same, with no rounding. FR1 has checked that every coordinate is finite.
- **Step 1 gaps lazily, AL-C4.** The four gaps are tested in the same order, gap(xhi(e), xlo(f)), gap(xhi(f), xlo(e)), gap(yhi(e), ylo(f)) and gap(yhi(f), ylo(e)). Each uses the same `Interval::point(low).sub(Interval::point(high))` and `proved_exceeds(gap, r)`. A gap is now computed only when the earlier ones did not decide. The interval operations are pure, and `.any` already stopped at the first decided gap.
- **Integer sort keys, AL-C4.** The merge sort orders (`total_order_key(xlo)`, index) pairs by integer comparison. `total_order_key` is the bit transform of the standard library's `f64::total_cmp`, so every comparison has the result of AL-U4b2's comparator `xlo[a].total_cmp(&xlo[b]).then(a.cmp(&b))`. The same merge sort therefore makes the same moves and gives the same order.
- **By reference, AL-C4.** `consecutive` and `clear_pair` read the two edges by reference, instead of copying two `CertEdge` values per scan visit and per candidate.
- **Fault switches once per call, AL-C4.** Defects (4), (9) and (10) are read once in `clearance_edges`, not once per position, visit or pair. They are test-build switches that no stage changes, and constant `false` outside test builds.
- **The candidate bitset only with debug assertions, AL-C4.** It feeds only a `debug_assert!`, so release builds no longer zero `MAX_EDGES × EDGE_WORDS` words per call (8 KiB since B1).
- **No reordering.** The sort, the scan order and bound, the consecutive skip, the step order, every decided quantity and the counter updates are unchanged. So the result, the reason and `sweep_candidates`, `clearance_step2` and `clearance_step3` are the same for every input.
- **The `observe` callback.** `clearance_edges` passes each visit's outcome to a callback. Production passes a no-op closure, which inlines away; only `clearance_trace` records the visits.

Equivalence tests, both by additions in `fill_ladder_tests.rs`. Both FR5 versions record every scan visit (a, b) in order with its outcome: consecutive skip, box gap, `CROSSING`, simplicity-only pass, clear, or `CLEARANCE` at point test k. They also record the three counters and the result. Equal traces therefore mean the same sort order, the same scan, the same skips, the same step reached for every candidate and the same first failing pair. Each case is compared without faults and under each FR5 defect: (1) with `Fault::R1WideningRemoved`, (4), (9) and (10).

- Debug test `al_u4b3_fr5_boxes_once_decide_as_before`. Its fixtures and real-path cases also compare the hook's result and counters with the production stop after FR5.
  - Fixtures reaching FR5: 90 cases, 810 visits, 353 candidates, 0 differ (66 pass, 15 `CROSSING`, 9 `CLEARANCE`).
  - Debug-sample paths reaching FR5: 195 cases, 8,886 visits, 4,665 candidates, 0 differ.
  - Random edges: 20,000 inputs of one to four closed contours of 1 to 12 edges, with points near a small grid (signed zeros included) or from the AL-U4b1 coordinate families, and δ around the point spacing. That makes 100,000 compared runs, with 466,845 visits and 302,050 candidates: 76,647 box gaps, 225,403 at step 2 and 84,306 at step 3. 0 differ: 10,173 pass, 67,874 `CROSSING` and 21,953 `CLEARANCE`.
- Ignored release test `al_u4b3_full_corpus_fr5_boxes_once_decide_as_before`, every corpus path that passes FR1-FR4:
  - B0 framed corpus: 5,905 cases, 405,837 visits, 210,750 candidates, 0 differ.
  - `p3-b0r-random/v1`: 42,465 cases, 1,920,757 visits, 1,183,947 candidates, 46,370 at step 3, 0 differ.
- Four mutations were run. Three were detected: using f's box twice in the fourth gap made 26 fixture cases differ, skipping defect (10)'s switch made 18 differ, and the raw bits as the sort key made 15 debug-sample cases differ. The fourth, using e's box twice in the second gap, was not detected, and is equivalent. In sorted order xlo(f) ≥ xlo(e), so gap(xhi(f), xlo(e)) ≤ 0 can never prove a gap above r > 0.

**Diagnostic, not evidence.** The AL-U4a command, unchanged. For same-session comparisons, the same diagnostic file was also run against `ae91297`'s kernel crate, exported with `git archive` to a scratch directory, alternating with the branch: three pairs on the final code. Every run passes, with mirror fidelity 1,270 B0 paths and 0 differ, and every kernel cross-check holds. The new `FR5.AL-U4b2` kernel, the AL-U4b2 FR5 kept in the mirror, passes with the same candidate count on every timed path.

Medians, summed over the 26 cells, in µs:

| Run pair | `ae91297` production FR5 stop | Branch production FR5 stop | Mirror FR5 / `FR5.AL-U4b2` (branch run) |
| -------- | ----------------------------: | -------------------------: | --------------------------------------: |
| 1        |                        251.84 |                     398.53 |                  388.01 / 421.58 (0.92) |
| 2        |                        746.23 |                     741.30 |                  601.48 / 641.64 (0.94) |
| 3        |                      1,170.34 |                   1,524.91 |                  641.87 / 718.06 (0.89) |

Other sessions were running on the machine, and the production stop differences are noise-dominated: S1, which neither side changes, ranged from 1,126 to 1,920 µs between runs. The production FR5 stop shows no resolvable change. Only the same-run kernel comparison isolates this change, and it shows a gain of about 6 to 11% of FR5. FR5 is about 5% of the fast rung, so this unit moves the fast rung by well under 1%. In the base runs, whose mirror is the same file, the ratio was 0.94, 0.94 and 0.91.

A first variant also stored FR2's controls in place and read FR2's edges by reference (the AL-U4a "FR2 residual", AL-C4). Its equivalence test, with bit comparisons of every stored control, every `deviation_squared` and `max_deviation_squared`, passed. Its same-run FR2 kernel ratio against the AL-U4b2 FR2 was 0.95 and 1.04 in two runs, which is noise. It was removed from this unit, because it gave no measurable gain.

### B2 ST-U5 stroke export

Unit ST-U5 of the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units), full tier under the approved and frozen contract. Scope: the `stroke_v0_*` export and `StrokeEngine` (section 7), the `StrokeMeshPort` types (section 6), `createStrokeMeshPort` (section 8), the conformance suite SC01-SC22 and its double (section 11.4), the transport fixtures (section 11.2), and the section 1.3 documents for ST-U5. Acceptance: ST-A7 (transport), ST-A8 (SC01-SC22 on the WASM adapter and the double), ST-A9 (nothing else changes) and ST-A12. Every result is functional evidence, and precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4. Reviews: the ST-A0 contract review ran. Its verdict was NOT READY: its B1 and S2 findings were fixed in ST-U4 (#173), and the remaining findings are contract amendments still to land. This unit's independent diff review ran on PR #174 and was NOT READY, on merge-gate items only (the code matched the contract). Those items are resolved on this branch: the merge of `main` with ST-U4, the reruns with their base hashes recorded, the re-measured memory rows, and the reviews recorded.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_export.rs` (new): `StrokeInput` framing, `run_batch`, `StrokeEngine`.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_export_tests.rs` (new, `cfg(test)`).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/lib.rs`: lines appended at the end only (the module, its tests module, `GLOBAL_STROKE_ENGINE` and the twelve `stroke_v0_*` exports).
- `packages/contracts/src/geometry.ts`: the section 6 types, appended verbatim.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/src/stroke-adapter.ts` (new) and `index.ts` (two export lines).
- `tests/support/stroke-mesh-port-conformance.ts`, `tests/support/stroke-mesh-port-double.ts`, `tests/unit/stroke-mesh-port-double.test.ts`, `tests/geometry/stroke-mesh-port.test.ts`, `tests/geometry/stroke-adapter.test.ts` (new).
- Dated pointers in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, ADR 0002, `docs/graphics-engine-architecture.md` and `docs/plans/p3-private-contract.md` (section 1.3). No frozen text is edited. The V2 contract needs no change, and the V3 notes belong to ST-U6.

Design:

- **Identity (7.1).** `stroke_v0_version() = 0`, `stroke_v0_revision() = 1`, input magic `VGT0`, output magic `VGU0`, `operationId` `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0'`. `abi_version()` and `fill_v0_*` are unchanged.
- **Engine (7.2, 7.5).** `StrokeEngine` is the third module-level engine. It has its own input and output arenas, epoch, result length, requirement and one `StrokeWorkspace`. It shares no field with `Engine` or `FillEngine`. `reserve`, `process` and `dispose` follow `FillEngine` line for line: reserve grows the arenas and never shrinks them; it builds the workspace once and checks the combined heap against `STROKE_V0_HEAP_CAP` with checked arithmetic (a refusal is `INTERNAL_ERROR`, an allocation failure `ALLOCATION_FAILED`). Each mutating call advances the epoch once, with saturation; dispose is terminal and idempotent; `process` allocates nothing.
- **Framing (7.3, 7.4).** V2's input rules, with 32-byte request records (`requestId`, `cap`, `join`, `i32` exponent, `f64` width, `f64` miter limit). A `cap` or `join` above 2 is `INVALID_BATCH`. Width, miter-limit bits, verbs, points and the exponent are path content. Output is V2's exactly: a 64-byte header written last, 80-byte records, contiguous blocks, and zero beyond the echoes and the status on non-`OK` records.
- **Per path.** `run_batch` calls `StrokeWorkspace::build` from main unchanged. It owns the stage order of section 7.8 and the exhaustive `map_front_end_error` and `map_rounded_error`, and it publishes `StrokeWorkspace::mesh()`. The export adds no geometry. Since ST-U4 merged (#173), `build` carries cubic strokes, and the export carries them with no change here.
- **Adapter (8).** `stroke-adapter.ts` mirrors `fill-adapter.ts`. The `fillRule` item of `invalid-request` becomes: `cap` or `join` not exactly one of its strings, and `width` or `miterLimit` not of type `number`. A number that is invalid passes through and returns `invalid-stroke`. It also has V2's one capacity retry, its fail-closed list, and its self-check rule (`batch-failed: internal-error` without disposing). The fill adapter is not touched.

**Status mapping (7.2, 7.8).** Batch statuses are P2's (`OK=0` to `INTERNAL_ERROR=6`). Per path: 0 `ok`, 1 `empty`, 2 `invalid-path`, 3 `invalid-tolerance`, 4 `numeric-range`, 5 `work-limit`, 6 `unsupported-geometry`, 7 `invalid-stroke`. The adapter rejects any other code by failing closed. Kernel rows follow the contract's table. Front-end source, segment, subpath, depth, piece and disk limits map to `work-limit`, `NumericRange` to `numeric-range`, and an invariant to a batch `internal-error`. Arrangement limits map to `work-limit`, `TopologyAmbiguous` to `unsupported-geometry`, `InvalidTolerance` to `invalid-tolerance`, and the rest to a batch `internal-error`. ST-U3's interim `unsupported-geometry` for cubics is gone since ST-U4. `former_interim_cubics_are_ok_through_the_export` shows ST-F16p, ST-F17 and ST-F19 `ok` through the export, with ST-F19's exact area 4. ST-F16 itself, and ST-U3's former interim fixture at `w = 2`, `e = −3`, are current-cap `work-limit`, as the 2026-10-20 ST-F16 decision states.

**Memory at reserve (section 7.5),** measured on this branch after merging `main` with ST-U4 (`e4ec82d`):

| Target   | Front end | `RoundedFillWorkspace` | `FillScratch` | Heap total (cap 48 MiB) | Linear memory after `stroke_v0_reserve(0, 0)` |
| -------- | --------: | ---------------------: | ------------: | ----------------------: | --------------------------------------------: |
| x86_64   | 1,255,120 |             14,262,272 |     2,654,208 |              18,171,600 |                                             — |
| `wasm32` |         — |                      — |             — |                       — |                 13,828,096 (1,114,112 before) |

The native row is printed by `stroke_outline_tests::stroke_v0_limits_fit_the_workspace_and_heap_caps`. The `wasm32` row comes from the release WASM in Node: a fresh instance, `stroke_v0_reserve(0, 0)` and `memory.buffer.byteLength`. That growth bounds the wasm32 heap. The kernel has no export for the per-part bytes there. `heap_cap_is_checked_at_reserve` checks the native heap against the cap at the limit and one byte below it.

Correction, 2026-10-20: ST-U3's recorded native row (front end 200,864 B, total 17,117,344 B) does not reproduce at `ae91297`. Re-measured there with the same test, the front end is 376,992 B and the total 17,293,472 B, and ST-U5's first measurement before the merge also gave 13,107,200 B of `wasm32` linear memory. The rows above, measured after ST-U4, supersede both. ST-U3's text is left as written.

**Conformance (ST-A8).** SC01-SC22 pass on the double under `pnpm test:unit` (22 of 22) and on the WASM adapter under `pnpm test:geometry` (22 of 22). No case needs a cubic, so none waits on ST-U4. After the merge with ST-U4, every SC case gives the same results as before; none changed because cubics are supported. SC15 checks ST-F1, ST-F2, ST-F6, ST-F11 miter and ST-F14 (a) and (b) with `checkStrokeExactRegion` (ST-O4) and an exact area. SC20 checks the ST-F1 to ST-F3 cap areas and the ST-F4 areas per join and limit, with round areas within 1e-12. SC17 sends MOVE + 65,537 LINE. SC21 checks `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0'` on the WASM run and the double's own id. SC22 creates a fill adapter and a stroke adapter from one compiled module (the double run uses the two doubles), disposes each first in turn, and checks that the other stays usable. The double's meshes are hand-built unions of unit grid cells and 16-gon fans.

**Transport (ST-A7).** Native `stroke_export_tests.rs`, 18 tests: identity; the mapping rows; 25 framing rejections, including a cap or join above 2 and non-zero padding; width and limit bits as path content; the empty batch; the stage order with every reachable per-path status except `unsupported-geometry` (see open items); the export's meshes equal to `StrokeWorkspace::build` bit for bit, with the literal areas of ST-F1 to ST-F4, ST-F6, ST-F8, ST-F11, ST-F14 and ST-F21; the exact capacity retry; the input and output caps at the limit and limit + 1; epochs and their exhaustion; dispose; construction failure and retry; the heap cap; the self-check `INTERNAL_ERROR` and recovery; zero allocation and unchanged storage in `process`, on both the `OUTPUT_CAPACITY` pass and the published pass; determinism; echoes; SC17 natively; and the former interim cubics through the export. `tests/geometry/stroke-adapter.test.ts`, 57 tests: the factory checks, isolation from a second stroke adapter, a fill adapter and a P2 session, engine separation inside one instance, the batch rules, the `invalid-stroke` pass-through, `i32` encoding, the one retry, resource-limit mapping, the self-check rule, path statuses 2-7, and 43 fail-closed triggers through wrapped exports.

Validation, 2026-10-20, on the merge commit `5250ce5` (`main` at `e4ec82d`, with ST-U4, merged in). The first runs on `09a3068`, against base `ae91297`, also passed and are superseded by these:

- **`pnpm test:geometry`** (with `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME` at the main checkout's `.tools`): PASS. The fixture generators pass, and so do `cargo fmt --check` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`. Native: 438 passed and 54 ignored. The 260 native intersection certificates are verified. The release WASM is identical to an independent rebuild, `d93556cc…6f37d1`. Vitest: 51 files passed and 4 skipped; 892 tests passed and 6 skipped, including SC01-SC22 on the WASM adapter and `stroke-oracle.test.ts`.
- **`pnpm check`**: PASS on the rerun. The first run had one known 5-second load timeout in `geometry-cubic-boundary.test.ts`. The rerun passed: format, lint, typecheck and boundaries, plus unit tests in 69 files and 937 tests, including the double's SC01-SC22.
- **`pnpm build`**: PASS.
- **`pnpm test:p2-identity origin/main`**, base **`e4ec82d`**: PASS. The P2 output identity holds over 10,048 batches, SHA-256 `876f4a49…046f`. Main's release WASM is `65a9f3c0…1212b` and the branch's is `d93556cc…6f37d1`; they differ because the stroke code is now exported.
- **`pnpm test:p3-fill-identity --base origin/main`**, plain mode, base **`e4ec82d`**, head `5250ce5`: PASS. `fill_v0_revision()` is 2 on both. The native, WASM and WASM-vs-native digests are all equal over 76,352 native and 6,041 WASM cases, and the coverage floors are met. Both tamper self-checks are detected, and the `b0r_oracle_full` oracles pass (16 tests, 3,070 s).
- **`git diff --check`** PASS. `git diff --exit-code origin/main` on `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` exits 0, so no dependency changed.

Deviations and open items:

- **`unsupported-geometry` has no export fixture.** Since ST-U4 it is reachable only through `TopologyAmbiguous`, for which no stroke fixture is known. The mapping row is covered by `status_mapping_rows_of_section_7_8` and by the adapter's path-status test.
  - Correction, 2026-10-21 ([cusp shared triple follow-up](#b2-st-u4-cusp-shared-triple-review-follow-up)): strokes did reach `unsupported-geometry`. Revision 1 failed with `TopologyAmbiguous` on 4 of the 40 random exact cusps in the ST-U4 post-merge review's corpus, because the cusp knot had two triples. Revision 2 removes that cause, and those 4 cases are `ok`. The identity above (`stroke_v0_revision() = 1`, `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0'`) is now revision 2, `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r2'`.
- **The fill adapter is not shared.** `stroke-adapter.ts` duplicates `fill-adapter.ts` rather than factoring a common core, so that V2's adapter and its identity stay byte-for-byte unchanged. A later cleanup may merge them.

### B2 ST-A0 contract amendments

Documentation-only follow-up to the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md), applying the ST-A0 contract review (verdict NOT READY: two blocking findings, eight SHOULD-FIX, five minor). Full tier (corrected from light by the independent review): it changes no requirement, threshold, user decision (B2-Q1 to B2-Q9, the ST-F16 decision, V1-Q4) or past evidence text, and it adds dated notes only, but its notes amend the ST-A0 acceptance criterion, a scene-validation outcome (S3) and a public mirror option (S4). Acceptance check: every adopted finding below has a dated note in the contract, every literal in those notes is backed by committed code or plan text on `main`, and the three documentation checks pass. Independent review of this diff (PR #176): ran 2026-10-21, verdict NOT READY (one blocking item: the ST-A0 amendment had dropped the READY requirement; six SHOULD-FIX items; two minor). All items were applied on the same branch; a re-review is the Primary's to arrange. **Pending user decision:** ST-U9 (the certified fast rung), on the ST-U3b line probe and the cubic `work-limit` data, escalated to the user in chat on 2026-10-20; nothing is decided by this unit.

**Review timeline.** ST-U3 (#169) and ST-U5 (#174) merged before the findings were applied, under the user's 2026-10-20 exception that skipped independent reviews for that day. The review did run, before ST-U5 merged. The gate is now: these amendments land, with an independent review of them READY (or READY WITH FOLLOW-UPS and its follow-ups recorded), before ST-U6 merges. Adopted findings apply to code already on `main`.

Nothing further was missing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

The non-crossing cases (chords cannot cross; reflex vertex with a hull term below 2r·sinα) are proved after the review; θ ≥ π/2 in the crossing case is covered by ST-O1. |

### B2 ST-U4 cusp shared triple (review follow-up)

Light-tier plan entry, 2026-10-21. It is a post-merge review follow-up to ST-U4 (#173): the review's finding SF1, plus minors M1 and M2 and minors M4 and M5 of the #169 review. It restores the frozen contract's §4.2 property and adds no behaviour. Acceptance check:

- the review's two reproductions are `ok` with literal membership, fixed before their first run;
- a native test shows one bit-identical mirrored triple at each cusp knot;
- the review's 100-case corpus changes no status except `unsupported-geometry` → `ok`, and changes `ok` meshes only in the right range's first start triple;
- the stroke identity moves to revision 2 under contract §7.1;
- the fill and P2 identities are unchanged;
- `pnpm test:geometry`, `pnpm check` and `pnpm build` pass.

Precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, as for every stroke result.

**Finding.** At a non-collinear reversal, the right range's first leaf computed its own start triple, `Q0 ± fl(r·n(D1))`. It did not reuse the left range's end triple. In exact arithmetic the two are mirror images; in binary64 they differed by 1 to 4 ulps. So the cusp knot had two triples, contrary to §4.2, and the ST-U4 entry above recorded the departure only as "after a split, its own". The arrangement's `embed_scalar` could then not keep a strict order, and failed with `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`, which maps to `unsupported-geometry`. This happened on 4 of the review's 40 random exact cusps.

**Fix.** Files:

- `stroke_outline.rs`: `LeafRange.source_start: Option<Triple>` becomes `start: (Triple, f64)`, the start triple with its normal error bound. `emit_range` returns its last end triple and bound. The cusp branch passes the left range's end triple, with `E−` and `E+` swapped, as the right range's start, the way collinear parts already share their knot triple. The path that computed a start triple after a split is removed.
- `stroke_cubic.rs`: `LeafContext.start_error` becomes `f64`, and `LeafMeasure.start_direction`, now unused, is removed. The guard formulas are unchanged.
- `stroke_export.rs` and `stroke-adapter.ts`: revision 2 (below).

**Error accounting.** The right range's first leaf now inherits the left's normal. Its bound:

1. For a non-collinear exact reversal, the hodograph factors as `D(t) = (t − t*)(A·t + w)` with `A·t* + w ≠ 0`. Otherwise `D = A·(t − t*)²` would be parallel to `A` everywhere, and the cubic would be collinear (section 4.6, case 3).
2. So the one-sided limit directions at `t*` are exactly opposite, `L_R = −L_L`, and the reference normals satisfy `N_R* = −N_L*`.
3. The swapped triple is exactly `triple(c, −fl(r·n))`, since `a + (−b) = a − b` in IEEE arithmetic. Its normal is the left's computed normal, negated bit for bit.
4. Its normal error against `N_R*` therefore equals the left leaf's computed-normal error against `N_L*`. That error is bounded by the left leaf's `end_error`, `dN = (18ε·|V_L| + 600u·M²)/aLow_L + 8u`.
5. That bound already holds the split end's one-sided limit-direction term, `576u·M²/aLow ≤ 600u·M²/aLow` (ST-G4 (4) above). It also holds the `L(t̃)` against `L(t*)` term, `17u·M·|V|/aLow`, which the `16s` part of `ε` covers. No further term is needed.
6. The right leaf charges this bound through the unchanged `gCtrl` term, `3r·max(dN_start, dN_end)`. This is the inheritance rule that ST-G4 already states for interior knots: "a knot's `dN` comes from the leaf that computed it".
7. No guard formula is loosened. The value supplied is a proven bound for the triple actually emitted.
8. The right first leaf's start term can now be smaller than its former self-computed one. That former term bounded a triple that is no longer emitted. In the corpus, no leaf's acceptance changed: piece counts, depths and indices are equal before and after.

**Identity (contract §7.1).** The fix changes `ok` meshes (below), so the rule "a change that can alter an `ok` stroke mesh ... changes the `operationId` and the revision together" applies, and the failure-to-success exemption does not. `stroke_v0_revision()` becomes 2 and `operationId` becomes `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r2'`, in the kernel, the adapter and its type, `stroke-adapter.test.ts` and the SC21 option of `stroke-mesh-port.test.ts`. The double keeps its own id. The fill operation and revision are untouched. The Primary decided this on 2026-10-21. ST-U6 has not landed, so nothing caches stroke meshes yet. The contract carries a dated note.

**Corpus regression.** The input is the review's 100-case corpus (40 random exact cusps, 40 random regular cubics, 20 collinear cubics), generated by the review's `gen2.mjs` (SHA-256 `425f7882…5746`) as `in.txt` (`044c23b6…478e`). It was dumped with `emit_stroke_dump` before the fix on `2940bee` (`before.txt`, `c3001537…ae74`) and after it (`after.txt`, `83034d34…cb37`). ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 then ran on every complete case, with the review's harness. The dumps were compared field by field: status, failure, mesh, and every piece's kind, parameters, depth, index, samples and points. The comparison scripts are ad hoc and are not committed. They and the dumps are in the implementer's session scratch directory, and the hashes above identify the dumps.

| Change                                 | Cases                                          | Detail                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| bit-identical                          | 85                                             | status, mesh and pieces equal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `unsupported-geometry` → `ok`          | cusp23, cusp24, cusp29, cusp39                 | each `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)` before; ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 pass after                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ok`, mesh changed                     | cusp8, cusp9, cusp22, cusp25, cusp30           | piece inputs differ only in the right range's first start triple (1, 1 to 2, 1 to 2, 2 and 64 ulps; cusp30's 64 is in an `x` near zero); the meshes then differ downstream with fewer vertices (68→60, 102→84, 104→90, 66→56, 326→322) and triangles (66→58, 100→82, 102→88, 64→54, 324→320); ST-O1 and ST-O2 pass, and ST-O3 on the four of at most 12 pieces |
| `work-limit`, samples changed, no mesh | cusp10, cusp11, cusp13, cusp14, cusp21, cusp36 | only the right range's first start triple                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

Before the fix, 15 corpus cases had unmirrored knot triples: the 11 that did not fail (the 5 `ok` and 6 `work-limit` rows) and the 4 failures the defect caused. After it, there are none. Before the fix the counts were 70 `ok`, 4 `unsupported-geometry` and 26 `work-limit`, the same as the review's own run. After it they are 74 `ok` and 26 `work-limit`. No oracle check failed in either run. No status changed in any other way.

**Fixtures** (native, `stroke_cubic_tests.rs`). Each literal was fixed before its first run, and each passed on its first run:

- **`ST-U4/cusp-shared-a`.** `M(0,0) C(−0.037109375,−0.22265625)(−0.0302734375,−0.21875)(0.1455078125,1.01171875)`, `w = 1`, butt, `e = −3`. It is `ok`, classified `Cusp` at `t̃ = 19/64` with one cusp disk. `(−0.018, −0.112)` (the cusp) and `(0.184, −0.112)` (0.4r from the centerline) are inside. `(−1.02, −0.112)` (2r) is outside.
- **`ST-U4/cusp-shared-b`.** `M(0,0) C(0,0.095703125)(0.7177734375,0.703125)(0.2783203125,0.322265625)`, `w = 0.25`, round, `e = −1`. It is `ok`, `Cusp` at `t̃ = 49/64` with one disk. `(0.42, 0.4465)` and `(0.1755, 0.2865)` are inside, and `(0.67, 0.4465)` is outside.
- **`st_u4_cusp_knot_has_one_mirrored_triple`.** On both cases and on ST-F16p, the leaves on either side of the disk share one parameter. The right leaf's start triple is the left leaf's end triple with `E−` and `E+` swapped, compared by bits. The disk center is the same point.
- **M1, `st_u4_reversal_near_a_segment_end_is_depth_limited`.** The review's `cusp-near-start-2^-20` and `cusp-near-end-2^-20` probes, `w = 0.25`, butt, `e = −3`, are `work-limit` with `Front(DepthLimit)`. This is a listed `work-limit` class and an ST-OB7 input (ST-U4 deviations above).
- **M2.** `tip_guard_values` adds `fl(1 + d̂) = 79u`, which is refused because `ρ` is just above `2^24`, and `80u`, which passes with `ρ = 2^27/√65`. Both use `r = 1e-12`, so only the ratio check decides.
- **M4.** ST-D5 and ST-D11 assert `numeric-range` (ST-U3 correction above).
- **M5.** `StrokeWorkspace::piece_points` returns `Option` through `get`. `piece_points_after_an_input_limit_overflow` checks that ST-F15 R3 at `e = −11` (`InputLimit`) has a piece whose points were not stored, and that the call returns `None` for it instead of panicking.
- The dump hook and the three test callers are adapted to the `Option`.

### B2 ST-U2 oracle hardening (review follow-up)

Light-tier plan entry, 2026-10-21. It is the post-merge review follow-up to ST-U2 (PR #169): the SHOULD-FIX item S1 and the minors M1, M2, M3 and M6, with documentation minors of the ST-U3b (#171) and AL-U4b3 (#175) reviews. The branch is stacked on `claude/p3-b2-st-u4-cusp-shared-triple` (PR #179), because it edits the same kernel and test files; once #179 merges, `origin/main` is merged in. It changes no kernel output, no export output and no threshold; it adds one dated contract note. Acceptance check (revised 2026-10-21 after review):

- the new positive controls are rejected and the whole corpus still passes the stricter checks;
- `stroke_v0` status and mesh are unchanged on all 145 dump cases; the release WASM bytes may change, because M3 adds a store to the piece record that only the dump reads;
- `pnpm test:geometry`, `pnpm check` and `pnpm build` pass.

Precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, as for every stroke result.

**S1, ST-O3 band mode checked only the area.** `checkStrokeResolution` in `band` mode compared `|area(union) − area(mesh)|` with `perimeter·τ` and ignored the symmetric difference, so a mesh of equal area moved far away passed (the review's ST-F1 sweep moved to `[100,104]×[50,52]`). Band mode now also requires `xor ≤ perimeter·τ·(1 + 2^-40)`, with the same allowance that the area check uses.

- **Measured first, before the bound was added.** Every band-mode call of the corpus was instrumented: `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle.test.ts` (22 line cases through the band path and 50 cubic cases of at most 32 pieces, 72 instances in all) on the ST-U4 cusp-shared-triple tree. The maximum ratio `xor / (perimeter·τ·(1 + 2^-40))` per family:

  | Family | Instances | Maximum xor / allowance | Case at the maximum | Maximum area difference / allowance |
  | ------ | --------: | ----------------------: | ------------------- | ----------------------------------: |
  | line   |        22 |                  0.2156 | `ST-F13`            |                              0.0420 |
  | cubic  |        50 |                  0.2268 | `ST-U4/folded-arc`  |                              0.0765 |

  No instance exceeds 0.23 and none needs a corner term, so the bound is added **without** one. The instrumentation was removed before the commit. The unit fixtures (`tests/unit/geometry-stroke-region.test.ts`) have two accepting band cases: the boundary moved by `2^-8` (xor `2^-8`, allowance about 0.4) and the new mesh translated by `2^-8` (xor `2^-6`).

- **Justification from the contract.** Section 3 gives the resolution share: the arrangement resolves the union of the emitted pieces up to its rounded embedding at `τ = 2^(e−2)`. When each boundary point of the union is a one-sided displacement of the mesh boundary by at most `τ` (the kernel declares `error_bound = τ`), the symmetric difference is at most `τ·perimeter`. The strict two-sided bound is the `τ`-tube, about `2τ·perimeter + π·τ²` per loop, which allows a hole just inside an edge and a flap just outside; that is the hole-closing residual the ST-A0 amendment names. The allowance `perimeter·τ·(1 + 2^-40)`, with no corner term, is therefore a test band justified by the measured margin (at most 23% used), not a consequence of section 3. A result over the band on a new fixture means re-checking the band before blaming the kernel.
- **This is stricter than §11.1's wording.** The table says "area difference at most perimeter times `τ`". A dated 2026-10-21 note in the contract says so; the frozen text is unchanged.
- **Positive control.** `ST-O3 band control: a translated mesh of equal area is rejected` (`tests/unit/geometry-stroke-region.test.ts`): ST-F1's sweep with the mesh moved to `[100,104]×[49,51]` has mesh area equal to the union area and is rejected on the symmetric difference, and the `2^-8` translation is accepted.

**M1, exact-mode scope.** `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle.test.ts` treated a region as exact when the ideal pieces were exact polygons and the input was axis-aligned. The oracle now also requires every ideal vertex to round-trip through binary64 (`idealStrokeVerticesBinary64`): `M(0,2^40) L(1,2^40)` with `w = 2^-30` has ideal corners `2^40 ± 2^-31`, which need 71 bits, so it takes the band. `axisAligned` consumed 2 scalars for a CUBIC verb; it now consumes 6 and accepts a cubic only when its control points and end lie on one axis line through the current point. A unit test covers the 71-bit example and ST-F1.

**M2, ST-O1 disk count.** `StrokeDumpPiece` carries the dumped `disk` count as `diskVertices` on round caps, round joins, cusp disks and round point caps. ST-O1 compares it with `strokeDiskVertexCount(r, e, ‖c‖∞)` of the oracle for the piece's center, and for the pieces that are a bare disk (cusp disk, round point cap) requires the hull to have exactly `n` vertices, so the disk polygon is its own hull (simple and convex). Controls: a reported count of 8 where the rule gives 16 (ST-F3 cap), and a cusp disk with one vertex removed (ST-F16).

**M3, the round cap's own triple.** `stroke_outline.rs` records a round cap's `E−` and `E+` in the piece's `extra`, as square caps record their extension points, and `dump.ts` reads them as the cap's triple (the center is the segment's). ST-O2 therefore compares the cap's own `E±` with the sweep triple at the end. Control: an `E−` replaced by `E+` on the ST-F3 end cap is rejected as not sharing the sweep triple; that oracle check already existed, so the control shows the check works on synthetic input. What M3 fixes is `dump.ts` taking the cap triple from the segment record, which made the comparison circular; its coverage is the integration run on the real dump. `extra` is read only by the dump test, so no mesh and no export byte moves. The release WASM comparison is below.

**M6, dump case names.** `stroke_dump_tests.rs` writes case names through `json_escape`, which escapes the double quote, the backslash and every control character; a native test covers them.

**Documentation minors (no code).** The ST-U3b table gains a derived "Builds" column and the measured tree's commit; the AL-U4b3 entry records its final base.

Files: `packages/geometry-reference/src/stroke-region.ts`, `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_outline.rs` (one `set_extra` call and one comment), `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/stroke_dump_tests.rs`, `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle.test.ts`, `tests/geometry/stroke-oracle/dump.ts`, `tests/unit/geometry-stroke-region.test.ts`, `docs/plans/p3-b2-stroke-contract.md` (one dated note), and this plan.

**Release WASM (M3).** It is **not** byte-identical to its base. With the change, the independent-build release WASM (`pnpm test:geometry`, two builds agree) is `480cb633…2b50`. The base (`HEAD` of #179, the same command and flags in a separate target directory) is `66324bcf…b449`. The ST-U5 export is live code, and the piece store is a live write, so the extra `set_extra` call changes the compiled bytes. The mesh outputs are unchanged: the 145 dump cases (all line and cubic fixtures) were dumped with the base and with the changed `stroke_outline.rs`, and the `status` and `mesh` of every case are equal (145 of 145). Every other dump field is equal too, except `extra` of the 12 round caps, which is the change itself. `extra` is read only by `stroke_dump_tests.rs`; no export path reads it.

- `pnpm test:geometry`: PASS. Fixture generators, `cargo fmt --check` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean. Native 446 passed and 55 ignored. The release WASM and the independent rebuild are identical (`480cb633…2b50`). Vitest: 51 files passed and 4 skipped; 892 tests passed and 6 skipped.

### B2 ST-U6 renderer stroke

Unit ST-U6 of the frozen [B2 stroke contract](p3-b2-stroke-contract.md#121-units), full tier under the approved and frozen contract: the scene field `PathStroke` and its validation (section 5, with the ST-A0 amendments S3 and S4), and the renderer composition of section 9 (the stroke port, keys, memo, batch, records, draws, culling, notices and the opacity staging, with the ST-A0 S5 whole-path reading). Acceptance: ST-A10 and ST-A12, and the ST-A9 rows that name ST-U6. Every result is functional evidence, and precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4; no Q4 coverage threshold and no performance claim. ST-U6 closes no A01-A08 criterion and no C03-C05 item. `composition-unsupported` stays until ST-U7. The ST-A0 gate (amendments landed with an independent review READY WITH FOLLOW-UPS and its follow-ups applied, #176) is met before this unit merges. Independent diff and evidence review (section 12.3 item 3): round 1 on PR #183 **NOT READY** (one blocking item, three SHOULD-FIX, five minor); all applied on this branch (review record below); a re-review is pending.

One V3 row changes by contract: `v3a-path-scene.test.ts`'s "style fields other than exactly fill" now reads "exactly fill and stroke" (`{ fill: null }` is `invalid-value`, `{ fill: null, stroke: null }` is `applied`). `primitive-packet-review.test.ts` gains one type-only narrowing cast, as V3's interpretation 5 did. `tests/support/v3b-mesh-harness.ts` needed no change.

Interpretations (after the round 1 review; the Primary's decisions are marked):

- **`paint` of `port-version`.** Section 9.8 lists "`port-version` of both ports" under `null`: a `port-version` state of either drawable paint's port has `paint: null`. Review S1: it is a node-level state keyed like `composition-unsupported`, so a node notifies once per transition even when both ports change version.
- **Composition precedence.** The node-level states are checked after culling and the singular check: `composition-unsupported` first, then `port-version`, then the per-paint states (tolerance, fringe range, packing). A path in a node-level state reports no paint state. They apply only to a path with at least one drawable paint. At opacity 0 both paints draw (both draws output zero, section 9.6).
- **Missing paint.** A stroked path draws only when every drawable paint is served; while one paint waits on the isolation stack, the path is not drawn (no partial path). An `empty` paint result draws nothing for that paint and is not a failure; `fringe-work-cap` still draws region-only.
- **Other paint during a failure (Primary decision, review B1, reversing the first reading).** Every drawable paint's state and memo are evaluated first; a path with a paint known to fail in this prepare (a state failure or a memo hit) sends no request for its other paint. A failure that is first discovered by this prepare's own batches (before they run, it is not yet known) can still let the other paint's request in the same prepare go out; that costs at most one call per failing key, and the next prepare sends nothing. The other paint's cached mesh stays (section 9.6). So an undrawable path never calls a port every frame, also under budget pressure (V3 sections d and h).
- **Stroke slot and entries.** A stroke slot exists for each path node whose stroke has `width > 0` (allocated after its fill slot by V3's first-free allocator, released when the stroke becomes `null` or width 0). Stroke cache and memo entries become unreachable when the stroke becomes `null` or width 0, or when any key field changes. Fill entries keep V3's rule (`fill: null` keeps them).
- **Memo key of `invalid-stroke` (Primary decision, review M4, reversing the first reading).** Like `invalid-path`, `invalid-stroke` is decided before the tolerance stage (section 7.8), so its memo key and notice carry a null exponent.
- **Eviction ties.** Budget eviction ties compare the paint tag first (`'fill'` before `'stroke'`), then V3's node id and `e`, so fill-only scenes evict as before.
- **Statistics.** `portCalls` stays the fill port's count; `strokePortCalls` is added only when a stroke port is given, so fill-only statistics are unchanged.
- **Files outside the expected list.** `primitive-scene-source.ts` (stroke placements), `scene-numeric.ts` (the stroke box), `renderer-webgpu/src/webgpu-backend.ts` (one `paint: null`), the V3 smoke fixture page (the GPU stroke mode), `stroke_outline_tests.rs` (review S2) and `docs/validation.md` (review M3) are touched because the listed behavior or the review needs them.
- **Reachability of the stroke tolerance state.** `e < -1020` needs `sigmaMax` above about `2^1017`, which f32 lanes cannot carry, so through the scene source the fill paint of such a path also fails (`packing-range`). The unit test checks that each paint reports its own state there.

### B2 ST-U6 pending-paint churn (review follow-up)

Light tier, closes ST-U6 re-review follow-up F1. No contract text changes.

- **Finding.** While one paint of a stroked path is pending (waiting on its isolation stack, or held back by `callsPerPrepare`), the other paint is still requested and published. The path cannot draw, so that mesh keeps `lastServed` 0 and budget eviction removes it first; it is rebuilt the next frame. The reviewer's probe (8 stroked nodes, a fill batch failing with `internal-error` above one request, `callsPerPrepare: 1`, `byteBudget: 1`) called the stroke port on 15 consecutive prepares.
- **Choice: option (b).** In `path-mesh-pipeline.ts` step 8, a path that cannot draw because a sibling mesh is missing adds its already-published entries to that frame's working set, without a `lastServed` stamp. Eviction cannot drop them before the path draws. Reason against option (a), holding the other paint's request: it would change V3 section d/h call-bound and isolation order (a request would be withheld based on a sibling's state, so the fill and stroke batches would no longer be independent per port), and B2 sections 9.4-9.6 define one batch per port from the missing set. (b) leaves every request, batch, isolation step and memo decision unchanged. This widens V3 section d's working set, so it is recorded as a dated amendment in the [V3 contract](p3-v3-renderer-mesh-pipeline-contract.md#d-mesh-cache-failure-memo-and-their-bounds) (2026-10-21): the working set also holds the cached entries of a path that passed the drawability, node-state, paint-state and memo checks of this frame and was not drawn only because a sibling paint's entry is missing; they are not stamped as drawn, and a protected entry still counts toward the over-budget statistics. At most one entry per node and paint (the entry served for the current exponent) is protected, so the protected set never exceeds the working set the same frame would have if every path drew. Fill-only paths have no sibling, so their behaviour is byte-identical.
- **Tests.** Two unit tests in `tests/unit/st-u6-stroke-pipeline.test.ts`: the probe (the stroke port is called exactly once: the single batch of 8 is ok and its meshes stay protected while the fills resolve one per prepare), and the reverse case (stroke pending, fill ok: the fill port is called exactly once). Both fail without the fix (seeded mutation: revert the working-set addition).
- **Independent review:** round 1 NOT READY on documentation only; round 2 READY (the V3 amendment matches the code exactly, and the anchors resolve). B1: the change widens V3 section d's working set, so it needs a V3 dated amendment, not only a plan reading; now added under V3 section d. S1: the final assertions are now exact, 16 draws and 0 evictions. S2: the protected paths are defined (drawability, node-state, paint-state and memo checks passed). S3: the no-stamp half stays untested, as stated in the code comment; accepted. The reviewer confirmed the protected set is bounded by the all-drawn working set, and that fill-only behaviour is unchanged.

Findings to carry forward (observations, not decisions; no behavior was changed):

- **`join-round` is `unsupported-geometry` in every view that shows it.** The simplest round-join case, a 90 degree chevron of two straight arms (width 12, butt caps), is not drawn. `thin-one-px` (round joins and round caps on a zigzag, width 1) and `cap-round` (round caps, miter joins) do draw. The cause is not investigated here; the contract maps `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)` to this status, which a round-join polygon tangent to the sweep edges could provoke. The review of PR #188 reproduced it natively: `arrangement:Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`, because the round-join disk has a vertex 1 ulp (8.9e-16) away from the line sweep's offset point, the same point in two binary64 representations (for example (4.242640687119285, -21.757359312880716) against (4.242640687119286, -21.757359312880716)), the same kind of defect as the ST-U4 cusp duplicate triple. It depends on an arm direction aligning with a disk direction that is not exactly representable: half angles 45 and 11.25 fail, 44.9, 45.1, 40, 33.75, 30, 22.5, 15 and 50 are `ok`, and the axis-aligned 90 degree chevron is `ok`. **Tracked as follow-up ST-U3c** (round-join disk endpoints shared with the sweep triples), with the review's reproduction as its fixture; the spec gates the current literal as a tripwire and its fix re-baselines every `join-round` expectation.
- **Cubic strokes at pixel scale.** `wide-cubic` (one cubic, width 24), `fill-stroke-circle` (four cubics, width 8) and `cusp-f16-large` are `work-limit` at zoom 1. A cusp that is `ok` at `e = -5` (`cusp-f16p`) is `work-limit` at DPR 3 and at zoom 64. This is the ST-U9 evidence the contract names. `fill-stroke-circle` is `fringe-work-cap` (it draws) at zoom 0.25.
- **Extreme and switch-point miters, thin strokes and caps render in every view that shows them,** including the quarter-pixel hairline and the 6 degree miter spike at zoom 64 (1 draw, 34 triangles for the spike).
