### B2 ST-U3c round-join shared vertex (review follow-up)

Light-tier plan entry, 2026-10-21, branch `claude/p3-b2-st-u3c-round-join-shared-vertex`. It is the ST-U3c follow-up that the ST-U8 review flagged: the playground path `join-round` was `unsupported-geometry`. It restores the frozen contract's §4.2 sharing, as the [ST-U4 cusp shared triple](#b2-st-u4-cusp-shared-triple-review-follow-up) follow-up did for cusp knots, and adds no behaviour. Acceptance check:

- the review's reproductions are `ok` with literal expectations fixed before their first run;
- a native test shows each shared offset point bit-identical in the disk piece;
- on the B2 dump corpus and the 100-case cusp corpus, no status changes except `unsupported-geometry` → `ok`, and `ok` meshes change only where coincident points are now shared;
- the stroke identity moves to revision 3 under contract §7.1;
- the fill and P2 identities are unchanged;
- `pnpm test:geometry`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the headed ST-U6 and V4 GPU specs pass.

Precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, as for every stroke result.

**Finding.** A round piece's disk polygon (§4.4) takes `c + r·dir_j` from the bisection table, with two roundings per coordinate. An adjacent sweep's offset point is `C ± fl(r·N)`, with `N = n(d)` from the segment direction (§4.2). When the arm's normal is a table direction, the two are one point in exact arithmetic but are computed by different operations, and they can differ by one ulp. For `join-round`, `chevron(45, 52, −26)`, `w = 12`, butt, round join, `e = −3`, the join disk's 45° vertex is `(4.242640687119285, −21.757359312880716)` and the incoming sweep's inner `E+` is `(4.242640687119286, −21.757359312880716)`: the table's `fl(6·0.7071067811865475)` against `fl(6·n.y)` with `n = n(d)` of the arm. The outer points were already in the piece (`X = {B, O_in, O_out}`), so there the 1-ulp disk vertex happened to fall strictly inside the hull and was dropped. The inner points are not in `X`, so the disk piece kept its own vertex next to the sweep's. The arrangement's `embed_scalar` then found no binary64 rank between the two events and failed with `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`. The trigger is any arm normal on a table direction of the disk's level: half-angles 45 and 11.25 at `n = 32` in the review, and in the probe corpus below also 33.75, 56.25, 67.5, 78.75 and 84.375 at some widths, offsets and tolerances.

**Fix** (`stroke_outline.rs`). The new `share_disk_vertices(center, r, targets)` runs right after `push_disk`. With `δ = 8u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`, each target point replaces its nearest disk vertex (max norm, first index on a tie) when that vertex is within `δ` and no earlier target took it. The snap is skipped when `δ` is not a normal number, so `δ` is the exact product `fl(‖c‖∞ + r)·2^-50`. The targets are the `E−` and `E+` of every triple at the disk's center:

- a turn or reversal round join: the incoming end triple and the outgoing start triple, four points;
- a round cap: its end triple;
- a cusp or turning-point disk: the knot triple (the signature of `emit_cusp_disk` now takes the triple; its center is the same point as before, bit for bit);
- the ST-D6 knot join (seeded defect only): the knot triple;
- a round point cap (ST-P7) has no triple and is unchanged.

The count `n`, the count rule, every guard and the hull are unchanged. The snapped point then also appears in the adjacent sweep, so the two pieces share it bit for bit.

**Proof that ST-G2 still holds.** Let `v_j = fl(c + r·dir_j)` be a disk vertex and `s` the triple point that replaces it.

1. ST-U3's ST-G2 proof (above) gives `|v_j − (c + r·dir_j*)| ≤ (3.5k + 6)u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`, with `dir_j*` the exact direction. `g_arc = (8k + 16)u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`, so the margin is `(4.5k + 10)u·(‖c‖∞ + r) ≥ 14.5u·(‖c‖∞ + r)` for `k ≥ 1`.
2. The snap requires the computed `max(|fl(v_j.x − s.x)|, |fl(v_j.y − s.y)|) ≤ δ`. Each subtraction is exact or has relative error at most `u`, and `δ ≤ 8u·(‖c‖∞ + r)·(1 + u)`. So `‖v_j − s‖∞ ≤ 8u·(‖c‖∞ + r)·(1 + 3u)`, and `|v_j − s| ≤ 11.32u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`.
3. So `|s − (c + r·dir_j*)| ≤ (3.5k + 17.33)u·(‖c‖∞ + r) < g_arc`, because `4.5k ≥ 4.5 > 1.33`. The snapped polygon is still inscribed up to `g_arc`, and §4.4's argument (Hausdorff distance at most sagitta plus `g_arc`, kept within `2^(e−2)` by the count rule) applies unchanged. No guard formula or constant changes.
4. ST-O1's disk checks hold by the same bound: every vertex is within `r + 2^(e−2)` of the center, and every chord is at least `r − 2^(e−2)` from it. `n` is unchanged, so the count check holds. Each vertex is replaced at most once. Whether a pure disk piece (cusp disk) keeps `n` hull vertices depends on each vertex's distance from the chord through its neighbours (about `2π²r/n²`), not on the vertex spacing, and the count rule leaves only about `8u·reach` of that margin at the chosen level; the existing vertex error `(3.5k+6)u·reach` already exceeds it, so `n` hull vertices was never provable from the bounds, before or after this change. It holds as a measured result: ST-O1's pure-disk count check passes on every corpus case below. The snapped point `C ± fl(r·N)` is radially at least as accurate as the table vertex it replaces, so this is not a regression (corrected after the PR #190 review).
5. The rule is not complete for arbitrarily rounded coincidences: a pair farther apart than `δ` is not snapped. The PR #190 review found such pairs: near-45° and near-11.25° chevrons perturbed by about 1e-15 rad at center (100.5, 37.25) leave coincident pairs 1.35-1.65 δ apart (9-11 ulps), which still fail; 4 of 656 probe cases, against 22 on the base kernel. **Follow-up ST-U3d:** let `δ` grow with the disk level within the ST-G2 margin `(4.5k+10)u·reach`, about `16.6u·reach` at k = 3, which covers both. Measured, every snapped pair in the corpora below was at most 1 ulp apart, far below `δ`.

**Identity (contract §7.1).** The fix changes `ok` meshes (66 of the probe corpus below), so the failure-to-success exemption does not apply. As in #179, `stroke_v0_revision()` becomes 3 and `operationId` becomes `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r3'`, in the kernel, the adapter and its type, `stroke-adapter.test.ts` (which also rejects revision 2 now) and the SC21 option of `stroke-mesh-port.test.ts`. The fill operation and revision are untouched. The contract and `docs/graphics-engine-architecture.md` carry a dated note.

**Corpus regression.** Each corpus was dumped with `emit_stroke_dump` before the fix (`origin/main` `4713307`) and after it, then compared field by field (status, failure, mesh, segments and every piece's metadata and points), and ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 ran on every complete case of the after dumps with the review's harness (ST-O3 on cases of at most 40 pieces; cases with defect flags skipped). The scripts and dumps are ad hoc, in the implementer's session scratch directory, and are not committed; the hashes are SHA-256 prefixes.

| Corpus                                                                      | Cases | Before                                           | After                          | Change                                                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----: | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| B2 line and cubic dump corpus (`lineCases`, `cubicCases`; `36cd8dcb…`)      |   145 | 104 ok, 20 empty, 2 numeric-range, 19 work-limit | same                           | bit-identical dumps (`ad6933bc…` both)                       |
| Review cusp corpus (`in.txt`, `044c23b6…`)                                  |   100 | 74 ok, 26 work-limit                             | same                           | bit-identical dumps (`3714546f…` both)                       |
| Review reproductions (`input.txt`, `input2.txt`, `input3.txt`; `f4476fd8…`) |    28 | 19 ok, 9 unsupported-geometry                    | 28 ok                          | the 9 failures → `ok`; the 19 `ok` dumps are bit-identical   |
| Table-direction probe (`gen-probe.mjs`, `probe.txt`, `2d6ded2c…`)           |   495 | 451 ok, 44 unsupported-geometry                  | 493 ok, 2 unsupported-geometry | 42 failures → `ok`; 66 `ok` meshes change; 387 bit-identical |

- **What changed.** In every changed case, the segments, the piece count and all piece metadata are equal. Only round-join, round-cap and cusp-disk point lists differ. Every point added to a piece is bit-equal to a triple's `E−` or `E+`, and every removed point is a disk vertex within 1 ulp of it (271 snapped hull points in the probe corpus, 20 in the reproductions). No other point differs. The meshes then differ downstream, as in #179.
- **Oracles.** ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 pass on all 768 after cases: 723 ST-O1 runs, 689 ST-O3 runs, no finding and no exception.
- **Remaining failures.** The probe's two `unsupported-geometry` cases, `chev-1.40625` with `w = 12`, round caps, `e = −3` and `e = 0`, centered at `(−3.125, 1000)`, fail the same way before and after. They are not this defect: no two distinct piece vertices are within `1e−6`, and the arm normals at 1.40625° are not directions of the disks (`n = 32` at `e = −3`, 16 at `e = 0`). They are near-antiparallel arms (2.55 apart at the ends, `w = 12`) under the existing arrangement. Recorded as an open observation for ST-OB7, not fixed here.

**Fixtures** (native, `stroke_outline_tests.rs`). The status, area and membership literals were fixed before the first run. The area band was not right on that run and was revised, as recorded below.

- **`ST-U3c/join-round`.** The playground path, `M(−36.76955262170047, 10.769552621700477) L(0, −26) L(36.76955262170047, 10.769552621700477)`, `w = 12`, butt, round, `L = 4`, `e = −3`. It is `ok`. `(0, −26)`, `(0, −31.5)` and `(0, −18)` are inside; `(0, −32.5)` and `(0, −17)` are outside. The ideal union area is `1240.0930063703227`.
- **`ST-U3c/chevron-45-exact`.** The dyadic chevron `M(−36, 10) L(0, −26) L(36, 10)`, same request and probes, ideal area `1213.9735242606764`.
- **`ST-U3c/half-angle-11.25`.** `chevron(11.25, 52, −26)`, same request. `(0, −26)`, `(0, −31.5)` and `(0, 0)` are inside; `(0, −32.5)` and `(0, 6)` are outside. Ideal area `1116.178539431534`.
- **`ST-U3c/join-round-round-caps`.** `join-round` with round caps. The probes above, plus `(−40.6586277, 14.6586277)` (5.5 beyond the start) inside and `(−41.3657345, 15.3657345)` (6.5) outside. Ideal area `1352.4650318516128`.
- **`ST-U3c/join-round-e-5`, `-e-1`, `-e3` and `-w4`** (`w = 4`): `ok`, with `(0, −26)` and `(0, −25)` inside.
- **Area band.** The ideal areas are the union of the two sweep rectangles and the inscribed 32-gons, by inclusion-exclusion over convex intersections (scratch `areas.mjs`). The first run used a `1e−9` band and failed on `join-round` with `1240.5621216146196`. The mesh is resolved under the arrangement's `τ = 2^(e−2)` rounding, so only an exactly representable region meets `1e−9`. The same kind of offset is present before the fix on the reproductions that were already `ok` (0.07 for the miter and bevel chevrons, up to 0.47 for `chev-h44.9`: union `1240.0300`, mesh `1240.4983`). The band is now `256·τ` (8 at `e = −3`), since each path's boundary is shorter than 256. Observed: 0.47, 0.37, 0.006 and 0.12. The membership probes carry the precise check.
- **`st_u3c_disk_vertices_share_the_sweep_offset_points`.** On all eight cases: each `E−` and `E+` of a triple adjacent to a round join or cap that lies within `1e−9` of a vertex of that piece is one of its vertices, compared by bits. Each join shares 4 points and each round cap 2. No two distinct piece vertices of the path are within `1e−9`.

### B2 ST-U7b joint overlay builder

Unit ST-U7b of the frozen [ST-U7 addendum](p3-b2-st-u7-joint-coverage-addendum.md#6-units), full tier under that addendum: the CPU overlay builder of its section 4.2 and the composite model of its section 3.2, with their independent checks. CPU only: no packet, shader, backend or GPU change, and nothing calls the builder yet (ST-U7c wires it). No existing behavior, contract, ABI, dependency or threshold changes, and no existing test file is edited. Acceptance: ST-A13, ST-A14 and ST-A12, and ST-A18's ST-U7b commands. Every result is functional evidence; precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, and no Q4 coverage threshold or performance claim is made.

Files:

- `packages/renderer-core/src/path-joint-overlay.ts` (new): `buildJointOverlay` and its `@internal` detailed form, and the Float64 composite model `jointFillComposite`. Not exported from the package. The builder re-runs the fill's `buildPathFringeDetailed` once per build for the pre-drop frame partition (S-c), computes the insertion points once per domain edge and inserts them into both triangles, splits each crossed triangle into the faces of its exact arrangement with the clipped stroke `boundaryEdges`, decides each face's stroke role by the exact winding number at an exact interior point of the unrounded face (the centroid of its first unrounded ear), rounds each Steiner point once to Float64 (an axis-aligned frame edge keeps its exact coordinate, packed as `k`), triangulates each face with the fringe builder's bridging and ear-clipping triangulator on the rounded positions, runs O02's exact partition check over the domain boundary multiset (subdivided), drops the pieces of empty-list exterior parents after the check, and emits the section 4.5 layouts: K4 offsets (the fill variant's vertices, then the Steiner points), 32-byte joint triangle records (bit-31 indices for stroke boundary vertices, preferred at exact ties) and the fill and stroke lists. Failures (`unsupported` with a reason): a failed partition check or triangulation, a Steiner point on a sloped outer edge (`sloped-outer-edge`, section 4.2 step 6 and ST7-Q8 a) and the ST7-Q5 (a) caps (`work-cap:tests`, `work-cap:steiner`; 1,048,576 tests and 4,096 Steiner points, current-cap estimates).
- `packages/renderer-core/src/path-fringe.ts`: its exact helpers, grid and triangulator are exported for internal use (`@internal`); `buildPathFringeDetailed` is promoted from "@internal Tests only" to an internal production use with an optional work counter; the frame partition check becomes a call of a general `checkPartitionOver` (domain boundary in place of the frame), whose interior-vertex test is prefiltered by exact coordinate ranges and reports the same vertex as before. The fringe builder's results are unchanged (V3c tests and the new counter test).
- `tests/unit/path-joint-overlay.test.ts` (new): ST-A14 on ST7-F1 to ST7-F10, ST7-F13 and ST7-F15 at several fringe buckets, including region-only fills and strokes, plus the positive control `U7b-axis-outer` (42 builds), and ST7-F14; an independent exact oracle in the test file (it imports nothing from the overlay builder; stroke-list entries are decoded through the stroke fringe variant's published feature records, by kind and lanes); fixture-specific checks; seeded defects ST7-D3 to ST7-D6 and ST7-D11; the builder-level work-cap checks, including 246 small rings in one large triangle, which stops at `work-cap:tests`.
- `tests/unit/path-joint-composite.test.ts` (new): ST-A13 against an independent BigInt rational model; seeded defects ST7-D1, ST7-D2 and ST7-D10 (the TypeScript model).

Interpretations fixed by this unit, open to its review:

- **Work counting (ST7-Q5 a).** One "exact test" is one exact predicate of a segment or point against an edge or triangle after the bounding-box prefilter: the domain-edge and stroke-edge intersection tests, the stroke-edge clips, the winding tests, the stroke-list tests, the partition check's interior-vertex tests, the triangulator's ear and bridge tests, the hole-assignment point-in-loop tests (one per loop edge visited), every exact angle comparison when the arrangement's half-edges are sorted, every insertion-point order comparison, every exact Float64 rounding, and every such test of the fill fringe re-run. The caps are checked during the build, which stops at the first excess. Work left uncounted is linear in work already counted: vertex registration and key lookups, the per-triangle lcm and cycle tracing (linear in the arrangement's edges, each produced by a counted test), cycle areas, and the partition check's orientation and edge-use passes (linear in the triangles, each produced by counted ear tests).
- **ST7-Q8 (a) class.** The builder has no transform, so it does not evaluate `r_phys·cos(θ/2) ≤ δ_F + δ_S`; the class is refused through its geometric consequence, a Steiner point on a sloped outer edge (section 4.2 step 6). ST7-F14 is that case. An axis-aligned outer edge of a region-only fill keeps its Float64 coordinate exactly and is not refused; the positive control `U7b-axis-outer` (a region-only rectangle under an inverted-U stroke with a leg from the non-dyadic (1.3, -1)) puts four Steiner points on such an edge and builds. This **narrows the documented failure class** of section 4.2 step 6, section 4.7 and ST7-Q8 (a): of the open-subpath and near-touching cases, only those whose crossed outer edge is sloped fail. ST-U7c+d records the narrowing in the contract note.
- **ST7-F9 and ST7-F10.** The builder takes no transform and no colour, so a reflected or translucent node has ST7-F1's overlay. ST7-F9 also builds ST7-F1 mirrored in local coordinates; ST7-F10's colours are exercised by ST-A13.
- **Seeded defects.** Each is an `@internal` test-only switch. With the builder's own partition check disabled (also test-only), each changed fixture build is caught by its named oracle check; with it enabled, ST7-D3, ST7-D6 and ST7-D11 are refused by the builder itself. ST7-D6 gives the second triangle of an edge its own copy of each Steiner point; ST7-D11 inserts a point only into the triangles a stroke edge enters.
- **Drop rule.** No fixture has an empty-list exterior parent (the fringe builder dropped no exterior triangle on any fixture), so the drop is implemented and oracle-checked but not exercised.

Seeded-defect results (fixture builds the defect changes, every one caught by the named check): ST7-D3 38 of 38 (partition), ST7-D4 38 of 38 (membership), ST7-D5 22 of 22 (stroke lists), ST7-D6 24 of 24 (partition: edge matching), ST7-D11 5 of 5 (partition: edge multiset), over the 38 builds of the fixtures other than ST7-F7 and ST7-F8 (left out to bound the run time); ST7-D1, ST7-D2 and ST7-D10 each fail the 2^-40 check of ST-A13.

Overlay costs, **diagnostic only** (not performance evidence; one machine, `vitest` in Node 24.15.0, mean of 5 builds after one warm-up): the largest fixtures are ST7-F7 (62 fill triangles, 144 stroke edges, region-only stroke): 726 overlay triangles, 178 Steiner points, 38,530 exact tests, about 56 ms; and ST7-F8 (a 136-gon region-only fill, 272 stroke edges): 850 triangles, 222 Steiner points, 89,755 tests with a fringed stroke (about 164 ms) and 66,113 region-only (about 63 ms). Every other fixture build stays below 4,000 tests, 16 Steiner points and 7 ms. Test counts are after the review follow-up S3 (more work counted); the times were measured before it. ST-U7d re-baselines the caps.

### B2 ST-U7c+d joint draw

Units ST-U7c and ST-U7d of the frozen [ST-U7 addendum](p3-b2-st-u7-joint-coverage-addendum.md#6-units), one PR as its section 6 requires, full tier under the addendum: the behavior change (packet, shader, pipeline, backend and renderer wiring of sections 4.5 and 4.6, the removal of `composition-unsupported`, the section 5.1 to 5.3 dated notes, the allow-listed unit test edits) and its headed evidence (the joint fixture page, the ST-A11, ST-A16 and ST-A17 GPU spec, the allow-listed GPU edits, the cap re-baseline). Acceptance: ST-A11, ST-A15, ST-A16, ST-A17, ST-A18 and ST-A12, with the ST7-F fixtures and ST7-D seeded defects assigned to ST-U7c and ST-U7d. No requirement, threshold, ABI, kernel or dependency changes. Every result is functional evidence; precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, and no Q4 coverage threshold or performance claim is made.

Files:

- `packages/renderer-core/src/path-mesh-pipeline.ts`: the joint condition (both paints drawn, neither failing nor empty, `0 < o < 1`); the joint fill draw at the fill draw's place, before the unchanged stroke draw; overlay variants built on first need with `buildJointOverlay` (ST7-Q3 a) and cached on the fill entry, keyed by the served fill fringe variant (or region-only), the stroke entry and its served fringe variant, at most two per node with the least recently served first, their bytes in the budget, unreachable and swept when either parent variant leaves, evicted with a parent; `overlay-unsupported` (renderer scope, `paint: null`, one notice per mark, the path hidden and both meshes kept, ST7-Q4 a); `composition-unsupported` removed from the code and the `PathMeshFailureCode` union; the overlay caps as `PathMeshLimits` fields at the ST7-Q5 (a) values; a test-only build counter (`getOverlayBuildCount`, not in `PathMeshStatistics`) and seeded defect ST7-D8.
- `packages/renderer-core/src/path-mesh-packet.ts`: the overlay's own ranges in the four geometry regions (its positions, the 32-byte joint triangle records with all eight words, its fill and stroke lists, no features); the fill record's words 12 and 13 at the overlay, word 14 at the fill variant's features, word 15 stroke slot + 1 from the current slot map; all 16 words recomputed for every draw of every packet after every variant is placed, written by the existing compare-and-write; repacks and reconstruction cover overlays; seeded defects ST7-D7 and ST7-D9.
- `packages/renderer-core/src/primitive-packet.ts` (not in the addendum's ST-U7c file list; it owns `MeshFillDraw`): `composite?: 'joint-fill'`, `layoutVersion` 0. `primitive-scene-source.ts`: the field on joint draws only, and a test-only overlay build count. `primitive-renderer-service.ts` (not in the file list): the same test-only count, `getOverlayBuildCountForTesting`, for the headed page's ST-A17 check. `packages/contracts/src/diagnostics.ts`: comment only.
- `packages/renderer-webgpu/src/mesh-fill-shader.ts`: in the existing module, `meshJointVertexMain` (it `select`s the record index, the stroke record named by word 15 − 1 for a bit-31 index, then calls the shared helper with the masked index) and `meshJointFragmentMain`; the shared `meshCornerIndex` and `meshPositionNdc` helpers, into which `meshVertexMain`'s body moved; `@invariant` on `MeshVertexOutput.position`, which the joint entry point also returns; the joint coverage helper `meshJointCoverage` over the shared per-feature `meshListCoverage`, which `meshCoverage` now calls with its signature and call unchanged; `c_S` from the fill record's word 13 with the stroke record's anchor, lanes and `featureBase`. `mesh-pipeline.ts`: the `'p3-mesh-joint-v0'` key and the `vector-studio/p3-mesh-joint-${n}x` descriptor. `native-primitive-scene.ts`: the joint pipeline created beside the fill pipeline at initialization and recovery (either failing makes the mesh path unavailable for the generation), the switch at a joint draw and back, the `composite` value check, and the word 15 − 1 check against written instances below capacity (word 15 of each written record is tracked from the writes). `webgpu-backend.ts` needed no change.

No other existing test file is edited.

Interpretations fixed by this unit, open to its review:

- **The joint output struct.** `meshJointVertexMain` returns `MeshVertexOutput`, so the joint output's position is the same `@invariant` member; no second output struct is declared. WGSL's `select` takes no struct, so the joint entry point selects the record **index** (fill instance or word 15 − 1) and loads that record, then calls the shared helper: the stroke record and the masked index give the stroke draw's exact expression.
- **Fill variant residency.** The fill slot keeps its fill variant placed (word 14 and the fill features need it) and places the overlay in addition. After the joint condition ends, the overlay ranges stay while the overlay is cached (the addendum's "resident overlay ranges, excluded from the comparison").
- **Mark persistence.** A failed overlay is a cached mark like a failed fringe variant: re-entering the joint condition with the same parent variants hides the path again without a new notice and without a rebuild; a new parent variant pair builds again. The notice's `toleranceExponent` is null, since the mark belongs to two entries with their own exponents.
- **The headed page.** ST-A16's scene keeps every fixture node of every view in each snapshot and hides the ones of other frames, so meshes and overlays stay cached and ST-A17 can require zero port calls and zero overlay builds after the loss, over the ST-A11 scene and every ST-A16 view and frame. The test-only build count is read through the service.
- **ST7-D8 on the GPU.** With an opaque stroke the joint draw outputs 0 in the overlap, so ST-A11's overlap value cannot see the order swap; ST-A16 catches it on ST7-F10 (translucent paints) and in AA bands, and ST-A15 catches it in unit form.

Acceptance and evidence (2026-10-21, Windows 11, Node 24.15.0, NVIDIA Turing adapter):

- **ST-A11** (`pnpm test:gpu`, `tests/gpu/p3-st-u7-joint.spec.ts`; Chrome 155.0.8059.85 and Edge 155.0.4283.62, 1x and 4x, DPR 1 and 2; identical in all eight runs): 1,092,176 gated interior pixel centers per run (3 px from every boundary), maximum deviation 0.5/255 against the single-opacity composite (gate 2/255); every node has gated overlap, fill-only and stroke-only pixels. The overlap's smallest distance to the double-opacity value is 63.75/255 for `joint-half` (o = 0.5), 47.81/255 for the opaque-stroke `joint-quarter` (o = 0.25) and 64.25/255 over the primitive (gate > 20/255). The half-opacity path over the opaque green primitive reads `(0, 128, 127, 255)` at its overlap center against the expected `(0, 127.5, 127.5, 255)`.
- **ST-A16** (same spec and runs): ST7-F1, ST7-F6, ST7-F7, ST7-F8, ST7-F9 and ST7-F10 at o = 0.5 and 0.25 against o × their o = 1 render, under the identity, a 30° rotation, a reflection and a 3 × 0.5 scale; every view fits one frame; 16 reports and 9,123,400 gated pixels per run, maximum RGB deviation 1.5/255 (gate 2/255), no gated pixel over the gate. **Excluded band** (pixel centers within 1 physical px of a region-only boundary edge: ST7-F7's stroke and ST7-F8's fill and stroke): 92,600 pixels per run over the 16 reports, at most 2.33% of the crop (the 3 × 0.5 view at DPR 1) and at most 0.539 of its `3·L + 8·E` bound; band pixels beyond 2/255: 6 per run at 1x and 40 at 4x, reported, not gated. The joint draw count equals the frame's node count at o < 1 and is 0 at o = 1.
- **ST-A17** (GPU and unit): after an injected device loss at DPR 2, the ST-A11 scene and every ST-A16 view and frame at o = 1, 0.5 and 0.25 are rendered again: 11,846,386 compared pixels (ST-A11's probed pixels and every non-band pixel of the ST-A16 frames) equal the pre-loss capture exactly in all four runs; port calls 10 → 10 on both ports and overlay builds 54 → 54. ### B2 ST-U3d/ST-U3e stroke sharing (review follow-up)

Light-tier plan entry, 2026-10-21, branch `claude/p3-b2-st-u3de-sharing`. It closes the two follow-ups that the PR #190 review recorded in the [ST-U3c entry](#b2-st-u3c-round-join-shared-vertex-review-follow-up): ST-U3d (the snap radius `δ` by disk level) and ST-U3e (one triple at a line reversal). ST-U3d widens ST-U3c's snap radius. ST-U3e adds a **new sharing rule** that extends §4.2 at exact reversals; §4.2 as frozen shares a triple only at a forward continuation and was not violated (corrected after the PR #195 review). Neither changes a guard, a stroke semantic row or the frozen piece shapes. Acceptance check:

- the review's near-direction reproductions and exact line reversals are `ok`, with literal expectations fixed before their first run;
- native tests show the snap factor inside ST-G2's margin at every level and each shared point bit-identical;
- on the B2 dump corpus, the review cusp corpus, the ST-U3c probe and reproduction corpora and both PR #190 review probes, no status changes except `unsupported-geometry` → `ok`, and `ok` meshes change only in shared points;
- the stroke identity moves to revision 4 under contract §7.1;
- the fill and P2 identities are unchanged;
- `pnpm test:geometry`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the headed ST-U8 and ST-U6 GPU specs pass.

Precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4, as for every stroke result.

**ST-U3d finding.** ST-U3c snaps a disk vertex to an adjacent triple's `E±` within `δ = 8u·(‖c‖∞ + r)`. The PR #190 review's near-45° and near-11.25° chevrons, with arm directions perturbed by about `1e−15` rad and the apex at (100.5, 37.25), leave pairs 1.35 to 1.65 `δ` apart (9 to 11 ulps). They are not snapped, and the arrangement fails with `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`: 4 of the review's 656 probe cases.

**ST-U3d fix** (`stroke_outline.rs`). `push_disk` returns the level `k` it used, and `share_disk_vertices` takes it. The radius is `δ_k = fl(F_k·2^-53·fl(‖c‖∞ + r))` with `F_k = share_factor(k) = 3.125k + 7`, that is 10.125, 13.25, 16.375, 19.5, 22.625 and 25.75 for `k = 1` to 6. The rest of the rule is unchanged: nearest vertex in the max norm, first index on a tie, at most one target per vertex, and no snap when `δ_k` is not a normal number.

**Proof that every guard still holds.** Let `R = ‖c‖∞ + r`, `v` a disk vertex of level `k` and `s` the target that replaces it.

1. `F_k` has at most eight significant bits, so `F_k·2^-53` is exact. `fl(‖c‖∞ + r) ≤ R(1 + u)`, and the product is rounded once while normal, so `δ_k ≤ F_k·u·R·(1 + u)²`.
2. The snap requires the computed `max(|fl(v.x − s.x)|, |fl(v.y − s.y)|) ≤ δ_k`, and each subtraction has relative error at most `u`. So `‖v − s‖∞ ≤ δ_k/(1 − u) < F_k·u·R·(1 + 4u)`, and `|v − s| < √2·F_k·(1 + 4u)·u·R`.
3. `√2·F_k = 4.4194k + 9.8995`, so `√2·F_k·(1 + 4u) < 4.5k + 10`, ST-U3's margin over the proved vertex bound, with at least `0.08k + 0.1` to spare. The native test `st_u3d_share_factor_stays_inside_the_arc_margin` checks this and the exactness at `k = 1` to 6.
4. So `|s − (c + r·dir_j*)| ≤ (3.5k + 6)u·R + |v − s| < (8k + 16)u·R = g_arc`. The snapped polygon is still inscribed up to `g_arc`, and §4.4's Hausdorff argument and the count rule apply unchanged. `k` and `n` are chosen before the snap by the unchanged rule. No guard formula or constant changes, and nothing is loosened. ST-O1's disk checks hold by the same bound, as in ST-U3c step 4.
5. **Distinct disk vertices.** The ideal spacing of adjacent vertices is `2r·sin(π/n)`, and each vertex is within `(3.5k + 6)u·R` of its ideal. Two vertices within `δ_k` (max norm) of one target are at most `2√2·F_k·(1 + 4u)·u·R` apart. So at most one vertex lies within `δ_k` of any target whenever `2r·sin(π/n) > (7k + 12 + 2√2·F_k·(1 + 4u))·u·R`. That is `r > 62.3u·R` at `k = 1`, `162.7u·R` at `k = 2`, `404.6u·R` at `k = 3`, `969.6u·R` at `k = 4`, `2,262u·R` at `k = 5` and `5,168u·R` at `k = 6`. For `k ≥ 2` the count rule gives a lower bound on `r`: level `k − 1` failed while `2^(e−2) > g_arc(k)`, so `r > 8u·R·4^(k+1)·(1 − 2^-40)/C_ARC ≈ 1.621·4^(k+1)·u·R`. That is 415, 1,660, 6,640 and 26,560 `u·R` for `k = 3` to 6, so the condition holds unconditionally for `k ≥ 3`. For `k = 1` and `k = 2` it holds when `r` is at least 63 and 163 `u·R`. Below that, the disk is narrower than about 330 ulps of its reach. A target may then be within `δ_k` of two vertices, and the rule snaps the nearer one deterministically. Step 4 does not use the spacing, so ST-G2 still holds. ST-U3c's `δ = 8u·R` had the same class below 54.4 and 124.7 `u·R`.
6. A snap can also identify two points that differ in exact arithmetic but lie within `δ_k`. The review's chevrons are such cases: the arm normals are about `1e−15` rad off a table direction. Step 4 bounds the moved vertex either way, so this is the same rounding-level identification as ST-U3c's.

**ST-U3e finding and re-diagnosis.** At an exact reversal, `classify_joins` left the outgoing start triple as computed from the outgoing segment's own normal. The exact tangents are antiparallel, but `n(d̃_out)` and `−n(d̃_in)` are separate roundings. When the length ratio is not a power of two they can differ by an ulp, so the point `B` carried two triples a few ulps apart, one per segment end. §4.2 as frozen allows this: it shares a triple only at a forward continuation (corrected after the PR #195 review; the first wording called it a violation of §4.2). The incoming sweep's `E+` and the outgoing sweep's `E−` were then distinct points a few ulps apart, and the arrangement could fail with `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`. It is the mechanism of the ST-U4 cusp defect, at a line reversal. An exact-reversal probe (below) fails on 324 of 4,800 cases on `main`, for example `M(0,0) L(5,2) L(−10,−4)`, round or bevel join, `w = 12`, `e = −3`.

The review's own examples are a different case. `M(0,0) L(3,1) L(−1,−1/3)` and `M(0,0) L(1,7) L(−1/3,−7/3)` with `λ = 4/3` are not exact reversals. The inputs are the binary64 values `−0.33333333333333326` and `−2.333333333333332`, so the exact cross product is nonzero (about `2.2e−16` for the first) and the join is a left turn of about `1.7e−17` rad. The two triples at `B` then belong to distinct ideal points, less than an ulp apart. §4.2 does not share them, and ST-U3e does not change them. These cases, `rev-4/3-3-1` at `e = −3` and 0 and `rev-4/3-1-7` at `e = −3`, −5 and 0, stay `unsupported-geometry` before and after. The test `st_u3e_review_near_reversals_are_turns` fixes their join class and status. They are recorded with `chev-1.40625` as the known defect ST-U3f below. They are not fixed here; a fix would need a near-coincidence rule that §4.2 does not have.

**ST-U3e fix** (`stroke_outline.rs`, `classify_joins`). At an exact reversal, the outgoing start triple is the incoming end triple with `E−` and `E+` swapped: `{minus: end.plus, center: end.center, plus: end.minus}`. This is what ST-U4's cusp fix and the collinear-part branch do, and the contract's §4.2 now states it as a rule (dated note, 2026-10-22). It applies to every segment pair, line or cubic, open or at a closed seam, and is not gated by ST-D3, which seeds only the forward-continuation case. The reversal join pieces are unchanged: round emits ST-P5 with `X = {B, E−_in, E+_in}`, which are now the outgoing start triple's points too; bevel and miter emit nothing.

**Proof that the error accounting holds.** The join is classified as a reversal from the exact tangents, so `d_out = −λ·d_in` with exact `λ > 0`, and the exact unit normals satisfy `N*_out = −N*_in`. ST-G1's per-point bound for a triple point, `‖E − E*‖ ≤ 7.5u·(M + r)`, depends only on `r`, the exact center `B` and `M`, which are the same for both segments. It does not depend on which segment's `d̃` was normalized. The swapped point `E−_out := E+_in = B + fl(r·N_in)` has the outgoing ideal `B − r·N*_out = B + r·N*_in`, so its error is `E+_in`'s own, within `7.5u·(M + r) < g_off`, and likewise for `E+_out`. Every bound built on ST-G1 holds unchanged: the outgoing sweep (ST-P1), the join's `X` points (ST-P5), the outgoing sweep's own end triple (not changed), and ST-O1. For a cubic on either side, the triple at `B` is a source triple, and the outgoing leaf range takes it with `start_error = 0`, as before. Its normal is within the same `5u` of the outgoing exact one-sided normal, so `g_ctrl` is unchanged. No guard changes.

**Identity (contract §7.1).** The fixes change `ok` meshes (455 in the corpora below), so the failure-to-success exemption does not apply. As in #179 and #190, `stroke_v0_revision()` becomes 4 and `operationId` becomes `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r4'`. The change is made in the kernel, the adapter and its type, `stroke-adapter.test.ts` (which now also rejects revision 3), the SC21 option of `stroke-mesh-port.test.ts`, the ST-U8 spec's stroke port gate and the playground comment. The fill operation and revision are untouched. The contract and `docs/graphics-engine-architecture.md` carry a dated note.

**Corpus regression.** Each corpus was dumped with `emit_stroke_dump` before the fix (`origin/main` `4e753fd`; `313d5ba` leaves the kernel unchanged) and after it, and dumped again from the final rebased tree, bit-identical to the first after dumps, then compared field by field. ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 then ran on every complete case of the after dumps with the review's harness (ST-O3 on cases of at most 40 pieces; cases with defect flags skipped). The scripts and dumps are ad hoc, in the implementer's session scratch directory, and are not committed. Hashes are SHA-256 prefixes, of the input and of the before and after dumps.

| ST-U3c table-direction probe (`2d6ded2c…`)                   |   495 | 493 ok, 2 unsupported-geometry                   | same                   | bit-identical (`361eabba…` both)                                                                                                 |
| PR #190 review probe 1 (`rev190-probe/in.txt`, `9630b5e7…`)  |   107 | 100 ok, 7 unsupported-geometry                   | same                   | 3 `ok` meshes change (`rev-7-3-1`, ST-U3e); `bde05085…` → `ceec242e…`                                                            |
| PR #190 review probe 2 (`rev190-probe/in2.txt`, `01a8edba…`) |   656 | 652 ok, 4 unsupported-geometry                   | 656 ok                 | 4 failures → `ok`, 168 `ok` meshes change (ST-U3d); `1234f9ec…` → `d72020c1…`                                                    |
| Exact-reversal probe (`gen-rev.mjs`, `f4914717…`)            | 4,800 | 4,476 ok, 324 unsupported-geometry               | 4,800 ok               | 324 failures → `ok`, 284 `ok` meshes change (ST-U3e); `d0e28c0d…` → `82d7b014…`                                                  |
| Mixed and seam reversal probe (review S1, `aa0b9d39…`)       |   720 | 580 ok, 102 work-limit, 38 unsupported-geometry  | 618 ok, 102 work-limit | 38 failures → `ok`, 20 `ok` meshes change, 8 `work-limit` cases change only in piece samples (ST-U3e); `549d3db1…` → `274c2f35…` |

The exact-reversal probe is new: 10 directions, 10 non-dyadic ratios `λ`, 3 base points, 4 width and tolerance pairs, butt and round caps, and round and bevel joins. Every coordinate is a multiple of 1/8, so each reversal is exact.

- **What changed.** A checker verified all 783 changed cases. In each, the request, the subpaths and every segment field are equal, except the outgoing start triple at an exact reversal. Each of those 611 triples equals the swapped incoming end triple bit for bit, and each replaced point moved by at most `1.83u·‖E‖∞`. The piece count and every piece's metadata are equal. Piece samples differ only by that triple. Every point added to a piece is a triple's `E±` or a triple center. Every removed point is one of three things:
  - a replaced reversal-triple point;
  - a disk vertex within `δ_k` of a triple point: 837 vertices, all in probe 2, all at level 3, between 8 and 16.28 `u·R`, so newly inside `δ_3 = 16.375u·R`;
  - a triple center.
- **Centre flips.** In 193 cases (108 `ok` → `ok`, 84 failure → `ok`, and `rev-7-3-1-4--5` in probe 1), the exact triple center `B` enters or leaves a line-sweep hull. The hull is boundary-inclusive, and `B`'s collinearity with the moved `E−` and `E+` changed by the ulp the shared triple moved. `B` is itself a shared triple point, so these are changes in shared points only. They are listed so the review can confirm that reading.
- **Oracles.** ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 have no finding and no exception on the after dumps: 1,531 cases of the first six corpora (1,486 ST-O1, 1,490 ST-O2 and 1,445 ST-O3 runs; 6 defect-flag cases skipped) and the 608 changed cases of the exact-reversal probe (all `ok`, 608 runs of each oracle). The 4,192 unchanged exact-reversal cases are bit-identical to `main` and were not oracle-checked, to bound the run time.
- **Remaining failures.** Probe 1's 7 failures are unchanged: the two `chev-1.40625` cases (ST-U3f below; both dumps bit-identical before and after) and the five `λ = 4/3` near-reversal turns above.

**Fixtures** (native, `stroke_outline_tests.rs`). The status, membership and sharing literals were fixed before the first run, and all passed on it. On `main` every one of the nine path cases of the first two items below is `unsupported-geometry`, as the before dumps show.

- **`ST-U3d/near-45-butt`, `-round`, `ST-U3d/near-11.25-butt`, `-round`.** The review's four failing probe-2 cases: `M(63.73044737830063, 74.01955262170158) L(100.5, 37.25) L(137.26955262169938, 74.01955262170158)`, which is half-angle 45° perturbed by `−30e−15` rad, and `M(90.35530325516271, 88.25083458096826) L(100.5, 37.25) L(110.64469674483729, 88.25083458096826)`, which is half-angle 11.25° perturbed by `−27e−15` rad. Both have `w = 12`, a round join and `e = −3`, with butt or round caps. They are `ok`. `B`, 5.5 below `B`, and a point just inside the inner corner are inside; 6.5 below `B` and just past the inner corner are outside. With round caps, 5.5 beyond the start along the outward tangent is inside and 6.5 is outside. Each triple point at a round join or cap is a vertex of that piece bit for bit (4 per join, 2 per cap), and no two distinct piece vertices are within `1e−9`.
- **`ST-U3e/rev-5-2-x3-round`, `-bevel`, `rev-1-3-x7-round`, `rev-13-11-x1.5-w2.5`, `rev-2-9-x5-offset`.** These are exact reversals `M(A) L(B) L(B − λ(B − A))`, with butt caps and `e = −3`. They are `ok`, the join is `Reversal`, and the outgoing start triple is the swapped incoming end triple bit for bit. Inside are `B`, the doubled sweep at `0.9r` either side, `0.1r` before the far end, and, for a round join, `0.9r` beyond `B`. Outside are `1.1r` beyond `B`, `1.1r` either side, `0.1r` past the far end, and, for a bevel, `0.1r` beyond `B`. No two distinct piece vertices are within `1e−9`.
- **`st_u3d_share_factor_stays_inside_the_arc_margin`** and **`st_u3e_review_near_reversals_are_turns`**, as above.

- **Review round 1 (S1) fixtures.** `ST-U3e/line-cubic-5-2-x3` (`M(0,0) L(5,2) C(−10,−4, −27,−5, −44,−6)`, `e = −3`), `ST-U3e/cubic-line-5-2-x3-e1` (`M(2,−5) C(−2,5, 0,0, 5,2) L(−10,−4)`, `e = 1`), `ST-U3e/seam-13-11-x1.5` (`M(0,0) L(13,11) L(2,24) L(78,66) Z`, `e = −3`) and `ST-U3e/seam-5-2-x3-e1` (`M(0,0) L(5,2) L(3,7) L(60,24) Z`, `e = 1`), all `w = 2`, butt caps, round join. Each is `unsupported-geometry` on `main` (cases `xlc-5,2-3-0,0--3`, `xcl-5,2-3-0,0-1`, `xseam-13,11-1.5-0,0--3` and `xseam-5,2-3-0,0-1` of the mixed probe) and `ok` on the branch: one exact reversal whose outgoing start triple is the swapped incoming end triple bit for bit, `B` inside, and no two distinct piece vertices within `1e−9`. At `e = −3`, `0.9r` beyond `B` along the incoming direction is inside and `1.1r` outside; at `e = 1` (`τ = 0.5`) only `3r` beyond `B` is probed outside. The literals were fixed before the first run, and all passed on it. ST-O1, ST-O2 and ST-O3 pass on all four, and on all 66 changed mixed-probe cases (58 ST-O3 runs; the 8 `work-limit` cases have no mesh).
- **Disk levels.** The ST-U3d fixtures exercise disk level 3 only (`n = 32`, as in the probe-2 snaps). The other levels are covered by the arithmetic test `st_u3d_share_factor_stays_inside_the_arc_margin` and by step 5's spacing bounds, not by a path fixture.
- **`ST-U3f/chev-1.40625-e-3`, `-e0`** (`st_u3f_chev_1_40625_is_a_known_topology_ambiguous_defect`): the known defect below, asserting `unsupported-geometry` with `Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`.

**ST-U3f decision, 2026-10-22 (dated pointer):** the user decided in chat: record only; A1 and S2 go to the rendering track ([MVP 1 closure record](#mvp-1-closure-record-2026-10-22), section 4).

**ST-U3f, near-coincidence configurations (no rule)** (formerly labelled ST-OB7), 2026-10-22: **known defect, recorded with fixtures (MVP 1 rule).** The investigation was done in a separate scratch session; the numbers below were checked against its dumps (`scratchpad\ob7\`).

- **Cases.** `chev-1.40625` (`M(−4.401143883191439, 1051.9843385722027) L(−3.125, 1000) L(−1.848856116808561, 1051.9843385722027)`, `w = 12`, round caps and join, miter limit 4, `e = −3` and `0`) and the five `λ = 4/3` near-reversal turns (`rev-4/3-3-1` at `e = −3` and 0; `rev-4/3-1-7` at `e = −3`, −5 and 0). All seven are `unsupported-geometry` (`Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`) on `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r4'`. Their pieces pass ST-O1 and ST-O2. The `chev-1.40625` dumps are bit-identical at `+r3` and `+r4`.
- **Root cause.** The pieces produce an inexact x event strictly between two adjacent binary64 values that are both pinned exact events. The rounded-fill embedding then has no free rank for it. This is the `Ordering::Equal` branch of `embed_scalar` in `rounded_line_fill.rs`, which returns `TopologyAmbiguous`, as the [rounded-fill mesh contract](p3-rounded-fill-mesh-contract.md) requires ("a pin behind/equal to the preceding rank also fails").
  - In `chev-1.40625` the pins are a sweep–sweep crossing at exactly `−3.125 − 1 ulp` and the join-disk and sweep vertices at `x = −3.125`. Two cap–cap crossings fall strictly between them, at `−0.547` ulp (`e = −3`) and `−0.778` ulp (`e = 0`) from `−3.125`.
  - In the `λ = 4/3` cases the pins are the incoming `E+` and the outgoing `E−`: adjacent floats of two distinct ideal points.
- **Not the cause.** Off-chord triple centers contribute but are not the cause. A prototype that drops the centers from the sweeps fixes `chev-1.40625`, but turns four `chev-22.5` probe cases from `ok` into `unsupported-geometry` and changes 230 `ok` meshes of the probe corpus alone.
- **Fix candidates, both deferred** because each changes a frozen contract and needs a user or contract decision:
  - **(A1)** In `embed_scalar`, let a pin that collides with the preceding rank fall through to its half-budget window instead of failing. In the prototype this fixes all seven cases, with no `ok` stroke mesh change on the B2, probe, review cusp and reproduction corpora. The investigator reports that it changes six frozen rounded-fill and fill fixtures that require `TopologyAmbiguous` (not re-run here). It gives up pin preservation. It would be full tier: it changes the V2 rounded-fill contract and needs the fill identity tool.
  - **(S2)** At a turn whose two triples agree with the swapped incoming end triple within `8u·(‖B‖∞ + r)`, share the swapped triple. In the prototype this fixes the five turns only, with `ok` meshes unchanged on the B2, probe, review cusp and reproduction corpora (not run on the PR #190 probes or the exact-reversal probes). It needs a §4.2 near-coincidence rule and an ST-G1 error argument.
- **Fixtures.** `st_u3e_review_near_reversals_are_turns` (the turns' join class and status) and `st_u3f_chev_1_40625_is_a_known_topology_ambiguous_defect` (both tolerances, with the failure cause).
- **Labels.** ST-OB7 (the contract's deferred tighter regular-stroke bound) is unrelated and keeps its meaning. Note 2026-10-22: the ST-OB7 label that the [ST-U3c entry](#b2-st-u3c-round-join-shared-vertex-review-follow-up) and the B2 status row (PR #194) gave `chev-1.40625` refers to ST-U3f from 2026-10-22; that text is kept as written.

**Reproduction tools** (review round 1, M4; manual, not run in CI). `tooling/p3-stroke-reversal-corpus.mjs` writes the exact-reversal probes: `node tooling/p3-stroke-reversal-corpus.mjs lines <out>` (4,800 cases, byte-identical to the scratch `gen-rev.mjs` output) and `… mixed <out>` (720 cases). `tooling/p3-stroke-dump-diff.mjs <before.dump> <after.dump>` is the changed-case checker; it exits 1 on any change that is not a shared-point change. The dumps come from `P3_STROKE_INPUT=<in> P3_STROKE_DUMP=<out> cargo test --release --lib stroke_dump_tests::emit_stroke_dump -- --ignored --exact`, before on `4e753fd` and after on this branch. Run on every corpus above, the checker reports 0 violations, with 611 reversal triples (3 + 608) and 837 snapped vertices for the 783 changes, and 66 reversal triples for the mixed probe. ### MVP 1 closure record (2026-10-22)

Light tier (a closure record and a manual checker fix; no contract, threshold or requirement changes). MVP 1 is the user's P3 delivery cut of 2026-10-22 (see the MVP 1 paragraph above). It is not the requirements MVP of `docs/requirements.md`, and nothing here satisfies a P3 acceptance criterion: A01-A08 stay TODO. This record fixes what MVP 1 contains, what it does not claim, and the baseline that later work compares against. All PR numbers below were checked against `git log` of `origin/main` `5c691ad` (#195).

**1. What MVP 1 delivers.**

- **Fill path end to end.** V1 path scene and port proposal #126; V2 contract #129 and fill-mesh export with the `FillMeshPort` adapter #131; V3 contract #130, V3a #132, V3b #133, V3c #134 (renderer mesh pipeline, exact fringe builder, coverage shader); V4 playground path page #166 with post-merge follow-ups #177. Kernel performance: B0R U0-U8 #137-#146 (the B0 remeasure record is in the baseline below); the Alt-A1 fallback ladder (contract #153, AL-U1 #154, AL-U2 #155, AL-U3 #156 switching the export to `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'`, AL-U4a #157, AL-U4b1 #158, AL-U4b2 #160, AL-U4b3 #175, AL-U5 #161, AL-U6 #162, AL-U7 records #163, AL-A15 review record #170); B1 kernel caps #168 (one path admits 32 cubics at λ = 8) with follow-ups #178; AL-OB5 scenario #182, OB5-U1 #184, OB5-U2 #185, OB5-U3 records #187.
- **Stroke, B2.** Proposal #164; ST-U1 contract #165; ST-U2/U3 #169; ST-U3b #171; ST-U4 #173 (cusp follow-up #179); ST-U5 `stroke_v0` export and `StrokeMeshPort` #174; ST-A0 amendments #176; ST-U2 oracle hardening #180; ST-U6 renderer stroke #183 (churn follow-up #186); ST-U8 stroke playground #188; ST-U3c round-join shared vertex #190; ST-U3d and ST-U3e disk-vertex snap radius and reversal shared triple #195, giving `'geometry-wasm/stroke-v0+r4'`.
- **Joint fill and stroke composition, ST-U7.** Design addendum #189; ST-U7a architecture note #191; ST-U7b joint overlay builder and composite model #192; ST-U7c+d joint fill draw at node opacity #193.
- **Playground pages.** The V4 paths page (#166, `apps/playground`, real `createFillMeshPort`) and the ST-U8 strokes page (#188). Status refresh and follow-up audit: #194.

**2. Known limits, stated plainly.**

- **Precision is UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 px A04 target. B4 (the A04 corpus and measurement) is not started. A01-A08 are all TODO.
- **Fringe work cap.** The fringe builder is region-only above 128 boundary vertices (`fringe-work-cap`, V3-Q2 (c)); it is a D8-interim TypeScript placement.
- **Stroke work-limit cases are not drawn.** Strokes that hit the kernel work limit give a notice and are not drawn (ST-U9 and the early abort are deferred, see section 4).
- **ST-U3f is a known defect.** `chev-1.40625` and five `λ = 4/3` near-reversal turns fail with `unsupported-geometry` (`Rounded(TopologyAmbiguous)`); there is no rule yet ([ST-U3f](#b2-st-u3dst-u3e-stroke-sharing-review-follow-up)).
- **D8-interim builders.** The overlay and fringe builders are TypeScript in the renderer; their move behind the kernel is not decided.
- **Measured scope of the fill ladder.** The AL-OB5 FEASIBLE verdict is for the workload of the [AL-OB5 proposal](p3-al-ob5-workload-scenario-proposal.md) (scope sentence at :132): 1,000 paths of 32 cubics at λ = 2, 4 and 8 leaves per cubic, `e = −3`, nonzero fill, simple snapped single-contour rings at zoom 1, in headed Chrome and Edge on one machine (DESKTOP-DKVUSAV). It does not cover the zoom range, generic coordinates, multiple contours, crossings, stroke, the renderer and draw stages, or lower-tier hardware.
- **Unsnapped fill input.** In the AL-OB5 observation set OB5-W8U (the same ring unsnapped, native and untimed), 89 of 1,000 paths are `work-limit` (AL-OB5 record :197).
- **No stroke performance record.** No committed result record measures stroke performance.
- **Cubic strokes mostly hit `work-limit`.** The stroke contract's S7 findings (:36 of `p3-b2-stroke-contract.md`): the canonical cusp at `e = −8` fails, R5 fails at zoom 1, and 64 square-capped line subpaths exhaust `max_work`.
- **No committed P1 result records** exist in `docs/benchmarks/results/`.

**3. Baseline for later comparison.**

**Baseline marking (2026-10-22).** Under [`docs/benchmarks/README.md`](../benchmarks/README.md) a result is a regression baseline only once the active plan marks it accepted, and comparisons go against the most recent compatible accepted baseline. This plan marks one record accepted as the MVP 1 fill-performance baseline: AL-OB5. The B0 2026-10-12, B0R U8, spike, AL-S1 and AL-S2 records are accepted evidence (their reviews are cited) and are kept as history. They are not regression baselines: their verdicts are INFEASIBLE or INCONCLUSIVE, and their estimates are extrapolations at provisional or measured k.

Compatibility is judged on scenario id, scenario version, configuration hash and source commit together, not on the operation id alone. The operation id `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2` does not pin the kernel: AL-S1 and AL-S2 (at `e2cf5b7`) predate B1 (#168) and AL-U4b3 (#175), which keep the same `fill-v0+r2`.

- **Accepted as the MVP 1 fill-performance baseline: AL-OB5 2026-10-21.** Evidence review ACCEPTED WITH NOTES. Scenario `p3-al-ob5-workload` v1, configuration hash `cb23d4ca…a651`, source commit `4713307` (`main`, PR #186; clean tree), operation `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2` (revision 2, port version 0), release WASM SHA-256 `480cb6336040254fed9bf3be2d620e87fcb723ea981c5b15c1bbb2275f0b2d50` (424,588 bytes; record :39), through `createFillMeshPort` in headed Chrome 155.0.8059.85 and Edge 155.0.4283.62 (record :28). Baseline for its measured scope only (section 2); the unmeasured scope of its verdict (proposal :132) is not covered by it.
- B0 2026-10-12: evidence accepted (independent evidence review ACCEPTED); history, not a baseline. Scenario `p3-b0-feasibility` v1, commit `34ae06d`, the pre-B0R kernel.
- B0R U8 2026-10-17: evidence accepted (evidence review ACCEPTED, PR #146); history, not a baseline. Scenario `p3-b0-feasibility` v1, configuration hash `a10c98e9…9271`, commit `0b48163`, the B0R kernel before Alt-A1.
- Spike ladder 2026-10-18: evidence accepted (SL-A11 ACCEPTED); history, not a baseline. Scenario `p3-b0-spike-ladder` v1, commit `9505ddd`; a test-only spike, not a production operation.
- AL-S1 2026-10-20: evidence accepted (AL-A15 ACCEPTED); history, not a baseline. Scenario `p3-fill-ladder` v1, configuration hash `8f07f146…672e`, commit `e2cf5b7`, operation `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2`, native.
- AL-S2 2026-10-20: evidence accepted (AL-A15 ACCEPTED); history, not a baseline. Scenario `p3-b0-wasm` v1, configuration hash `f337d0ef…2bfd`, commit `6590083` (`main` `e2cf5b7` plus a records-only commit), operation `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2`, release WASM in Chrome and Edge.

E14 makes no speed claim (E14-G4); a later `r3` comparison needs a new record ([E14 contract](d9-e14-fill-rung0-edge-cap-contract.md), [E14-U1 evidence](../evidence/e14-u1-2026-10-25.md)).

**6. Checker fix (`tooling/p3-stroke-dump-diff.mjs`, PR #195 round-2 review minor items, and PR #197 review round 1).** Gaps closed: (a) a removed disk vertex with no segment centre at distance `r` is a violation (before, `reach` stayed infinite and the ratio was 0); (b) a triple centre may enter or leave a piece's point set only as a hull-membership flip, defined precisely as: it is a centre in both dumps; it lies inside or on the exact (rational-arithmetic) convex hull of the point set that lacks it; it is on or outside the hull of the other points of the set that contains it (so a centre strictly inside that hull is no flip); and another, non-centre point of the same piece changed too (before, any centre change was accepted); (c) a case present only in the after dump is a violation; (d) a duplicate case name, or a header count that differs from the number of case lines, is a violation. New SHA-256 `9c1d648942cef193ba9063b01e38f90e93f60ab2f9f1860f5b4a5b04e4253d22` (the table at the ST-U3d/ST-U3e entry lists the earlier `1a7e1fb7…58f3`, which stays as written). Re-run, 2026-10-22, on **all eight** #195 corpora, `before` against `final` dumps (the `mixed` pair from its own scratch directory): `b2` (145 cases, 0 changed), `probe` (495, 0 changed), `r190a` (107, 3 changed, 3 reversal triples, 1 centre flip), `r190b` (656, 172 changed, 837 snapped vertices, max 16.28 u·R), `repro` (28, 0 changed), `rv` (100, 0 changed), `xrev` (4,800, 608 changed, 608 reversal triples, 192 centre flips) and the mixed reversal probe (720, 66 changed, 66 reversal triples, 8 centre flips): **eight of eight re-run, 0 violations, exit 0 each**. Across `r190a`, `r190b` and `xrev` that is 3 + 172 + 608 = 783 changed cases and 611 reversal triples (the independent reviewer's tallies); with the mixed probe, 849 changed cases and 677 reversal triples. The 201 accepted centre flips (1 + 192 + 8) all satisfy the stricter on-or-outside test, so it is kept (the looser rule without that test gave the same tallies on these corpora). Negative control: the centre-drop experiment (scratch `ob7/*-s1.dump` against the `main` dumps of the same inputs) gives violations in all five of its corpora (`ob7` 4, `repro` 116, `rv` 45, `b2` 260, `probe` 1,827 violation lines), with violations in all five; centre violations ("removed a centre that is not a hull-membership flip") dominate in `ob7`, `repro`, `b2` and `probe` (19 of 45 lines in `rv`), and the rule still accepts 13 and 12 on-boundary centre removals in `rv` and `b2` that leave the region unchanged; the previous tool reported no violation at all on `ob7` and `repro` for the same pairs. Two further controls, a dump with a repeated case name and a dump whose header count is one too high, are both rejected (exit 1).

### Unit-suite timeout stabilization (light tier)

Plan entry: stop two heavy unit tests from timing out at vitest's 5 s default under a loaded parallel `vitest run`, keeping every assertion. The P3 B0 "frames the corpus deterministically" test compares its two 581 KB encodings byte for byte instead of with a deep `toEqual` (about 1.4 s down to under 50 ms). The P3.1d "certifies every frozen baseline" test keeps its exact certification of all 22,912 lines and gets a 30 s per-test timeout, because `rounded-fill/exact.ts` and `cubic-boundary/` are pinned by source manifests. Acceptance check: `pnpm check` passes on five consecutive runs; the global timeout is unchanged and no test is skipped. Evidence is recorded on the PR.

### B0 runner (light tier)

Plan entry: implement section 9 item 1 of the [B0 contract](p3-b0-feasibility-benchmark-contract.md) without changing it. Acceptance check: `pnpm benchmark:p3-b0 --profile functional` completes end to end; the TypeScript generator and an independent Rust generator match committed fixture bits; a tampered record is rejected; `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and `pnpm test:geometry` pass; the diagnostic flag builds and passes clippy in its own target directory; the WASM release artifact is byte-identical to main's. Evidence is recorded on the runner PR. A functional run is not performance evidence.

Interpretations the contract leaves open, fixed by the runner and reviewed on its PR:

- check 2 applies to the p95 edit-proxy fits as well as the median fits;
- H1 is Inconclusive when a counted instantiation has no captured unit cost;
- a reference record with missing metadata fails check 8, so its verdict is INCONCLUSIVE rather than absent;
- the curvature guard breaks a tie for the half-L point toward the smaller L, which does not occur in the frozen sweep;
- a failed or timed-out process is retained under `failures` in the record and fails check 7.

Current status: P3.1k integrated through [PR #95](https://github.com/npclown/vector-studio/pull/95). P3.1l is integrated through PRs #96-#98. P3.1m and its [U1-U4](p3-private-contract.md#user-decisions-on-mesh-position-representation-2026-10-06), U2 and R3 follow-ups are integrated through PRs #99-#105. P3.1n R0/R0a is integrated through PRs #106-#108: the C2 clearance admits 143 of 158 fixture rows. The user decided [Q4 and Q5](p3-private-contract.md#user-decisions-on-mesh-position-representation-2026-10-06) on 2026-10-07: A5 with inherited P1 thresholds. ## P3.1p R2 tiling contract

The [R2 tiling contract](p3-r2-tiling-contract.md) is FROZEN.

- **Technical review (Astra high):** NOT READY twice, then READY.
- **Implementability review (Sol medium):** NOT IMPLEMENTABLE, then IMPLEMENTABLE.
- **User decisions (2026-10-08, chat):** the user confirmed E1-E3:
  - shared vertex records with a per-vertex owner-cell carrier, for watertight seams;
  - builds only at epoch changes, with a per-level cache;
  - the Q1 coverage reference restated as the exact reference tiling.

**What it fixes.**

- The epoch-based successor certificate:
  - S1 domain;
  - S2 epoch position `E_ep`;
  - S3 merged-mesh C2 at `s_lo`;
  - S4 exact partition;
  - S5 lists.
- The per-cell exterior fringe.
- The reach constants.
- γ = 4.
- A pre-stated thin-tip sliver hazard, which is reported, not gated.
- The T02 offline exact evidence, with four pre-registered gates.

O03, the GPU evidence, follows under its own contract.

**T02 implementation** (branch `codex/p3-1p-t02-impl`):

- **Primary:** epoch, `E_ep` (S2), pruned merged C2 (S3), lists (S5), camera variants, and the report and replay.
- **Sol medium:** the merged mesh (E1 records and carriers), `fringeFaces`, S4, and the trajectory cell sets.

Astra high's stable-source review was CLEARED after one fix round. Its evidence review was EVIDENCE ACCEPTED, with 15 of 15 rows independently recomputed.

**Result: T02 FAILS.** See the [review record](../evidence/p3.1p-t02/review-2026-10-08.md); the archived report SHA is `3e89e55a…`.

- **Gates 1 (partition) and 4 (frame coverage):** pass.
- **Gate 2:** fails. The three class A rows are admitted, but 16 camera variants fail on genuine grid slivers: thin tips that span several cells, and a near-diagonal grid corner.
- **Gate 3:** fails. 100 of 429 O rows are lost.
  - The losses are δ- and scale-driven: S3 runs at `s_lo`, and Ē sits 1.4 to 2.3 times above T01's δ̄ because of the zoom band and the record extent. Fringe triangles add to them.
  - The larger window's geometry passes with T01's δ̄.

**User decision (2026-10-08, chat).** Both are the recommended options:

- for gate 3: reduce δ and improve the fringe;
- for gate 2: compare grid-sliver mitigations, then decide.

**R2 revision 4.** The [delta contract](p3-r2-tiling-rev4-contract.md) is FROZEN. Its technical review (Astra high) went NOT READY, then READY; its implementability review (Sol medium) was IMPLEMENTABLE. It adds four deltas:

- **D-A:** localized C2, with best rotation for the triangle term;
- **D-B:** four certificate sub-bands per epoch;
- **D-C:** exact Delaunay flips on the fringe, ordered by position;
- **D-D:** owner carriers centred on the geometrically owned records.

Gates are not relaxed. A pre-registered prediction set R (16 ids, from the disclosed census) defines a FAIL-AS-PREDICTED verdict, which is not acceptance.

**T03 implementation** (branch `codex/p3-1p-t03-impl`):

- **Primary:** D-A and D-B (`local.ts`), the report with its gates, prediction and verdict, and the replay.
- **Sol medium:** D-C (`delaunay.ts`) and D-D (`owner.ts`), with their locality tests.

Astra high's stable-source review was CLEARED after one fix, an unpruned oracle test. Its evidence review returned EVIDENCE ACCEPTED, with 14 of 14 rows independently recomputed.

**Result: FAIL-AS-PREDICTED, which is not acceptance.** See the [review record](../evidence/p3.1p-t03/review-2026-10-08.md); the archived report SHA is `5679e76e…`.

- **Gates 1 and 4:** pass.
- **Gates 2 and 3:** fail on exactly the pre-registered set R, with the predicted outcomes:
  - 12 class A variants on `vertex`, which are full grid-sliver clearance failures;
  - 4 `Z/depth-positive …/S5` O rows at DPR 1 on `triangle`.
- **Against T01:**
  - O losses fell from 100 to 4 and C losses from 32 to 0.
  - Six admitted rows pass only some sub-bands. They are instances of the zoom-only U4 case.

**T04 contract.** The [T04 grid-sliver mitigation comparison contract](p3-t04-sliver-mitigation-contract.md) is FROZEN.

- **Reviews.** Astra high: NOT READY four times, then READY with one verbatim fix. Sol medium: NOT IMPLEMENTABLE twice, then IMPLEMENTABLE.
- **User decisions, all 2026-10-08 in chat:**
  - evaluate needle-tip truncation; this was later withdrawn because it removes a visible line;
  - evaluate M-D, needle-as-fringe, instead;
  - evaluate a relaxed coverage meaning for features narrower than 2Ē.

T04 compares these configurations against C0, the T03 control:

| Configuration | Adds                                   |
| ------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| C1            | K = 8 sub-bands                        |
| C2            | + M-B, per-cell region retriangulation |
| C3(w)         | + M-D, for `w` in {1/32, 1/16, 1/8}    |
| C4(w)         | M-D without M-B                        |

It adopts nothing.

**Parked under [D8](p3-d8-parallel-tracks.md).** T04 implementation (`tests/geometry/extent-t04/`) would come next, then the evidence and a user decision. Adopting anything would lead to R2 revision 5; adopting the relaxed semantics would also need a requirements update. The residual set R stays an open risk.

## P3.1o O02 A5 coverage rule contract

The [O02 contract](p3-o02-a5-coverage-contract.md) is FROZEN after independent review rounds. The technical review went NOT READY ×2, then READY. The implementability review went NOT IMPLEMENTABLE ×2, then IMPLEMENTABLE.

It fixes the test-only A5 realization accepted by the user on 2026-10-07:

- screen-space segment/vertex distance at the pixel center, with multi-sector pinch vertices;
- an analytic ramp width;
- a complement-triangulation fringe with an exact partition check.

It gates G1/G2 (Q4) and G3 (Q9) on headed Chrome/Edge at DPR 1, 1.5 and 2, at 1x and 4x. DPR 3 is observed only.

The census before freeze, per DPR: 143 renderable and 6 empty crops. 96 rows have degree-4 pinch vertices, and the maximum frame NDC magnitude is 31.2.

Production adoption, R2 and C04 are not implied.

**Implementation** (branch `codex/p3-1o-o02-impl`):

- Primary wrote the page, the shaders (main, count, readout), the builder, the spec, the evidence helpers and the configs.
- An independent Sol medium worker wrote the variants, the exact complement triangulator with sectors and the partition check, the exact rule oracle, the capture schema, the metrics and the replay, together with host tests.
- Sol made three triangulator choices that the contract does not spell out. Astra high ruled all three within the contract, because they only tighten checks that end in the exact partition check:
  - a hole is bridged by the minimum-distance visible vertex pair, with distinct coordinates, plus an exact wedge check;
  - the half-edge walk is re-traced after each clip, and segments covered from both sides cancel;
  - an extra ear is rejected at pinch corners.
- Verdict precedence, fixed before any evidence exists: INCONCLUSIVE when any INCONCLUSIVE condition holds; otherwise FAIL on any G1-G3 violation; otherwise PASS. Both reason lists are always emitted.

**Stable-source review** (Astra high): CLEARED. Its own float32 shader emulation over all 572 renderable variants (84.8M centers) found a maximum |emulation − rule| of 8.5e-5, and no center covered twice.

**SHOULD-FIX applied:**

- non-rendered rows carry the crop origin;
- the verdict precedence above;
- fragment feature-evaluation proxy, `clipOutside` and feature statistics;
- a committed shader-emulation host test.

**Local checks:** a pre-review WGSL compile check and an `init()` check in the in-app browser, with no draws, both passed.

**Evidence:** [review record](../evidence/p3.1o-o02/review-2026-10-07.md); verdict **PASS**, EVIDENCE ACCEPTED.

- Headed Chrome and Edge (NVIDIA Turing) at DPR 1, 1.5 and 2, 1x and 4x: 143 rendered variants per DPR, with zero G1, G2 or G3 violations.
- The two browsers and the two runs are byte-identical (596 of 596 crops), and 1x equals 4x.
- `|obs − rule|` is at most 1 LSB.
- The O01 line-not-segment deficit is gone.
- Observations, not gates: an acute-tip boundary-shift outlier of +0.28 px over 0.93 px of boundary, and THIN over-coverage at DPR 1.5.

Next, all needing user direction: production adoption (C04 layout, Q11, R2 clipping), the partial-coverage tolerance, and R1/R4.

Shift covers 83 rows; the remaining 60 rows are `N/A:L=0`.

That contract needs independent review before any implementation.

## P3.1o O01 observational coverage contract

Base: `1a6aa2a`, the PR #109 squash merge. Branch: `codex/p3-1o-o01-coverage-contract`.

The [O01 contract](p3-o01-coverage-experiment-contract.md) freezes a test-only headed experiment. It compares A1 (discontinuous outward fringe), A2 (inside-only ramp) and A5 (symmetric straddling ramp) at 1x and 4x against an exact dyadic pixel-area oracle.

**Inputs.** The 143 C2-admitted fixture rows plus literal F07, F11, SQ and THIN rows.

**Precision path.** NDC is rounded once on the CPU. The reference region is the exact preimage. Distance is computed from the fragment position and per-edge coefficients relative to the crop origin.

**Metrics.** These are reported with no thresholds:

- interior and exterior error;
- band deviation, with and without corner pixels;
- boundary shift over the inset viewport;
- seams;
- the 1x/4x difference;
- overlap mass, from float `max` and `add` diagnostic passes.

**Runner and evidence.** These follow P3.1l:

- page API;
- 64-byte vertex record;
- timeouts and statuses;
- exclusive records;
- contract identity;
- an offline replayable metrics file;
- a 25 MB archive cap;
- a confirmation-run rule.

**Review.** Independent Astra high technical review and Sol medium implementability review each ran two rounds, plus confirmation fixes.

- **Round 1** found five problems: pixel-aligned literal rows; precision noise in the shader NDC and distance path; an unstated Q3 rule; an ambiguous A1; and the wrong corpus source. It also found infeasible oracle and archive sizes, and missing page, layout and identity details.
- **Round 2** found that the A5 fringe conditional broke continuity, that fringe vertex construction was unspecified, that overlap mass mixed quantizations, that classification cost was unbounded, and that pass identity was ambiguous.

Nothing is adopted. Q4 and Q5 remain user decisions after the evidence.

## P3.1m M01-M06 position certificate contract

Base: `0491340a6859cb9efa86d23be612635780a328ca`, the PR #99 squash merge. Its required check passed in run 37459250400, job 112254419942. Branch: `codex/p3-1m-position-certificate-contract`.

The [contract](p3-position-certificate-contract.md) freezes a test-only sufficient position certificate for K's carrier under the pinned WGSL §15.7 rules:

- a syntactic evaluation family F, with residual risk R1 stated;
- `E = Pack + Γ8·(absolute monomial sum) + 2^-40`, with proof obligations P1-P5;
- an exact window-uniform bound comparing P1's origin rule with a physical-window origin;
- rational clearance transport of K's four eligibility terms;
- a pinned K/L data corpus plus prospective and term-targeting rows;
- a deterministic N02 adversarial evaluator (5400 evaluations per vertex-axis);
- a byte-identical `report.json`;
- open user decisions U1-U4.

Nothing is adopted.

**Review.** Two independent read-only reviewers reviewed it: Astra high on the proofs and Sol medium on implementability and governance.

- **Round 1.** Both found it not freeze-ready.
  - Value-defined F did not imply the five-monomial form.
  - A form with 9 operations would have flipped EXT-32768. This was fixed with the exactness argument for doubling, and Γ9 sensitivity is recorded.
  - Three window samples could not bound the whole window.
  - Exports, imports, evidence and the N02 budget were missing.
- **Round 2.** All round-1 findings were resolved. New findings:
  - window values omitted the F-lane rounding term;
  - the Pack64 underflow term was too small;
  - P4 omitted the subnormal case;
  - some rows were not literal;
  - the tables rendered broken;
  - N02 asserted wall time.
- **Round 3.** All round-2 findings were fixed. Confirmation added the 2^60 original-input guard and its boundary control. Both reviewers found the contract FREEZE-READY.

**Checks.** Changed-Markdown Prettier, link, whitespace and scope checks PASS. Product, unit, build, GPU and benchmark commands were NOT RUN for this documentation-only checkpoint. Required protected CI still applies.

**Native runs on an NVIDIA Turing hardware adapter, headed Chrome 154.0.8037.98 and Edge 154.0.4258.53:**

The L00 paragraph below records its contemporaneous pre-merge state; its "pending" CI/integration wording is superseded by this record, not edited.

The [readiness note](p3-native-projection-readiness.md#l01-l06-written-contract) now gives concrete answers for every freeze-checklist row:

- the 158-row identity and the packed binary32 byte layout, with independent provenance checks;

All native rows remain OBSERVE, and the 1/16 physical-pixel bound and topology rule are unchanged. This checkpoint is documentation-only: no runner, page, configuration, script or GPU execution is added. L01-L06 FROZEN, local PASS, 2026-10-06. Primary wrote the contract after inspecting the K model/audit/fixtures, the archived K observations, the P1 probe, the GPU/Vite/vitest configurations, the evidence helper and the boundary tooling. There was no recursive delegation. Two independent read-only reviewers then reviewed it:

- **Arithmetic/provenance and WebGPU correctness.** Round 1 found one blocker: the short-draw control could never decode as `missing`, because the decoder used `drawCount`. It also found eight should-fix items:
  - division in the point position could be inexact, so the shader uses `* 0.125`;
  - clip texels carried no identity;
  - texel precedence was not literal;
  - fallback detection was missing;
  - vertex-stage storage limits and an explicit layout were missing;
  - the rounder import conflicted with independence;
  - the sign of zero lanes was not pinned;
  - the contract identity was undefined.
- **Implementability, ownership and governance.** Round 1 found four blockers:
  - `pnpm check` was wrongly said to run the host controls;
  - the interfaces between work units were not fixed;
  - the import rules contradicted each other;
  - the record could be lost on a hang.

  It also found seven should-fix items: control COMPLETE semantics, root/worker restart, backend and archive metadata, software adapters, ambiguous vertex order, the validation.md row and a port collision (4176 changed to 4177).

Round 2 confirmed that every round-1 finding was resolved. It found three new blockers:

- control COMPLETE contradicted the `capture-invalid` PARTIAL rule;
- the orientation reason did not reproduce K's `topology:orientation:0`;
- a type-only import was missing from the allowlist.

It also found should-fix items: a squash-stable contract identity and packer/auditor author independence (the packer moved to Primary L07). Primary applied all of them. Both reviewers stated that the contract is freeze-ready after these one-line fixes.

Validation:

- Changed-Markdown Prettier, local link/anchor, whitespace and two-file scope checks PASS, as recorded in the PR.
- Product/unit/build/native/browser/GPU/benchmark commands NOT RUN for this documentation-only checkpoint.

Next: L07-L09 implementation from integrated `main`, then stable-source review before any GPU dispatch.

## Scope and current seams

Implement only the [P3 roadmap scope](../prototype-plan.md#p3-fill-and-stroke-meshes): multiple subpaths/holes, nonzero/evenodd fill, open/closed paths, required caps/joins, thin/extreme strokes and coverage-fringe/MSAA comparison. [Graphics architecture](../graphics-engine-architecture.md) owns algorithms and numeric policy; [system architecture](../../ARCHITECTURE.md) owns inward dependency direction. Dash editing remains in the roadmap's later editor coverage; P3.0b must explicitly resolve the dash design gate without silently adding runtime dash scope.

P2 already supplies local Float64 bounds and flattened paths, source provenance, a private batch/reserve ABI and owned adapter/cache results. Its fillRule/strokeStyleHash fields currently affect cache identity only. They are not implemented fill/stroke behavior. Preserve [P2 v1](p2-private-contract.md) rather than reinterpreting its output as triangles.

`packages/contracts/src/scene.ts` accepts primitives and containers, with a color/width stroke; it has no path node or cap/join contract. `packages/renderer-core/src/primitive-packet.ts` and the existing WebGPU path consume primitive packets. Thus private numeric mesh work can be planned independently, but end-to-end path integration requires a concrete scene/API proposal before changing those exported meanings. Do not hide a path API in primitive data or import geometry-wasm directly from renderer-core.

The composition root must inject geometry through plain contracts. Rust/geometry-wasm owns reconstructible geometry; renderer-core owns retained scene/order/cache coordination; renderer-webgpu alone owns GPU resources and its scheduler/device generation. No worker independently redesigns these boundaries.
